Each M must specify the source and target scales, whether the object's K is retained, how relations or units are converted, what information is lost, and when the error exceeds the limits of applicability. Without bridging evidence, only parallel descriptions of the two scales may be given; successful transfer must not be claimed.

At least three questions must be distinguished for the same mapping: whether it suffices to answer the current static query, whether it can advance to the next state on its own, and whether it still suffices to evaluate admissible interventions. There is no automatic progression among the three. A total can answer a question about the total exactly while omitting local overload. Adding the maximum can recover a particular static answer and yet may still omit the positional identity needed for targeted operations. The success of a mapping must therefore always be specified with its target, horizon, environment, and admissible actions, not merely with a note that “information is retained.”

### 3.4.1 Representational Recasting and Discursive Recasting

Representational recasting rewrites a sourced state, information, or model expression, through a named converter, bridging party, or rule, into a new representation directed toward a target scale, circle[^p03-tn-circle], task, or vocabulary system. When the payload is primarily language, institutional texts, or explanation, it is called discursive recasting. The new representation must retain its parent record and source lineage.

Recasting differs from scale transformation, transformation of circle relations, network propagation, compression, and lateral analogical transfer. These operations may participate in a recasting, but must not stand in for the recasting contract. Whether recasting actually changes understanding or action on the target side still requires materials concerning the specified channel. Whether the collapsed differences still provide conditional increments, and whether different orderings fail to commute, are still tested under the existing contracts for scale closure.

A recasting must register at least: the source formulation and context; the target circle and task; the converter or rule and its version; retained elements; permitted changes; collapsed differences; omissions; added explanations; ambiguities; error; the parent record; the path back to the source record; review on the target side; the validity period; and conditions for failure and withdrawal. Without independent review on the target side, the recasting may only be registered as awaiting testing.

## 3.5 The 11 Universal Structural Primitives

### 3.5.1 U01 Candidate Object Interface

U01 registers only the D0 candidate object S*=<B,X,R,I,T,SP,K> and its traceability across scales. N0 is frozen in a specific G-instance; completing U01 does not guarantee in advance that G1 holds.

The function of U01 is to turn an object of intended study into a traceable input: the object-instance ID and the seven D0 fields must be complete, and a contract for a root instance is then established when the analysis proceeds to empirical judgment. It permits soft boundaries, short-duration objects, forms with multiple centers, and distributed forms, but does not judge these forms valid in advance. U01 is not applicable to purely metaphysical objects for which the declaration of empirical fields is refused. Completing U01 permits only the registration of candidate objects and the generation of questions awaiting testing; it does not generate value, responsibility, choice, or authorization to act.

### 3.5.2 U02 Boundaries and Interfaces

Boundaries distinguish the inside of an object from its outside; interfaces record exchanges and constraints across the boundary. Boundaries can be permeable, movable, nested, or overlapping; interfaces can filter, delay, convert, or block processes across the boundary. Spatial proximity does not automatically constitute a shared boundary. The existence of a boundary does not imply that the object is closed; still less does the existence of an interface imply that any one party holds control rights. Across scales, the distinction between inside and outside and the traceability of channels should be retained, while allowing boundaries and interfaces to be reorganized. This primitive is not applicable to objects for which no distinction between inside and outside can be declared.

### 3.5.3 U03 States and Observables

States are described through declared variables and observables, with measurement positions and errors retained. A state is the object's registered configuration at a given T and resolution; an observable is a record formed from a state through a measurement channel. Measurable quantities are not the same as the entire state; not having been observed is not the same as not existing; and a record may also include contributions from the measurement process itself. Changes of scale can alter resolution and observables, but variable definitions, measurement positions, and errors must be retained through the transfer.

### 3.5.4 U04 Components, Carriers, and Substrates

Structural realization must distinguish components, carriers that carry causal transitions, and substrates that provide the conditions for realization. A component is a constituent unit of the current object; a carrier is a material, informational, or relational position through which a specified state transition actually occurs; a substrate provides the background conditions that make this realization possible. A given component is not necessarily the key carrier, and the object may retain K after the component is replaced. Identifying a carrier specifies only a position of realization; it must not be used to attribute intent, responsibility, or an obligation to continue carrying to the carrier. This primitive is not applicable to purely definitional objects that make no claim to empirical realization.

### 3.5.5 U05 Relations and Constraints

A relation is registered as a structural constraint only when it changes what states or transitions are feasible. Relations describe repeatable dependencies between endpoints; constraints narrow the set of feasible states or transitions. Correlation, adjacency, and co-occurrence must not by themselves be upgraded to causal constraints; a causal upgrade also requires temporal order and a specified channel, together with intervention or a counterfactual. The existence of a constraint does not establish that it is justified, either. Scale transformation may change effective relations, so the endpoints, direction, and applicable window of a relation must remain traceable.

### 3.5.6 U06 Flow, Channels, and Transmission

For systems in which transitions across components or boundaries are claimed, flow specifies the object, direction, rate, and distribution of resources, signals, or effects; channels specify the carrying paths; and transmission specifies how inputs change downstream states. Capacity, delay, reliability, controllability, and loss are candidate attributes for measurement channel by channel and dimension by dimension; unmeasured dimensions remain unknown. U06 provides descriptive positions, not proof that any particular channel actually exists; channel effects still require a G2-instance. The source, target, and channel must remain traceable through scale transformation, and a channel must not automatically be deemed a responsible party.

### 3.5.7 U07 Feedback and State Updating

A prior state or output constitutes effective feedback only when it returns through a channel and changes subsequent states, probabilities, or constraints. Feedback is a return relation; state updating is the subsequent change caused by a return or other input. The two need to be registered separately. Feedback can be reinforcing, inhibiting, delayed, distorted, or blocked. Effective feedback is not the same as learning, nor is it the same as repair: for the carrier-learning branch of C3, current updates, subsequent invocation, and task results are checked under §4.5; the historical-increment branch `C3-learning/CM-LEARNING-history` additionally requires updates that can be retained, repeated rounds, and G3's historical conditional increments (see §6.3); repair additionally requires K*/F* and N/O review. U07 is not applicable to a one-way process with no return channel.

### 3.5.8 U08 Conditions and Environment

The environment and conditions enter explanation only when they change an object's states or transitions through detectable interfaces. A condition is a local prerequisite for a mechanism to hold in the current instance; the environment is the set of variables left outside the current boundary that may affect the object through interfaces. The environment is not a personified overall cause, nor does it determine every outcome. Changes of scale redraw the object–environment boundary, so the relevant environmental variables and interfaces through which effects occur must be declared again. For a model that is explicitly closed and has no external channel, this primitive may be marked not applicable.

### 3.5.9 U09 Load, Capacity, and Recovery Margin

In nonequilibrium systems with sustained flux, maintenance requirements, or recovery requirements, load must be described together with finite capacity and recovery margin. Load is demand of the same type at a given position and within a given window; capacity is the range of what can be taken up in the same type or through an explicit conversion mapping; recovery margin is the room still available after a disturbance for replenishment, reorganization, or a return to target K/F. Units, distributions, windows, and bottleneck positions must be declared for all three. An instantaneous shortfall does not automatically amount to cumulative damage, nor does overload create an obligation to continue carrying. An object with neither sustained nonequilibrium nor maintenance requirements may be marked not applicable.

### 3.5.10 U10 Trace, Memory, and Paths

A trace is a durable record that a prior change leaves in a state, relation, boundary, interface, transition constraint, or environment. A trace counts as structural memory only when it enters subsequent transitions. Historical-increment path differences are registered under G3 only when a historical term changes future paths, probabilities, or conversion costs after controlling for the currently registered state. General historical effects transmitted by measured current carriers are tested separately under §4.5. Retaining a trace is not the same as G3 holding, nor is a path difference the same as fate or irreparability. Trace carriers and durability thresholds can change with scale; U10 is not applicable to static classifications with no temporal extension.

### 3.5.11 U11 Phase, Transition, and Dissolution

A phase is a conditional range of operation under the same K, distinguished by pre-registered variables and criteria; a transition is a detectable change between ranges beyond noise and sensitivity to binning; dissolution means only that the original K no longer holds. The basic phase pattern does not require path dependence; a causal claim about a specified trigger separately requires G2. Sustained effects or delayed recovery transmitted through current carriers require separate evidence under §4.5; G3 is additionally required only when a historical conditional increment beyond the current representation is claimed. A phase is not a maturity ranking of S0–S6, nor a stage that every object must pass through. If K has already ceased to hold before a transition, the classification should be changed to object conversion or dissolution.

## 3.6 Combining Primitives and Purpose-Relative Closure

U01–U03 answer “what is currently treated as a candidate object, and how is it seen”; U04–U06 answer “what realizes the structure, and through what is change transmitted”; U07–U10 answer “how a return updates states, how conditions act on an object, how load is distributed, and how history leaves effects”; U11 answers “when it is still the same range of operation, and when there is transition, conversion, or dissolution.”

These primitives serve as interfaces for one another, not as proofs of one another. Every missing field must be marked unknown, not applicable, unobservable, or withheld for protection, and how that state affects the ceiling on conclusions must be stated. The familiarity of natural language must not be used to fill in missing contract fields, nor may the existence of a primitive be directly upgraded to a stage, value, responsibility, choice, or authorization.

[^p03-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

[^p03-tn-fields]: Translator's note: The Chinese edition writes this as `M(SP0→SP1)=<保留变量, 改变变量, 丢失变量, 转换算子, 误差, 适用区间>`.

[^p03-tn-circle]: Translator's note: *Circle* renders 圈层. Circles may be parallel, nested, overlapping, bridged, or competing, and may also form only temporarily around particular events; the term does not imply a hierarchy.



<a id="part_4"></a>

# Part 4 Empirical Questions, Outcome Contracts, and Conditional Formal Propositions

G1–G4 are four kinds of empirical question: whether a candidate grouping brings a conditional gain, whether a specified channel has an identifiable effect on a target transition, whether historical terms provide conditional increments beyond the current representation, and under which tasks, dynamics, and interventions a specified representation has closure. The question families themselves are not laws governing all objects. A specific G-instance registers both a factual assertion awaiting testing and rules for evidence; the two must not be conflated in writing. Whether an effect exists is determined by the world and the conditions at issue; whether the available evidence suffices to support that assertion is determined by the research design and results.

This Part separates empirical testing, the use of results, and formal proof. G instances answer factual questions; C contracts[^p04-tn-contract] specify which limited outputs can be assembled from an existing basis; F propositions state necessary relations under explicit mathematical conditions. Registering the same result again does not add independent evidence, nor does the number of passes under root families or contracts measure the usefulness of the entire framework.

## 4.1 Contracts for Root Instances

This section provides a standard recording procedure for confirmatory testing. Under this procedure, each instance has two sections, pre-registration and results: pre-registration is completed before results are read, and the results section is appended after execution while preserving the original registration. What has not yet been run is explicitly recorded as not evaluated; times, observed values, or materials must not be fabricated. The two sections together determine what outcomes count as support, what outcomes count as refutation, and where conclusions may be extrapolated.

The single primary criterion used here is a default design to prevent choosing favorable results after they appear, not the only reasonable form for every study. Multiple outcomes, multiple models, joint criteria, confirmatory work following exploratory work, and sequential studies can each have valid designs, but the primary target or joint rule, selection procedure, stopping rule, and applicable handling of multiple comparisons must be stated in advance. The G and H subtypes with a single primary criterion below belong to this standard procedure. Materials outside the procedure are still evaluated according to their actual research designs; they must not be judged to have no knowledge value merely because their record fields have a different form. Nor are the four question families claimed to exhaust all scientific questions or to be logically independent axioms.

| Contract Section | Required Fields | Function |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity and freezing | `instance_id`, `root_id`, `contract_version`, `preregistration_timestamp` | Distinguish instances from root templates; use an immutable version and an auditable timestamp earlier than access to results to establish that the contract precedes the evidence |
| Candidate object | `candidate_object_id`, `object_contract_id` | Point to the D0 candidate object and its contract, preventing a name from being treated as support for an object |
| Scale and target | `scale_profile`, `time_window`, `identity_criterion`, `target_variables` | Freeze SP, T, K, and the variables to be explained |
| Proposition instantiation | `selected_subtype`, `selected_success_criterion`, `candidate_specification`, `zero_model`, `controls`, `model_classes` | Select only one subtype before evidence appears, then only one success criterion from that subtype's admissible list; declare the candidate proposition, N0, controls, and model families |
| Sampling and extrapolation | `sampling_unit`, `generalization_unit` | Distinguish the source of the data from the scope to which the conclusion may be generalized |
| Decision rules | `evaluation_metric`, `decision_threshold`, `decision_rule`, `null_decision_rule` | Separate the positive-support check from the null-conclusion check; freeze the primary target or aggregation, model selection or ensembling, multiple comparisons, equivalence or sufficiency, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance |
| Mode and failure | `evidence_mode`, `falsifier`, `pause_condition` | Declare an exploratory, confirmatory, replication, or falsification mode, together with its ceiling on conclusions, falsification conditions, and suspension conditions |
| Deviations and status | `deviation_record`, `preregistration_status` | Record deviations, times, reasons, and impacts item by item; register `none` even when there is no deviation, and distinguish draft, frozen before evidence, completed under the frozen contract, and deviation after evidence |
| Results and time | `result_timestamp`, `observed_primary_result`, `decision_rule_outcome`, `null_decision_rule_outcome`, `result_state` | The result timestamp is later than the freezing timestamp; record only the observed results for the pre-frozen primary target and rules, record separately whether the positive-support check and the three null-conclusion checks pass, and close the instance using the closed set of four states |
| Evidence and analysis artifacts | `evidence_refs`, `analysis_artifact_refs` | Point to resolvable external materials, data freezes, code, models, or logs; internal concept identifiers, string prefixes, and self-reported conclusions cannot replace instances |

When the single-primary-criterion procedure is used, the selection rule is denoted `one_subtype_and_success_criterion_before_evidence`: one `selected_subtype` and one `selected_success_criterion` are determined before results appear. A joint or multi-outcome design must register its overall criterion separately; several individual items that did not pass must not be reassembled after the fact into a success. Evaluation metrics, positive thresholds, the null model, `decision_rule`, `null_decision_rule`, and falsification conditions are all frozen around that criterion. If the results are unfavorable, multiple subtypes or success criteria must not be combined after the fact, nor may the target, model, metric, or threshold be changed to rescue the empirical question family.

`decision_rule` also closes off researcher degrees of freedom for multiple targets and models: if there is more than one target variable, a single primary target or aggregation rule must be frozen before results; if there is more than one model kind, a model-selection or ensemble rule must be frozen; if there are multiple comparisons, the correction rule must be frozen. Without these rules, an instance may have only one primary target and one primary model. `null_decision_rule` separately declares which equivalence or sufficiency test, statistical-power or sensitivity check, and tolerance can support N0. After execution, `decision_rule_outcome` must record the threshold, observed value, whether the criterion passed, and analysis artifacts in structured form; `null_decision_rule_outcome` records the three null-conclusion checks separately. Failure to pass the positive criterion is not the same as the null model having obtained support. If the null-conclusion checks were not frozen or have not all passed, the result remains `unsupported_or_undecided`.

Empirical instances use four summary states: `supported`, `unsupported_or_undecided`, `null_supported`, and `not_evaluated`. A summary state does not replace the specific conclusion: which predetermined criteria passed, which were countered by evidence, and which remain unresolved only because of insufficient precision or materials must be recorded separately. Clear counterevidence and an inability to judge must not be conflated as one kind of evidence. `supported` requires an eligible instance to pass the positive-support check; `null_supported` requires all of equivalence or sufficiency, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance to pass; `not_evaluated` means only that the instance has not yet been run. Result records must link `evidence_refs` and `analysis_artifact_refs`, and retain the result timestamp, contract version, and deviation record. Neither an abstract root family nor a correctly formatted instance ID can produce support on its own.

Whether an instance can support a C contract is also constrained by `support_eligibility_rule`. Positive support comes only from `confirmatory` or `replication` instances with auditable versions and timestamps, whose status before results is `frozen_before_evidence` and whose completion under the frozen contract is recorded as `completed_from_frozen`. It additionally requires `decision_rule_outcome.passed=true`, and the result state and external evidence/analysis artifacts must be resolvable. A `falsification` instance may support only a pre-registered falsification or null-model judgment; it must not be used in reverse to rescue positive G. Instances that are `exploratory`, `draft`, `deviated_after_evidence`, or have undisclosed deviations may only generate candidates, record deviations, or remain unresolved; they must not be treated as having formal-instance eligibility in order to support C branches that require it. Internal definitions, concept labels, and the framework's own descriptions do not constitute instance-level empirical support.

The scale invariants shared by the four roots include the preselected subtype, success criterion, `decision_rule`, and `null_decision_rule`; these must be retained during scale transfer and must not be replaced. An appeal may challenge the candidate object, subtype, success criterion, N0, primary target or aggregation, model selection or ensembling, multiple comparisons, controls, positive threshold, null-conclusion rule, data split, extrapolation unit, contract version, pre-registration status, deviation record, and falsification conditions. If any item is shown to have changed after results, support eligibility must be reduced. A version produced after the fact must not overwrite the frozen record.

## 4.2 Human Empirical Instance Contracts

H1, H4, and H5 are not universal empirical question families, but they are likewise abstract families of empirical hypotheses. They cannot acquire truth values through names, interface fields, or framework definitions. They use a separate `human_empirical_instance_contract`: in the identity fields, `claim_id` replaces `root_id`, and only H1, H4, and H5 are allowed. The remaining provisions governing the pre-registration section, results section, missing-value encoding, version freezing, stages, four result states, deviation disclosure, positive-support check, null-conclusion check, evidence references, and analysis artifacts are isomorphic to those in the root-instance contract. The corresponding instances are formally called H1-instance, H4-instance, and H5-instance; empirical truth belongs to these specific H-instance cases, not to abstract H propositions.

An H-instance must also preselect exactly one subtype and one success criterion for that subtype before results are read. The following table is the standard registration classification for the single-primary-criterion procedure, not an exhaustive classification of meaning, power, or historical carriers. If the object or design at issue falls outside the list, the testable question and research procedure must be stated separately; it must not be forced into a favorable category.

| Proposition | Subtype or Outcome Family of Which Exactly One Must Be Preselected | Admissible Success Criteria | Stopping Point for the Conclusion |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H1 Meaning and coordination | Choose one of three: resource allocation outcomes, action-choice outcomes, or coordination outcomes | The one among `meaning_arrangement_resource_allocation_effect`, `meaning_arrangement_action_choice_effect`, and `meaning_arrangement_coordination_outcome_effect` corresponding to the selected subtype | Register only a conditional orientation anchor within the specified object, scale, window, and outcome family; do not infer a unified inner state, genuine consent, or a common will |
| H4 Power, mediation, and reflexivity | Choose one of four: evidence coverage, safety of expression, object behavior, or reflexive response | The one among `position_or_mediation_evidence_coverage_effect`, `position_or_mediation_expression_safety_effect`, `mediation_or_publicity_behavioral_response_effect`, and `observation_or_publication_reflexive_response_effect` corresponding to the selected subtype | Register only masking, amplification, behavior, or reflexive response within the specified position, mediation, disclosure conditions, channel, and outcome family; do not infer malice, responsibility, or automatic real-world action |
| H5 Historical trace carriers | First preselect exactly one subtype among five carrier families: `institutional_or_textual_record`, `role_or_organizational_arrangement`, `habit_or_practice`, `trauma_record`, and `collective_memory_carrier`; then provide the `selected_carrier_family_id` corresponding to that subtype and one specific atomic `selected_carrier` reference | Choose one of three: `threshold_persistence_over_preregistered_window`, `repeat_detection_across_preregistered_windows`, or `persistence_after_event_or_exposure_end` | Register only candidate traces within the specified carrier, observables, and window; obtain separate evidence under §4.5 for subsequent carrier effects; only historical increments beyond the current representation additionally require a G3-instance |

H1 must not treat “any favorable result establishes the claim” as a rule across the three outcomes of resources, action, and coordination. H4 must not select favorable results after the fact among evidence coverage, safety of expression, behavior, and reflexive response. H5 must not search across multiple carriers or durability criteria for a surviving result. H5's `selected_carrier` must be one nonempty atomic reference, not a list, and `selected_carrier_family_id` must exactly match the selected subtype's `carrier_family_id`; otherwise, the instance is already ineligible before results are read. Multiple targets, multiple models, and multiple comparisons still require the single primary target or aggregation, model selection or ensembling, and correction rules to be frozen in `decision_rule`. When the positive-support check does not pass, the default is `unsupported_or_undecided`. Only when all of equivalence or sufficiency, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance in the pre-registered `null_decision_rule` pass is the state `null_supported`. An exploratory H-instance, one with post-evidence deviations, or one with undisclosed deviations generates only candidates or unresolved judgments; it must not upgrade the HV03, HV08, or H5 historical-carrier routes to empirically established claims.

H2, H3, and H6 do not enter this empirical instance contract. H2 is a rule for distinguishing uptake and responsibility types. H3 is a classification rule for institutional write-back. H6 is the normative boundary of open-ended undertaking. They may constrain how an interface is classified or where it stops, but cannot produce empirical mechanism support through successful classification. Even if an H-instance receives `supported`, it increases only the strength of description or explanation; it does not generate value, responsibility, obligation, justification, authorization, or real-world action.

## 4.3 G1 Conditional Gain from Candidate Grouping

Proposition: In an instance whose object, scale, time window, K, candidate grouping, and threshold have all been registered in advance, if the candidate grouping still achieves the predetermined out-of-sample gain relative to a complexity-matched N0 after controlling for the common environment, sampling or classification bias, and grouping leakage, and passes boundary-perturbation checks and review using independent time blocks or locations, then the grouping receives limited support for object identification under that SP/T/K.

G1 does not test “whether this object ultimately exists in the world.” It tests whether the current candidate grouping explains predetermined out-of-sample differences better than a complexity-matched N0 that does not use information from the grouping. G1a permits only `out_of_sample_predictive_gain`; G1b permits only `out_of_sample_intervention_gain`. Neither subtypes nor criteria may be exchanged after results appear. The common environment, sampling procedure, classification bias, grouping leakage, and model complexity must enter the controls or competing explanations.

The minimum positive support requires predetermined evaluation metrics and thresholds, an out-of-sample gain relative to N0, robustness to boundary perturbation, and review using independent time blocks or locations; it also requires the instance to meet support eligibility. If the out-of-sample gain does not pass the positive threshold, or if the result is not reproduced under boundary perturbation or is not reproduced in independent review, the default is only that G1 is not supported or that the result is unresolved. N0 may be supported, or the corresponding G1-instance refuted, only when the equivalence or sufficiency test and the checks for statistical power, sensitivity, and tolerance in the pre-registered `null_decision_rule` pass. If the object, grouping, success criterion, N0, decision rules, thresholds, data split, or leakage controls were not frozen before results, the instance is suspended. Passing G1 permits only the registration of object-identification strength, boundary credibility, and the grouping's applicable window within the specified SP/T/K. It does not permit an ultimate ontological verdict, nor does it generate a subject, target, value, responsibility, or authorization.

## 4.4 G2 Specified-Channel Effects and Per-Dimension Boundedness

Proposition: G2 must preselect exactly one subtype and success criterion before results appear. In a pre-specified carrier or channel instance, the channel is registered as a candidate for causally carrying that transition only when the preselected controlled perturbation, controlled intervention, or natural variation meeting identification conditions produces a difference in the specified state transition that exceeds a predetermined threshold. Capacity, delay, reliability, controllability, and loss must be measured separately, channel by channel and dimension by dimension; unmeasured dimensions are not established along with the proposition.

G2a pre-registers controlled identification and must preselect exactly one of `controlled_perturbation_effect` and `controlled_intervention_effect`; “either perturbation or intervention succeeds” must not be written as a disjunctive criterion after the fact. G2b pre-registers natural variation meeting identification conditions, with `identified_natural_variation_channel_effect` as the only admissible criterion. Both subtypes must first specify the channel, target transition, quantity type and unit, comparative threshold, and dimensions to be measured. The null model is written only for the selected criterion: the preselected perturbation, intervention, or natural variation does not produce a difference in the target transition exceeding the threshold; criteria not preselected do not enter the instance's success or failure. Each bounded dimension separately takes no difference or a predetermined baseline as N0.

G2 does not claim that all causal processes have currently visible channels, nor that all channels are bounded in every dimension. If only delay has been measured, capacity, reliability, controllability, and loss must not be declared established along with it. Shifting may be traced further only for quantities of the same type, or quantities with explicit type conversion, units, an accounting boundary, and accounting rules. If the preselected criterion does not produce a difference above the threshold, the default record is only that positive G2 is not supported or is unresolved. The instance may be refuted only if it was pre-registered for falsification or a null judgment and the corresponding null-conclusion rule passes. When common inputs, alternative channels, or measurement protocols can explain the difference, support for the candidate channel should be withdrawn; favorable results on criteria that were not preselected must not be used to rescue it. G2 can locate only candidate causal carrying and the limits of measured dimensions. It cannot create a unified cost across domains, still less turn a carrier into a responsible party.

G2 tests specified channels and measured dimensions; it is not the sole entry point into all causal knowledge. If a qualified comparison identifies the total effect of a treatment while channels cannot yet be distinguished, the total effect should be retained and the channels marked unresolved; mechanisms must not be fabricated to fill in G2. If the total effect is near zero, local processes that offset or compensate for one another must not be negated on that basis alone; the relevant processes still require independent evidence. When intervention implementation, common inputs, selection, or measurement conditions change, review the current claim again. Switching afterward to a favorable criterion does not recover the original confirmatory eligibility.

## 4.5 Historical Formation, Current Carriers, and G3 Conditional Increments

Historical research first distinguishes three questions: how earlier experiences form the current state; how current carriers retain, update, and continue to affect later outcomes; and whether historical materials still provide additional information or effects given the currently registered representation. The first two can hold while the third does not. Lack of support for the third also cannot establish that the first two hold. G3 tests only the third question; it cannot replace all research into historical causation, learning, accumulation, continuation, or reproduction.

Proposition: G3 must preselect exactly one subtype and success criterion before results appear. If historical variables have been defined before future outcomes are observed and provide reviewable out-of-sample conditional-increment information for a predetermined outcome after controlling for the currently registered state, environment, and measurement protocol, G3a can support predictive path dependence. Beyond that conditional increment, G3b must also preselect one criterion among history erasure, history recovery, or an equivalent intervention, and supports causal path dependence only when that criterion exceeds the predetermined threshold.

G3a tests predictive path dependence, with `historical_conditional_predictive_gain` as the only admissible criterion. G3b tests causal path dependence and must preselect exactly one of `history_erasure_effect`, `history_restoration_effect`, and `equivalent_intervention_effect`; it must not wait for results and then select a favorable result from erasure, recovery, or an equivalent intervention. The former requires historical variables to provide an out-of-sample conditional increment for a predetermined future outcome beyond the currently registered state; the latter additionally requires an outcome change under the preselected causal criterion. Both G3a and G3b take as their null that the currently registered state is a sufficient statistic and historical terms provide no conditional increment. G3b's null additionally requires no outcome change under its own preselected intervention criterion; criteria not preselected play no part in success or failure.

The preservation of traces is not the same as their entering future transitions; a predictive gain is not the same as history having causal efficacy, either. If historical variables are defined only after future results have been seen, current-state information is plainly omitted, or a subtype and success criterion have not been uniquely preselected, the instance is suspended. If historical terms provide no positive increment after controlling for the current state, environment, and measurement protocol, or if G3b's preselected criterion does not pass the positive threshold, the default record is only not supported or unresolved. A corresponding refutation is formed only when the frozen falsification or null-conclusion rule has been followed to completion. Other unselected criteria must not be used to rescue the claim. Passing G3 permits only the registration of predictive or causal path differences within the pre-registered window; it does not permit inferences of historical fate, absolute irreversibility, responsibility, or authorization.

Scope of application: G3a and G3b are this framework's narrow classifications for “historical conditional increments beyond the currently registered state,” not definitions of all historical causation. Suppose earlier experience H changes current skill S, and S then affects outcome Y. If S already carries the relevant historical information sufficiently, H may add no further predictive information about Y given S. The narrow G3 condition is then not satisfied, but the historical formation process H→S→Y and learning in its general sense may still be supported by independent process materials or a research design. The total historical effect, current carrier, and conditional residual should be reported separately; G3 gains must not be manufactured by omitting known S. 〔Studies 03.03, 04.01–04.04, 24.04, 25.08〕

Intertemporal judgments register the carrier-transmission route and the historical-increment route separately. The carrier-transmission route requires a measured current state or carrier, grounds for how earlier inputs or changes entered that carrier, its retention or updating within the declared window, and longitudinal materials showing its continued effects on predetermined outcomes. Seeing only a lasting outcome or the existence of a carrier cannot confirm the carrier-transmission mechanism. A causal claim must additionally compare interruption, replacement, recovery, or natural variation meeting identification conditions under §2.4, and address common inputs, sustained exposure, selection, attrition, and measurement changes. When there are only correlational materials without the corresponding basis for identification, stop at an intertemporal description or a candidate mechanism.

Applying for formal mechanism support for the carrier branches below additionally requires an eligible G2-instance or the H-instance specified by that interface to support the relevant channel and outcome. H5 supports only traces within the selected carrier and window; it cannot by itself satisfy the check for subsequent effects or causation. The original instance's object, outcome, and window must cover the claim being made. A single immediate effect must not be extrapolated to a long-term effect, nor may several separately established local links be treated as a connected chain as a whole. Existing domain evidence still retains its own conclusions and limits under §2.1.1; the separation into routes in this section does not automatically confer eligibility as a G or H instance on it.

The historical-increment route separately tests conditional increments beyond the currently registered state under the G3 contract, with predictive and causal conclusions still kept separate. Carrier transmission can hold while G3 is not supported; failure of G3's positive-support check does not establish that carrier transmission holds, either. Known current states must not be omitted to obtain a historical increment, and routes must not be switched after results to rescue the original claim. Each route separately states its basis, competing explanations, failure conditions, and ceiling on conclusions. Accumulation, continuation, learning, delayed recovery, and intertemporal reproduction do not acquire support by being named.

Historical conditional increments depend on the choice of current representation; they must not be treated as an essence of the system independent of representation. Let the complete current state be `x_t`, the registered representation be `z_t=π(x_t)`, and the retained history be `h_t`. If history still provides an increment for a future target given `z_t`, this may be because `h_t` recovers current differences collapsed by `π`; that alone does not establish that the complete dynamics contains an additional history term. Proportionate comparisons should be made among available, task-relevant current carriers, measurement errors, and model complexity. Known current-state information must not be deliberately deleted to manufacture historical gains.

In reality, the complete state is usually unavailable. Thus, “can in principle be expanded into a sufficient state” must not be treated as having already established the absence of historical increments in this case. If the materials support only an increment relative to the currently registered state, report it under that representation, target, and window; redo the comparison when the representation changes. The value of using history to assist prediction, how history forms current carriers, and whether a narrow G3 instance receives support are three questions that can have different answers. Appendix C.11 gives an exact example of historical gains arising from omissions in the current state projection.

## 4.6 G4 Representational Closure and Object or Intervention Conversion

Proposition: G4 must preselect exactly one subtype and one success criterion for that subtype before results appear. G4a, closure failure, may test conditional-increment information, conditional predictive gain, or conditional intervention gain from excluded scale variables given the retained variables at the target scale. G4b, object or intervention conversion, may test noncommutation between scale transformation M and object dynamics or intervention, failure of a target mapping to satisfy the pre-frozen K, changes in effective relations, or changes in intervention responses. Only when the preselected criterion exceeds the predetermined threshold or tolerance is the corresponding instance supported.

G4a and G4b answer different questions. G4a permits the success criteria `conditional_information_gain`, `conditional_predictive_gain`, and `conditional_intervention_gain`; each instance may preselect only one. Its null model is that, under the preselected criterion, the excluded scale variables provide no conditional increment beyond the threshold given the retained variables. G4b permits the success criteria `object_dynamics_non_commutation`, `intervention_non_commutation`, `identity_criterion_violation`, `effective_relation_change`, and `intervention_response_change`; each instance likewise may preselect only one. The null model is also written only for the selected item: object dynamics commute, interventions commute, the target mapping satisfies the pre-frozen K, effective relations do not change beyond tolerance, or intervention responses do not differ beyond tolerance. The five must not be combined into a disjunctive fallback.

General correlation, unconditional coupling, similar narratives, and omitted variables at the same scale are all insufficient to support G4. The instance is suspended if a subtype and a success criterion were not uniquely preselected before results, or if any of the D3/E5 mapping, comparison models, positive threshold, `null_decision_rule`, statistical-power or sensitivity check, and tolerance has not been frozen. When the positive criterion passes, G4a registers only closure failure within the pre-registered variables, metrics, scales, and window; G4b registers only the preselected conversion candidate under the pre-registered mapping, K, scales, and window. When the positive criterion does not pass, the default record is only that G4 is not supported or is unresolved. Only when the equivalence or sufficiency test, statistical-power or sensitivity check, and tolerance in the null-conclusion rule actually pass may G4a register closure within the limits of the criterion, and G4b register that the preselected conversion was not detected. A result that is merely “not statistically significant,” or that merely fails to pass the positive threshold, must not be registered directly as closure, absence of object conversion, or equivalence of interventions. Unselected criteria must not be used for rescue or refutation. Passing G4 does not mean that higher scales are truer, more important, or have greater authority to act.

G4 maintains a symmetrical research interest in closure and nonclosure. Supporting closure within a specified scope under a registered test can show that a concise representation is already sufficient for the current task; detecting nonclosure shows that the representation, task, or scope of operations needs revision. `supported` and `null_supported` are only evidential states relative to the preselected factual assertion, not rankings of the quality of a method or of theoretical progress.

G4 comparisons must specify the targets, horizons, environments, and admissible operations relative to which closure is assessed. Sufficiency for prediction under ordinary conditions does not guarantee continued sufficiency under a new intervention; the same macro-level state may respond differently to the same operation because of differences in the composition collapsed by the representation. After an insufficiency is found, necessary variables may be added, composition or history retained, the set of actions limited, the horizon shortened, or the use narrowed. There is no need to recover all micro-level details. Closure holding can itself be a successful result of an effective macro-level description; emergence, aggregation, omissions at the same scale, and cross-boundary transmission do not universally require G4 closure failure.

Example of intervention sufficiency: two micro-level states A₁ and A₂ both have probability 0.2 of entering B under natural evolution, so merging them into A may be sufficient for this predictive task. Under a particular intervention, their probabilities of entering B are 0.8 and 0.1, respectively. The macro-level intervention outcome then depends on the share p of A₁, with probability 0.1+0.7p. One can then try adding only p rather than recovering all micro-level details. This teaching construction distinguishes predictive closure from intervention closure; it does not establish that real-world systems necessarily take these values. 〔Study 05.06〕

### 4.6.1 Formal Closure, Approximate Closure, and Empirical Eligibility

In a deterministic model, if the source state space is X, the representation is π:X→Y with Y=π(X), and the one-step dynamics is F:X→X, then a macro-level update F̄:Y→Y satisfying π∘F=F̄∘π exists if and only if any two source states with the same representation still have the same representation after updating. This condition is an exact closure criterion within the model. If it holds throughout the state domain, commutation over multiple steps under fixed dynamics follows by induction; holding only at a few visited states cannot be extrapolated to closure over the entire domain. Sufficiency for a static query cannot replace this dynamic condition, either.

When multiple operations are admissible, the specific version of each operation should be declared and its update under state projection tested. If a macro-level operation does not determine its corresponding micro-level implementation, it is also necessary to check whether different admissible implementations yield identical macro-level responses or responses within tolerance. A stochastic model must test the conditional transition probabilities to each target state or target block; identical means alone are insufficient to establish closure of the distribution. Adding a particular composition proportion can repair a one-step prediction at a specified time, but does not automatically supply an update law for that proportion itself or closure under every operation.

Approximate closure must be tied to an error quantity, initial conditions, targets, an operation set, and a horizon. Suppose the per-step error under state projection is at most ε, and the Lipschitz constant of the proposed macro-level update on the relevant region does not exceed L. Starting from the same initial representation, the error at step t is then at most `ε∑_{j=0}^{t−1}L^j`; this bound requires the premises to hold at every step. When L<1, a uniform upper bound ε/(1−L) is available; when L=1, the bound is tε; when L>1, the error bound may increase rapidly. A one-step mean error or a short-term fitting error must not be substituted directly into this state-by-state, step-by-step bound.

These proofs provide structure under stated conditions; they do not confer real-world G4-instance support. Measurement, mapping, samples, operations, and error conditions must still be checked empirically, and the positive-support check and the null-conclusion check must be carried out separately. A formal proof of exact closure and failure to detect a deviation in limited data have different evidential statuses. Without sufficient sensitivity, the latter remains unresolved.

## 4.7 A Fictional Group of Root Instances

The following m1–m4 serve only to show how the contracts work. They describe a fictional transport model with multiple layers, cite no external observations, and constitute no empirical support for any G proposition.

m1 / G1a. The candidate object consists of several interconnected groups of units. B, X, T, SP, K, the grouping method, and a complexity-matched N0 are frozen in advance, and only `out_of_sample_predictive_gain` is selected, together with its metric and threshold. If the candidate grouping is effective only in the training sample, or if the gain disappears after a slight boundary perturbation, m1 fails. Limited support for object identification within that window is obtained only if the result is also reproduced in independent time blocks.

m2 / G2a. For m1's candidate object, specify a transmission channel and target state transition in advance, select only `controlled_perturbation_effect`, then block the channel under controlled conditions. If the blockage changes the target transition by more than the threshold, the channel may be registered only as a candidate for causal carrying. Suppose only delay is measured in this example; capacity, reliability, controllability, and loss remain unknown and cannot be established along with m2.

m3 / G3a. First define the historical variable “backlog in the preceding three rounds,” select only `historical_conditional_predictive_gain`, then control for the current queue, environmental inputs, and measurement protocol. If the historical term still provides an out-of-sample conditional increment for the next round's outcome, predictive path dependence may be registered. Without an intervention for history erasure or recovery, this must not be upgraded to causal path dependence.

m4 / G4a. Before results, select only the closure-failure subtype and `conditional_information_gain`, and freeze the micro-level and aggregated scales, M, retained variables, excluded variables, metric, and threshold. Closure failure within that window is registered only if the excluded local temporal sequence provides a conditional increment above the threshold under that criterion given the aggregate variables. A predictive or intervention gain must not be substituted after results are seen, nor may the record be rewritten as G4b object conversion.

This example shows that the four instances can generate further questions for one another, but do not replace one another. Passing m1 does not guarantee that m2's channel is effective; passing m2 does not guarantee m3's historical effect; and passing m3 does not guarantee m4's scale-closure failure.

## 4.8 Epistemic Constraints E1–E5

### 4.8.1 E1 Constraint on Object Declaration

For the current judgment, E1 requires the object, scope, scale, time window, necessary variables, and the object's identity conditions to be explicit; comparative claims must separately specify the corresponding baseline or null model. When a field is missing, state which inferred conclusion it limits and how, without canceling independently established source facts because of a missing format field. When D0 fields are incomplete, the analyst's grouping must not be declared an objective structure; ordinary facts, textual meanings, and mathematical proofs are still reviewed under their own evidence and burden of argument.

### 4.8.2 E2 Observation Position and Model Limits

Observation positions, measurement channels, and model choices limit what is visible; one record must not be treated as the object's entire state. Nor is it presupposed that every specific task must add perspectives. Whether a representation is sufficient depends on the target, horizon, environment, and admissible operations. Invisibility is not the same as nonexistence. When blind spots relevant to the task are substantial, evidence should be added, the use narrowed, or the judgment kept unresolved; whether probing is permissible still requires comparing risks, windows, and authorization.

### 4.8.3 E3 Conditional Observational Participation

Observational participation is registered only when observation, naming, scoring, or publication enters into actual causal coupling with the object. Temporal order alone, or a mechanism story that “being observed changes the object,” is insufficient to pass. A channel must be located, and a counterfactual established for no observation, alternative observation, or blockage.

### 4.8.4 E4 Retaining Competing Explanations and Residuals

In an explanation, competing accounts that the materials permit and that are sufficient to change the judgment should be compared, and counterexamples and unexplained residuals retained. The competition must specify what is disputed: the size of an effect, its mechanism, contextual meaning, the scope of extrapolation, or normative premises. Compatible explanations can bear responsibilities at different layers. When no reasonable competing explanation is available, one must not be fabricated to fill a quota; the scope of the search and failure conditions must still be stated. Openness, love, complexity, or a sense of theoretical wholeness must not be used to fill gaps in evidence. How competing explanations that have not yet been distinguished limit action must be judged in conjunction with harm, reversibility, external obligations, and current permissions.

### 4.8.5 E5 Testing Cross-Scale Transfer

Cross-scale transfer must pass D3's tests of the mapping, invariants, changed items, lost items, error, competing explanations, and residuals. The only hard inferential premise for E5 is D3; E1, E4, EVIDENCE, and ANALOGY are all method checks. They do not enter the inferential acyclic graph, nor masquerade as additional empirical causes. E5 does not depend on G4 being true; conversely, every G4-instance must pass E5. Expansion of observational scope does not imply expansion of authorization scope, and the J axis must not be extrapolated automatically along with the other scale axes.

## 4.9 Evidential Status of Human-Experience Clauses

H1 (meaning and coordination), H4 (power, mediation, and reflexivity), and H5 (historical carriers) are families of empirical hypotheses awaiting testing. The specific factual assertions for all three are tested against their objects and evidence. This procedure grants eligibility for formal support only to an H-instance for which `human_empirical_instance_contract` has been completed and the corresponding criteria met; definitions, interfaces, and the framework's own formulations are not external empirical support. Without eligible external instance records, `empirical_evidence_records=[]`, and the evidential status is `requires_instance_evidence`.

Thus, H1 must preselect exactly one outcome family among resources, action, or coordination in each case and test differences in meaning arrangements. H4 must preselect exactly one outcome family among evidence coverage, safety of expression, object behavior, or reflexive response in each case, then establish the channel and counterfactual for position, mediation, or disclosure conditions. H5, “historical trace carriers,” must preselect exactly one carrier family, one specific atomic carrier reference, and one durability criterion. Candidate traces within the specified carrier and window may be registered, and predefined candidate historical variables submitted to a G3-instance, only if the family matches the subtype; the historical event, baseline, trace observables, threshold, and durability window were all frozen before results; and the selected criterion passes. Historical path increments beyond the current state and C4's corresponding repair window are assessed separately under G3/C4. Historical effects transmitted by current carriers and general effects of intervention timing obtain independent grounds under §4.5 and §6.10, respectively; protection, harm reduction, or repair actions separately undergo C12, explicit N premises, and O procedures. Proposition names, internal definitions, or this framework's own narrative cannot complete instance testing on behalf of these three hypothesis families.

## 4.10 Outcome Contracts and Mechanism Conditions C1–C12

Each contract, according to its own definitions, empirical basis, additional conditions, or normative and procedural premises, delimits the outputs that may be registered; branches that depend on root instances must obtain the corresponding instance support. Inferential dependencies list the hard premises that enter the acyclic inference graph; method checks list protocol requirements, which determine whether a judgment may be released but do not serve as empirical causes. Domain specialization and applicability relations are explained separately in the body. The following four fixed contract fields remain synchronized with the outcome contracts, while evidence, counterexamples, and failure boundaries are presented together by groups of inferred conclusions.

The C identifiers serve different argumentative tasks. C1, C2, the historical-increment branch of C4, and C6 primarily register the outcomes within specified limits already supported by the corresponding empirical instances, adding a scope of use. C3, C5, C7, and C9–C11 must also combine separately listed mechanism, temporal, or domain conditions. The basic C8 pattern depends on separately obtained evidence of variation, differential retention, and reproduction; C12 is a check for normative procedures. A shared numbering scheme does not turn these into empirical laws of the same kind. Nor does citing the same materials again in a root instance and an inferred conclusion constitute a second piece of independent support.

[^p04-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

## 4.11 Group 1: Objects, Carrying, and Feedback

### 4.11.1 C1 Objects and Boundary Strength

Inferential dependencies: D0; G1

Additional conditions: Use a G1-instance that has completed pre-registration and obtained support for the current instance; the candidate object S*, N0, the evaluation threshold, the extrapolation unit, and the boundary-perturbation plan have been frozen.

Permitted conclusions: Within that SP/T/K and extrapolation unit, the following results may be registered. They are the strength of object identification, boundary credibility, and the window in which the candidate grouping applies.

Prohibited leaps: Turning limited support for object identification into an ultimate ontological verdict; inferring a natural boundary, unified subject, common target, or S0–S6 stage; inferring the value of persistence, an allocation of responsibility, a choice, or authorization to act.

Method checks: E1; E4; EVIDENCE

The derivation first uses D0 to freeze the candidate object without presupposing its validity, then reads the out-of-sample gain of the same G1-instance relative to a complexity-matched N0. Finally, boundary perturbation and independent review are combined to register the strength of support and applicable window.

### 4.11.2 C2 Carrying and Channel Constraints

Inferential dependencies: D0; G2

Additional conditions: Use a pre-registered G2-instance that has obtained support for the current instance. When discussing shifting, the source and target quantities must be of the same type or have an explicit type conversion. The quantities' units and equality criteria, the time window, the accounting boundary, and the conversion mapping or accounting rules have been frozen. Capacity, delay, reliability, controllability, and loss are registered channel by channel and dimension by dimension. Unmeasured dimensions remain unknown.

Permitted conclusions: Candidate causal carrying for a specified state transition may be registered, together with channel limits within the measured types, units, windows, and dimensions. Shifting may be traced only when the conditions for quantities of the same type and for mapping are satisfied.

Prohibited leaps: Combining quantities of different types or nonconvertible quantities into a unified cost; generalizing from one measured, bounded dimension to all dimensions or all channels; automatically identifying a carrier or a channel as the responsible party or the cost bearer; deriving conservation across domains from the occupation of resources.

Method checks: E1; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

The derivation holds only for the specified channel, target transition, and measured dimensions. Quantities of the same type can be traced through equality of units; quantities of different types must go through explicit conversion or an accounting mapping. Unmeasured dimensions and quantities outside the boundary remain unknown, rather than being recorded as conserved, vanished, or costs already shifted.

### 4.11.3 C3 Effective Feedback and Feedback-Mediated Learning

Inferential dependencies: D2; G2

Additional conditions: The feedback branch must have a return channel, temporal order, changed fields, and a counterfactual with no return or a blocked channel. Effective feedback requires only a change in at least one subsequent state, probability, or constraint; it does not require a durable historical trace. The carrier-learning branch separately checks the update carrier, retention or updating rules, subsequent invocation over multiple rounds, a predetermined task comparison, and the corresponding causal evidence under §4.5. The historical-increment learning branch additionally requires a G3-instance, updates that can be retained, repeated rounds, and a predetermined task comparison.

Permitted conclusions: The basic branch may register effective feedback, its time lag, and the fields it acts on. When the carrier-learning branch's conditions hold, candidate feedback-mediated learning may be registered within the specified updates, task, and window. With an additional G3-instance, updates that can be retained, and repeated rounds, candidate historical-increment feedback-mediated learning may be registered.

Prohibited leaps: Equating a feedback entry point or proof of receipt with effective feedback; equating one state update with learning, repair, or positive improvement; inferring a direction of value or authorization to act directly from facts about feedback or learning.

Method checks: E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

The basic branch uses a G2-instance to identify the return channel as a candidate for causal carrying, then compares conditions with a return against no return or blockage under D2. Carrier learning must trace how updates are retained, invoked in subsequent rounds, and affect the predetermined task, and must check longitudinal and causal materials under §4.5; sufficiency of the current state does not negate this route. Historical-increment learning additionally requires G3 to provide an increment beyond the currently registered state. The two kinds of evidence cannot substitute for one another.

The branches are registered as `C3-feedback` (a return channel, changes in subsequent states or transitions, and a blocking counterfactual), `C3-carried-learning` (additional evidence under §4.5 of an update carrier, invocation over multiple rounds, and task outcomes), and `C3-learning` (the historical-increment kind; additionally G3, with updates that can be retained, repeated rounds, and an out-of-sample comparison on a predetermined task).

| Contract[^p05-tn-contract] | Minimum Evidence | Key Counterexamples | Failure Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | Complete D0; a G1-instance passing the pre-registration check; out-of-sample gain, boundary perturbation, and independent review | The gain disappears in an independent block; slight boundary perturbation invalidates it; N0 absorbs the entire gain | Return to candidate grouping when G1 does not pass or SP/T/K is exceeded |
| C2 | G2 passing the threshold; quantity types, units, equality or conversion mappings; measurement channel by channel and dimension by dimension; accounting boundary and window | A redundant channel replaces it completely; quantities of different types lack a mapping; common inputs explain the difference | When the channel, quantities of the same type, equality of units, or conversion mapping are insufficient, describe only inputs, occupation, loss, or candidate limits |
| C3 | Return channel, temporal sequence, blocking counterfactual, and changed fields; carrier learning additionally has update retention, invocation over multiple rounds, and a task comparison; historical-increment learning additionally has G3 | Signals arrive but transitions do not change; updates are not retained, or are not invoked; common inputs or proxy indicators explain the difference | Without a return, or without subsequent change, describe only signal arrival; when evidence for the learning route is insufficient, stop at effective feedback; absence of G3 blocks only the historical-increment branch |

## 4.12 Group 2: History, Load, Scale, and Spillovers

### 4.12.1 C4 Historical Effects, Path Differences, and Repair Windows

Inferential dependencies: D1; the historical-increment branch additionally requires G3, while the current-carrier and intervention-timing branches obtain the corresponding evidence under §4.5 and §2.4.

Additional conditions: Historical variables for the G3 branch must be defined before future results, with the currently registered state, environment, and measurement protocol controlled; the predictive and causal subtypes of historical increments must not be conflated. Obtain evidence for general historical formation and for the effects of current carriers through the separate route set out in §4.5. Discussion of a repair window additionally requires K*/F* made public in advance, comparisons of intervention at different times, and the corresponding causal identification. G3 is added only when an additional historical residual is claimed.

Permitted conclusions: The G3 branch may register predictive or causal historical increments within the pre-registered window and under the current representation. The current-carrier route may register limited historical effects according to its own materials. With additional K*/F*, timing comparisons, and the corresponding basis for identification, a conditional repair window may be registered; this does not require the outcome to retain a historical residual beyond the current state.

Prohibited leaps: Equating trace preservation with a historical effect; equating predictive gain with causation or irreversibility; equating path differences with fate, progress, decline, responsibility, or regression to an earlier stage; claiming repair before K*/F* have been disclosed.

Method checks: E1; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

Evolution is first classified under D1; historical effects cannot be inferred from a definition. The current-carrier route checks formation, retention, invocation, and subsequent outcomes; the historical-conditional-increment route reads G3a's predictive gain or G3b's causal basis separately. A repair window compares how interventions at different times affect the attainability of K*/F* made public in advance and the price to be paid. Only when it also claims a historical residual beyond the current representation does the repair window bear the corresponding G3 testing burden.

### 4.12.2 C5 Load, Capacity, and Recovery

Inferential dependencies: D0; G2

Additional conditions: The instantaneous branch must have definitions of demand and capacity of the same type, units or a conversion mapping, measurement within the same window, and distribution across positions. The carrier-accumulation branch must trace how load enters the current damage or capacity state, the process of accumulation or recovery across windows, and its subsequent effects under §4.5, and satisfy G2's channel requirements; the historical-increment accumulation branch additionally requires a G3-instance and a locatable historical carrier.

Permitted conclusions: The basic branch may register an instantaneous load–capacity shortfall at a specified window and position. When carrier-accumulation conditions hold, candidate cumulative damage or delayed recovery may be registered within that carrier, window, and outcome. With an additional G3-instance, candidate historical-increment accumulation or delayed recovery beyond the current state may be registered.

Prohibited leaps: Claiming that load necessarily accumulates monotonically or that overload necessarily causes collapse; directly subtracting demand and capacity of different types; treating an instantaneous shortfall as automatic proof of durable damage; allowing a need for recovery to directly generate an obligation of sacrifice for a named subject.

Method checks: E1; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

The basic branch freezes the types and units of demand, capacity, replenishment, loss, and the time window, uses a G2-instance to confirm the carrying channel, then compares the immediate shortfall and spillover positions within the same window. Accumulation and delayed recovery require materials spanning windows: the carrier route identifies how historical load forms the current state, how that state is maintained or recovers, and how it affects subsequent outcomes under §4.5; the historical-increment route separately uses G3 to test increments beyond the currently registered state. Sufficiency of the current state is compatible with a carrier explanation, but cannot replace evidence of its formation process and causation.

The branches are registered as `C5-instant` (a demand–capacity mapping for quantities of the same type and measurement within the same window), `C5-carried-cumulative` (additional evidence under §4.5 of a current carrier, accumulation or recovery across windows, and subsequent effects), and `C5-cumulative` (the historical-increment kind; additionally G3, with a historical carrier and subsequent differences in capacity or recovery).

### 4.12.3 C6 Scale Closure and Object Conversion

Inferential dependencies: D3; G4

Additional conditions: Before results, preselect exactly one of G4a and G4b, and from that subtype's `allowed_success_criteria` freeze exactly one `selected_success_criterion`. M(SP0→SP1), retained items, changed items, lost items, error, comparison models, positive thresholds, and tolerance have been frozen. If single-scale closure within a specified scope or failure to detect the preselected conversion is to be registered, the equivalence or sufficiency test, the check of statistical power or sensitivity, and the tolerance in `null_decision_rule` must be frozen before results and must actually pass.

Permitted conclusions: G4a's single-scale closure failure within pre-registered variables, metrics, scales, and windows, or G4b's object/intervention conversion within pre-registered mappings, K, scales, and windows, may be registered separately. When the positive criterion does not pass, the default record is only not supported or unresolved. Only when the pre-registered equivalence or sufficiency test and the checks for statistical power, sensitivity, and tolerance pass may closure within the limits of the criterion be registered for G4a, or failure to detect the preselected conversion for G4b; neither may be extrapolated to unselected criteria.

Prohibited leaps: Using general correlation, unconditional coupling, or omissions at the same scale to support G4; combining G4a and G4b after the fact; changing success criteria within the same subtype after the fact or using unselected criteria to rescue the claim; directly calling a result that is not statistically significant or does not pass the positive threshold single-scale closure, absence of object conversion, or equivalence of interventions; treating a wider scale range as a truer explanation or greater authorization; treating a shared term as proof of object or intervention identity.

Method checks: E4; E5; EVIDENCE

G4a separately tests conditional increments from excluded scale variables. G4b tests only its preselected item among noncommutation of object dynamics, noncommutation of intervention, failure of the pre-frozen K, change in effective relations, or change in intervention response. The G4-instance cited by C6 must have frozen both one subtype and one success criterion before results; switching is prohibited after results, whether between subtypes or within one subtype.

### 4.12.4 C7 Local Improvement and Cross-Boundary Spillover

Inferential dependencies: G2; claims that actually cross scales additionally require a D3/E5 mapping, and claims of closure failure or object/intervention conversion further require the corresponding G4 instance.

Additional conditions: The local target, object boundary, window of impact, and variables outside the boundary have been registered. A G2-instance locates the cross-boundary channel, with temporal order, a mechanism bridge, and a blocking or substitution counterfactual between the local change and the outcome outside the boundary. Crossing an object boundary or an administrative boundary does not automatically constitute a change of scale; D3/E5 is checked separately when scales are actually crossed. The corresponding G4 is tested separately only when closure failure, object conversion, or intervention conversion is additionally claimed. Using “externality” requires stating the costs and benefits and who bears or receives them. These must be specified within the institutional or accounting arrangements at issue.

Permitted conclusions: Cross-boundary spillover accompanying local improvement, together with its distribution across positions, may be registered when the mechanism bridge and counterfactual hold; it is called an externality only when an additional institutional or accounting definition is available.

Prohibited leaps: Claiming that every local improvement necessarily harms a larger scale; directly calling cross-boundary correlation a spillover mechanism; still calling something an externality without an institutional or accounting definition; automatically assigning compensation, responsibility, or an action plan upon finding a spillover.

Method checks: E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE; additionally E5 when scales are actually crossed.

First measure the predetermined improvement in the local target, then trace outputs, risks, resource occupation, or signals outside the boundary along the channel specified by G2, using proportionate comparisons to distinguish a common shock from a cross-boundary effect. When making a cross-scale statement, specify the D3/E5 mapping; when stating scale-closure failure or object conversion, provide the corresponding G4 evidence. A model with sound closure can also represent cross-boundary effects correctly; inadequacy of a model must not be made a necessary condition for the existence of an effect. The institutional or accounting definition further determines whether the costs and benefits at issue are externalities.

| Contract | Minimum Evidence | Key Counterexamples | Failure Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C4 | The historical-increment branch requires G3 and control of the current representation; the carrier route obtains evidence under §4.5; the repair branch requires K*/F*, timing comparisons, and the corresponding causal basis | Carrier formation or subsequent effects have not been distinguished; the residual is absorbed by a sufficient current state; intervention timing makes no stable difference | Absence of G3 blocks only additional historical increments; without causal evidence, do not call it historical causation; without K*/F*, or without a timing basis, do not call it a repair window |
| C5 | G2-instance; demand and capacity of the same type or a conversion mapping; measurement by position within the same window; carrier accumulation requires formation, retention, recovery, and causal comparison across windows; the historical-increment kind additionally has G3 | Capacity expands at the same time; demand and capacity are incomparable; no subsequent difference remains after the shortfall is removed; sustained common inputs explain the outcome | When type, unit, window, or channel is insufficient, describe only a limited resource state; insufficient evidence across windows does not establish an accumulation mechanism; absence of G3 blocks only the historical-increment branch |
