An identity mapping, reversible encoding, or mapping sufficient for the target task may yield no detected relevant loss. The rule is therefore “do not assume losslessness in advance,” not “necessarily lossy.”

| Audit Item | Minimum Question | Prohibited Substitutions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Preservation | Which facts, relations, modalities, rights, or uncertainties must stay the same? | Preserving a title does not amount to preserving its semantics |
| Change | Which formats, units, levels of granularity, or expressions may change? | Permitted change does not amount to arbitrary change in meaning |
| Collapse | Which source distinctions are merged on the target side? | Collapsing a distinction is not the same as its absence from the source |
| Omission | What was left out of the target representation, and why? | Non-disclosure for protection does not amount to absence |
| Addition | Which explanations, inferences, or target variables does the conversion produce? | Adding a variable does not amount to discovering a real-world entity |
| Return | Which parent record or contract is revisited when an anomaly occurs? | Returning only reopens review. It does not patch the old run |

Round-trip reconstruction can serve as a test but is insufficient by itself. A conversion may appear to round-trip by using hidden source records while failing to preserve the semantics of the target task. A one-way summary may be sufficient for the specified task. Review must return to the predeclared items to preserve, the tolerances for loss, and the results on the target side.

Whether the target representation retains a needed distinction and whether the actual rule uses it correctly are two separate judgments. An error in acting on the representation does not by itself establish a recasting loss. If the distinction is still discernible but used incorrectly, register the gap in the rule or its execution. Representational loss and execution errors can also coexist. Their grounds should be stated separately, and other local results that have already been established should be retained.

When both kinds of failure occur, the same unavoidable error should not be counted again as an additional failure of the rule. If the target answer is nonunique within a preimage, no deterministic rule using only the current representation can answer correctly for all source states. This by itself establishes that the representation is insufficient. If the target answer is unique in another nonempty preimage but the actual rule gives the wrong answer, that provides a sufficient witness to an independent error in the rule. When the actual internal representation is unknown, the original material’s continued public availability does not establish that those implementing the rule had the distinction available to them at the time. The existence of material, timely access to it, and actual use must be checked separately.

### 5.10.3 Effective Variables, Closure, and Returning in Response to Residuals

An effective variable is a candidate variable proposed for a target scale or task in order to compress the state and improve a prespecified explanation, foresight[^p06-tn-foresight], or comparison of options. It must be tied to an object, scale, time window, task, measurement, and simple baseline. Error, failure, and retirement must also be specified for the variable. Being effective does not amount to being a real entity, a root cause, a universal variable, or a basis for authorization.

The target representation carries no presumption of dynamic closure. For static descriptions or same-scale queries, first check sources, conversion rules, and answers to the task, using F1 and F4 when necessary. Check formal dynamical models for closure under F2 and obtain separate evidence for their real-world mappings. G-specific fields that do not apply to a descriptive mapping may be marked `not_applicable` with reasons. Apply the positive-support and null-conclusion checks of the relevant G4 instance only when seeking formal empirical support under §4.6 for scale closure, object conversion, or intervention conversion. A scale change must not be fabricated to meet a format requirement. Failure to find a significant increment does not by itself establish closure. When an increment occurs, register the corresponding impact as a candidate for memory, noise, hysteresis, an unresolved term, or a residual. A new entity must not be named directly on the basis of the shape.

The unified scale transformation record must specify the target task in its purpose and semantics fields. Its transformation, evidence, and loss fields must record the parent representation, collapsed differences, return point, reconstruction test, and candidate effective variables for the target. If relevant information is missing, retain an explicit missingness status. The completeness of a table must not be used to presume that all of the source semantics have been preserved.

## 5.11 Representing Parallelism and Nesting Together

Real-world structure is not a single hierarchical tree. Local containment relations can coexist with lateral networks, membership overlap, competition for resources, and bridging through platforms. The representation layer uses a directed multigraph of relations. Nodes retain their own object contracts, and edges retain their own relation contracts. “Higher layer” denotes only a particular relation of containment, jurisdiction, or aggregation. It carries no implication of greater reality, correctness, or authority.

When several circles share an environment but have no direct channel, the model retains a node for the common conditions and must not invent a direct edge between the circles. When a channel is open only within an event window, its edge has a start time and an end time. When different relations run in opposite directions, create separate edges. Only in this way can one determine after an event which relation actually changed, instead of redrawing the whole structure at once as a story told after the fact.

[^p06-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

[^p06-tn-protocol]: Translator's note: A protocol here is a methodological check that a judgment must pass.

[^p06-tn-circle]: Translator's note: *Circle* renders 圈层. Circles may be parallel, nested, overlapping, bridged, or competing, and may also form only temporarily around particular events; the term does not imply a hierarchy.

[^p06-tn-foresight]: Translator's note: Foresight in this book requires registration of a target, horizon, and baseline, together with calibration. A single such judgment is called a forecast.

<a id="part_6"></a>

# Part 6 Operation and Evolution

This Part develops explanations of processes from object contracts[^p07-tn-contract]. Operation, feedback, learning, maintenance, load, phase, and systemic selection are not a set of interchangeable narrative terms but conditional mechanisms with distinct inferential premises, enabling conditions, counterfactuals, and stopping points. The name of a mechanism does not by itself constitute a sufficient condition. Only when the checks on dependencies, conditions, minimum evidence, and competing explanations all pass may a mechanism instance be registered within the specified SP/T/K.

## 6.1 Operation Registration

An operation record must include at least inputs, a specified carrier or channel, state transitions, outputs, environmental conditions, a time window, and possible traces. If the output returns, also register the return channel, the receiving position, and the fields that change. For a claim about intertemporal effects, first distinguish historical formation, transmission by current carriers, and additional historical increments under §4.5, and check the materials on timing and mechanism for each. Only a claim about an additional historical increment requires a comparison between a history-aware model and a model in which the current state is sufficient. If the operation crosses scales, also complete the D3/E5 mapping. This registration makes it possible to test each delay, loss, alternative channel, and gap in evidence, as well as the limits of extrapolation, without forcing a complex process into a linear chain.

## 6.2 CM-FEEDBACK Effective Feedback

Inferential dependencies: D2 and G2. E4, CAUSAL, and EVIDENCE are method checks, not additional empirical causes. Effective feedback does not require G3.

The enabling conditions are: a prior state or output enters a later process through a specified return channel that a G2-instance has identified; compared with a condition with no return or with the channel blocked, at least one subsequent state, transition probability, or constraint differs by more than the threshold; and the temporal order and the changed fields have been registered before the results are known. The mechanism chain is:

Prior state or output → return signal → transmission through the specified channel → signal uptake at the receiving position → change in a subsequent state, probability, or constraint → review against a blocking counterfactual

Minimum evidence includes a G2-instance that passed its pre-registration check, the return channel and receiving position, temporal order, the changed fields, the subsequent difference, and a comparison with no return or a blocked channel. Do not register effective feedback if the signal arrives but the subsequent transition is unchanged, if an independent common input explains the change, or if the difference still remains unchanged after the return channel is cut. Effective feedback establishes only that the return caused a subsequent change on that occasion. It does not amount to learning, repair, or a positive outcome. It requires no durable historical trace.

### 6.2.1 Feedback Delay and the Stability Interval

A local model that can be analyzed on its own is x\_{t+1}=x\_t−k x\_{t−d}. Here x\_t is the signed, dimensionless deviation from the target, k is a fixed real-valued gain, and d is a fixed nonnegative integer delay. The sampling interval is fixed. The model excludes external shocks, measurement noise, saturation in execution, and state bounds. With respect to all finite initial histories, the necessary and sufficient condition for asymptotic stability of the zero solution is 0<k<2sin[π/(4d+2)]. Equality at the bounds is outside the stability interval. The proof is in Appendix C.10.

For example, let k=0.8 and set every entry in the initial history to 1. The model is asymptotically stable at delays of 0 or 1 step and unstable at delays of 2 or 3 steps. In all four cases the first step takes the value from 1 to 0.2. With the channel blocked at k=0, the value stays at 1. The model thus exhibits both effective feedback and failure of stability. Crossing the stability boundary means that a nondecaying or growing mode exists. It does not imply that every trajectory, including the one with zero initial history, grows.

Stability does not guarantee that the deviation shrinks at every step, that transient peaks are acceptable, or that the price to be paid for execution is bearable. A negative x\_t indicates only that the target has been overshot. It does not represent a negative number of tasks. The equation does not model nonnegativity, demand, heterogeneous service, strategic responses, or permissions in real queues. Independent measurement and model validation are needed before applying this criterion to a real process. The criterion must not be used to set a real-world corrective gain that has not been identified.

## 6.3 CM-LEARNING Feedback-Mediated Learning

Basic inferential dependencies: CM-FEEDBACK. E4, CAUSAL, and EVIDENCE are method checks. Feedback-mediated learning requires a return to cause an update that can be retained and is actually used in a subsequent task. Transmission by a current carrier and historical increments beyond the current representation are two branches, each requiring its own evidence.

`CM-LEARNING-carried` is the carrier branch. Its enabling conditions are: effective feedback changes an internal state, parameter, rule, memory, or structure that can be located; a specified carrier retains the update or sustains continued updating, and the update is actually used in repeated rounds; and the prespecified later task shows a reviewable difference relative to a predeclared baseline or an alternative process. A causal claim requires interruption, replacement, recovery, or a comparison that meets the conditions for identification. It must address common inputs, continued training, selection, attrition, and changes in measurement. A claim about transfer or predictive gain also requires evaluation suited to that claim, using an independent task or out-of-sample data. The mechanism chain is:

Effective feedback → locatable internal update → retention or continued updating through the update carrier → actual use in later rounds → change in the outcome of the prespecified task

A single parameter change, a single success, a single write-back, or the archiving of materials is insufficient to establish this learning mechanism. The corresponding mechanism claim is not supported if the update is not retained, if it is not used later, if a common input alone explains the task outcome, or if only a proxy measure changes while the prespecified task is unaffected. Effective feedback, learning in general, transfer across contexts, and net benefit are judged separately. Established learning does not imply improvement, a unified goal, or authorization to act.

When the goal itself changes, the reasons for forming the new goal and the decision procedure should be stated. The results, failures, and costs under the old goal should be preserved before a comparison is established for the new task. A goal reconstructed later must not be used to rewrite failure on the old task as success.

Learning in general can be supported by evidence from the relevant domain, such as new tasks that can be completed repeatedly, skills that are retained and used, or traceable teaching or organizational processes. It need not first qualify as a formal mechanism instance under this framework. A report may state together that “the practice has changed,” “the subsequent net effect remains undetermined,” and “the feedback-mediated mechanism has yet to be identified,” without collapsing these judgments into a single pass or fail. 〔Studies 16.02, 25.01–25.08, 31.08〕

Learning evaluation should also distinguish task completion with support, independent transfer after support is withdrawn, and subsequent outcomes in everyday life. Necessary support can form part of an expressive or collaborative task. In that case, failure without support does not negate an earlier success. If independent transfer was the original goal, testing it requires a comparison suited to that goal with support withdrawn. Responsibilities for choosing the goal, confirming retention of the update, measuring task performance, and judging net benefit are borne separately. Continuing support must not be used to deny a functional gain, and performance with assistance must not stand in for independent skill.

`CM-LEARNING-history` is the historical-increment branch. In addition to carrier updates, repeated use, and a task comparison, it requires a G3-instance that supports an additional historical conditional increment relative to an explicitly stated current representation. When the current carrier sufficiently retains what has been learned, this increment can disappear while learning through the carrier still holds. Known current carriers must not be omitted to manufacture an increment. Branches must not be switched after results are known to rescue the original test. The two routes specify their comparisons, counterexamples, and failure conditions separately.

## 6.4 A Hypothetical Appeal Illustrating Feedback, Institutional Write-Back, and Learning

Suppose a hypothetical institution receives an appeal. This example illustrates the method of classification only. It draws on no real-world material and provides no external empirical proof.

The appeal is received and a receipt is generated, but records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, and stopping conditions all remain unchanged. This is signal arrival; it is neither effective feedback nor institutional write-back.

The appeal changes a case status field through a specified channel, but the change is not put into effect. This can be effective feedback in the sense of D2 while still falling short of complete institutional write-back in the sense of H3.

The field change is actually put into effect, and intake, field changes, execution, and duration are recorded. This is an instance of institutional write-back. The fact of write-back does not establish that the institution is justified, nor does it amount to long-term repair.

When the write-back produces an update to a rule or state that can be retained, the update enters several later rounds of similar cases, and a corresponding task comparison and causal basis are available, a candidate for feedback-mediated learning through a carrier may be proposed. If a historical conditional increment beyond the current state is additionally claimed, a G3-instance tests it independently.

Thus, neither feedback nor write-back amounts to learning. The evidence requirements increase from one of the three to the next. An institution’s having an appeal entry point or making a public response must not be taken as clearing them all at once.

## 6.5 CM-MAINTENANCE Maintenance and Preservation–Dissipation

Basic inferential dependencies: D0 and G2. E4, CAUSAL, and EVIDENCE are method checks. D1 provides the specialization of the state terms. Maintenance requires advance declaration of the current K or a functional criterion F, together with a maintenance window. A G2-instance must identify replenishment, replacement, correction, or removal as the specified channel that changes how K/F continues to hold. Maintenance, reduced-maintenance, mismatched-maintenance, and cessation-of-maintenance conditions must also be compared.

The mechanism chain is:

Wear, drift, loss, or component exit → specified maintenance channel → replenishment/replacement/correction/removal → change in how long K/F holds or in the probability of failure

`CM-MAINTENANCE-current` checks the effect of maintenance inputs on maintaining the current K/F. `CM-MAINTENANCE-carried-cumulative` uses §4.5 to check the current carrier of wear or capability, how previous inputs formed the carrier, and how the carrier is retained or replenished across windows. It also checks longitudinal and causal evidence that maintenance changes how K/F holds later. `CM-MAINTENANCE-cumulative` is the historical-increment branch. Add G3 only when a historical conditional increment beyond the current representation is claimed. The maintenance claim should be narrowed or withdrawn if the object continues to satisfy K/F in the target window without ongoing maintenance, if a change in maintenance input makes no difference to the relevant outcome, or if an independent shock explains the supposed wear. The contract also fails if the old maintenance criteria are still used after the original K has changed.

A need for maintenance is not the same as a requirement that the object persist indefinitely. The existence of a maintenance channel must not be used to directly designate a named party to take up the work, assign responsibility, or impose a duty to make sacrifices. Mismatched maintenance can prolong the period during which the old K holds while undermining the target F. An orderly stop, object conversion, or dissolution may also be outcomes permitted by the contract.

Maintenance analysis must also distinguish tasks during normal operation, migration, failure, and recovery. Interface standards specify conditions under which interoperability is possible. Actual compatibility still depends on implementation versions, business semantics, environment and load, testing, and on-site adaptation. The existence of parts is not the same as their specifications fitting, their quality being verifiable, personnel being able to reach the site, or personnel being entitled to shut the system down. Additional maintenance work can take place outside the original object and be supplied by users, households, contractors, or other institutions. The total amount of maintenance must not be estimated only from central work orders and operational outputs.

Redundancy is tested against failure conditions and must not be assessed only by counting backups. Two paths may share a power source, software state, transport channel, supplier, operator, or approval node. They can substitute for each other in normal operation and still fail together during a fault. Spare capacity, exposure to common causes, the time needed to switch, on-site skills, and the actual authority to switch should be verified separately. The communications, roads, fuel, spare parts, and personnel on which recovery depends should be included in the same window. Adding backups, reducing coupling, and retaining independent local operation are only candidates. Their net effects are judged according to the specific failure and the cost of maintenance.

## 6.6 CM-LOAD Load, Capacity, and Recovery

Basic inferential dependencies: D0 and G2. E4, CAUSAL, and EVIDENCE are method checks. Demand and capacity must be quantities of the same type or have explicit units and a conversion mapping. Demand, available capacity, replenishment, and spillover must be measured in the same window and at the same position. A G2-instance supports only the measured channel and its relevant dimensions.

The mechanism chain is:

Demand of a matching type enters the specified channel → occupies available capacity → produces an immediate gap or cross-boundary spillover → load reduction/replenishment/capacity expansion changes the gap. To explain subsequent accumulation or delayed recovery, separately check carriers of damage, capacity, or recovery and their effects across windows.

`CM-LOAD-instant` registers only demand and capacity of the same type and their immediate gap within the same window. `CM-LOAD-carried-cumulative` checks under §4.5 how historical exposure formed the current damage or capacity state, whether that state is retained or recovers across windows, and the subsequent outcomes and comparison conditions. Causal claims require grounds for identification appropriate to the claim. `CM-LOAD-cumulative` is the historical-increment branch. G3 separately tests the conditional increment beyond the current representation. Capacity that expands at the same time can absorb demand. An instantaneous gap may leave no lasting difference once it closes. Load therefore need not accumulate monotonically, and an instantaneous overload need not cause damage or collapse.

Load must be registered by position and distribution and must not be reported only as a mean. For capacity, distinguish peak and sustained levels, alternative channels, and replenishment. For recovery, distinguish recovery of visible output, of K/F, and of the internal margin. Quantities of different types must not be directly subtracted. A need for recovery must not be used to directly create a duty to make sacrifices, or an authorization, for any subject.

A recovery judgment should specify what recovered, for whom, under what support conditions, and through what point in time. Gather evidence separately for the quantity, quality, continuity, and accessibility of actual output. Obtain separate evidence for staff recuperation, spare parts, maintenance backlogs, and continuity of funding. Separately document the added burdens outside the boundary from care, commuting, borrowing money, or substitute services. Subsequent disruptions and near misses also require separate evidence. The pre-disaster population, current population, eligible people, and actual users must not be silently substituted for one another when calculating recovery rates. Confirming a particular limited function does not require all other outcomes to recover at the same time.

Temporary facilities, continuing outside support, or assistive equipment can provide real functional repair. Dependence on support does not by itself indicate failed recovery. The sustainability and expiration of support should be verified, along with whether alternative uptake is already in operation. Any demand peaks, maintenance cycles, or seasonal exposures that have yet to be experienced should also be specified. The term “false recovery” should be reserved for a claim that extends beyond what the evidence supports about the object, population, function, or conditions for continuation, and deception is not presumed on that basis. If destabilization recurs after recovery, the persistence of earlier damage, unreplenished reserves, interruption of support, new shocks, and changes in measurement definitions must be compared separately. A single new failure must not be used to erase an earlier result whose limits were accurately stated. Necessary support must not be withdrawn to validate recovery.

## 6.7 CM-PHASE Phase and Threshold Transition

Basic inferential dependencies: D0 and D1. E4 and EVIDENCE are the shared method checks for the phase pattern branch. Add CAUSAL when offering a causal explanation of a trigger, a mechanism of persistence through a carrier, or historical-increment hysteresis. This method check is not a prerequisite for basic pattern classification. A purely predictive historical increment does not thereby become causal. Basic phase identification does not require G3. Under the same candidate object contract and K, at least two reproducibly distinguishable ranges of operation may be tested for, provided that the state variables, candidate parameters or trigger conditions, threshold, and robustness checks for noise and binning are registered in advance.

The mechanism judgments have the following branches.

`CM-PHASE-pattern` is the phase pattern. It requires “the same K, two reviewable intervals, and a prespecified threshold pattern.” It registers only the relation between the intervals and the threshold and makes no causal claim about the trigger.

`CM-PHASE-causal-trigger` is causal triggering. It requires “a specified trigger channel and either an intervention on the channel or natural variation suitable for identification.” A G2-instance is also required to locate the effect of the trigger.

`CM-PHASE-carried-persistence` checks how, under the same K, the current carrier maintains a phase, sustains it across windows, or gives rise to delayed recovery. Use §4.5 to document the carrier and temporal order, make longitudinal comparisons, and obtain causal evidence appropriate to the claim. `CM-PHASE-hysteretic` is restricted to the historical-increment branch of hysteresis. A G3-instance separately tests the conditional increment beyond the current representation. An identical curve shape must not stand in for evidence under either route.

The phase conclusion should be withdrawn if a continuous trend fully explains the change within the same state distribution, if the threshold shifts arbitrarily with the binning, or if K had already ceased to hold before the transition. In these cases, record the result as continuous change, an effect of how the measurements were partitioned, or object conversion/dissolution, respectively. A reversible phase need not have a historical path. Only the historical-increment hysteresis branch adds G3. Phases do not form a mandatory S0–S6 sequence of maturation. A new phase is not thereby higher or better, nor does it thereby carry greater authorization.

## 6.8 CM-SELECTION Variation–Differential Retention–Reproduction

Basic inferential dependencies: D1. E4, CAUSAL, and EVIDENCE are method checks. This mechanism has its own operationalization. It does not follow automatically from the definition of “evolution has occurred,” and a conceptual exposition or the framework’s account cannot establish it directly.

The three outputs must be kept separate.

`CM-SELECTION-pattern` is the selection pattern. It requires “V, D, R, entry into the next round, repeated rounds, and random drift as a competing explanation.” Before results, define distinguishable variations V and their sources. In comparable environments, observe differential outcomes D that exceed the threshold. The differences must carry forward into the next round through retention R and be reviewable across repeated rounds. Test random drift, common external causes, and sampling bias at the same time. The sole basic dependency is D1.

`CM-SELECTION-carrier` is the specific mechanism. It requires “a specified channel of retention or reproduction and either a perturbation of the channel or natural variation suitable for identification.” Beyond an established pattern, a G2-instance is required to locate the carrying mechanism.

`CM-SELECTION-history` is the path across rounds. It requires “a conditional increment from historical terms across rounds and control for the current state.” Beyond an established pattern, a G3-instance is also required.

Change without D is mere alteration. A one-off difference with no R, no entry into the next round, and no repeated rounds is a one-off outcome. Do not register a selection pattern if the random-drift model is already sufficient. If retention remains unchanged after the candidate reproduction channel is perturbed, do not register that specific mechanism. One instance of persistence does not amount to differential retention. What is retained is not thereby better, more advanced, or more justified. Systemic selection is not choice by an acting subject, still less collective governance choice. A choice by an acting subject requires a subject, options, and evidence of a decision; collective governance choice requires normative premises, J-axis authorization, and O procedures.

## 6.9 Conditions for Coevolution

A candidate for coevolution may be proposed when two candidate objects change each other’s environments, feasible paths, specified channels, or retention conditions and the effects in both directions are traceable within their respective time windows. The record must separately include the two objects’ D0 contracts and K, their respective SP/T, the G2 channels in both directions, temporal order, differences in speed, and the shared environment outside the two objects. Obtain separate evidence under §4.5 for carrier effects across rounds. Add the corresponding G3-instance separately only for claims of historical conditional increments beyond the current representation.

Joint change, synchronized fluctuations, or long-term correlation must not substitute for bidirectional mechanisms. One-way dependence, common shocks, and synchronization of measurement protocols are competing explanations that must be considered. Coevolution does not imply symmetry, mutual benefit, shared values, or a duty to maintain the relationship.

Sharing a final state is not the same as sharing a mechanism. Two processes can converge on the same fixed point while having different update rules, speeds of return, transients, and responses to intervention. A simpler representation may suffice if the only question concerns the long-run terminal value. The original conclusion does not automatically extend to questions about whether the target can be reached by a deadline or about targeted operations. Analyses of recovery, coevolution, and cross-circle continuation must retain these differences as required by the question, and convergence of the curves must not substitute for identifying the mechanism.

## 6.10 Dissolution, Object Conversion, and the Limits of Repair

The criterion for dissolution is that the original K no longer holds. If the original K fails but another object contract can hold, object conversion should be distinguished from termination. A phase change is distinguished from dissolution only if the original K continues to hold. Dissolution does not automatically count as failure, and persistence does not automatically count as success.

Repair uses the target K* or F* made public before results as its criterion. It can make use of old traces, and it can change components, boundaries, relations, interfaces, carriers, and scale positions. It does not turn back time to an earlier state. A claim about a repair window requires K*/F* and a comparison of how intervening at different times affects the attainability of the target and cost. Use the carrier route to obtain evidence about current constraints formed through history. G3 is additionally required only for a claim of a historical conditional residual beyond the current representation. Even if these empirical conditions hold, whether the goal is justified, who chooses it, who is responsible, and who may implement it are still subject to review under the N layer, the J axis, and O1–O4.

## 6.11 Final Gate for Mechanism Outputs

For each of the six conditional mechanisms, the object and scale declaration, inferential dependencies, enabling conditions, mechanism chain, minimum evidence, competing explanations, counterexamples, and failure boundary must be completed in that order. If any item is missing, retain only a candidate mechanism or a question to check. Feedback must not smuggle in learning, instantaneous load must not smuggle in accumulation, and a basic phase must not smuggle in hysteresis. A selection pattern must not smuggle in a mechanism or a value judgment, nor may maintenance and repair smuggle in a duty to persist. Conceptual expositions and teaching examples only illustrate how to use the contracts. They supply no external empirical support.

## 6.12 Operation with Multiple Asynchronous Clocks

A multi-circle joint state includes at least immediate, interactive, organizational, institutional, and long-term clocks. An event can first change fast variables, then change expectations about relationships through repeated interaction, then change resources through organizational decisions, and only then potentially lead to institutional write-back. If these changes are compressed into one moment, a delay can be misread as a lack of effect, or a short-term fluctuation as long-term evolution.

Changes in fast variables do not automatically indicate long-term institutional outcomes or outcomes in everyday life. Industrial expansion, a signed agreement, the end of overt violence, the completion of facilities, or public cooperation still calls for checking execution, distribution, rights, relations, and continuity. If the material is insufficient, retain the local results that are known and stop extrapolating over longer ranges.

Each mechanism should declare its update clock and its bridges across clocks. The arrival of a feedback signal is not the same as organizational rules having already changed. An organizational decision does not amount to institutional execution. A change in institutional text does not amount to actual write-back at every local position. A bridge across clocks must specify the carrier, execution, confirmation, and failure conditions.

| Clock Transition | Minimum Evidence for the Bridge | Common Failures |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Immediate→Interaction | A difference in state persists over multiple rounds and changes the other party’s response | A one-off emotional reaction fades |
| Interaction→Organization | Changes in relations or strategies are incorporated into roles, processes, resources, or records | Verbal consensus is not put into practice |
| Organization→Institution | Verifiable changes in rules or procedures, arrangements for responsibility, and actual execution. Authorization, legal validity, and normative justification are registered separately | A document is issued without implementation, or an institutional change that actually occurred is omitted on the grounds that it was unlawful |
| Institution→Long Term | Continued execution, maintenance, learning, and alternative paths | Rebound after short-term compliance |

The table records how changes actually carry forward. Lawfulness, justification, and authorization do not serve as criteria for establishing that an event occurred. Rule changes and their execution must enter the process record if there is corresponding factual evidence, even when they violate rules, exceed authorization, or are contested, and their authorization and legal status must be recorded separately. Explaining how they formed and what consequences they had does not amount to permitting their implementation. Conversely, authorization, issuance of a rule, or an institution’s announcement of completion must not substitute for evidence of actual execution.

## 6.13 Cross-Circle Cascades and Coevolution

A cascade occurs when a local change in one circle[^p07-tn-circle] triggers changes in other circles through overlapping membership, bridging interfaces, shared resources, network propagation, or top-down institutional transmission. The object, channel, scale, and evidence must be checked again at each step. It must not be assumed that all later steps hold because the first step holds. Capacity limits, filtering, delays, offsetting effects, and local exclusion zones can cut a cascade short.

For cross-circle propagation, what propagates and what conditions the target needs in order to change must be identified first. A message can arrive through a single bridge, but an action that requires actual cooperation from several people, confirmation from different sources, or adequate support for acting safely can stall in that same network. The number of relays from one source is not the same as the number of independent confirmers, and the number of confirmers is not the same as actual collaboration. A bridge’s effect depends on how the sources are related, where the transmission arrives, the order of arrival, the duration of action, and the conditions of feasibility. It must not be determined directly from the shortest path, the number of connections, or any one model threshold.

One failure triggering the next, multiple failures jointly obstructing recovery, and a common external cause damaging multiple locations at the same time must also be distinguished. The first requires evidence of transmission at each hop. The other two require checks on recovery dependencies and exposure to common causes, respectively. Mechanisms of human adoption based on multiple sources can coexist with supply dependencies among facilities, but their state variables and thresholds must not be interchanged. Complex contagion does not guarantee that higher clustering is more beneficial. Gains from reinforcement can be offset by reduced reach across groups.

Coevolution requires at least two objects to change one another’s selection environments, transition probabilities, or constraints over a period of time while retaining their independent K. If one object is simply absorbed by another, record it as nesting or object conversion. Synchronized changes due solely to a common external environment do not qualify as coevolution. If a return signal changes no subsequent state, classify the observed relationship only as coupling or shared exposure.

Multi-circle cascades and coevolution carry no inherent direction of value. Tighter coupling can improve coordination or increase vulnerability and diffuse responsibility. Decoupling can reduce efficiency or protect local autonomy and the ability to limit losses. Evaluation and action still belong to the normative layer.

## 6.14 The Search Order for Mechanisms and Paths

For a specific question, first screen for potentially applicable mechanisms according to the object, scale, window, and requested output. Then look for paths based on observed differences in state. The state prototypes for human collectives apply only to objects that meet the corresponding conditions. Actors, technical systems, and natural processes must not be assigned the same set of states because their names are similar.

Start the search with four kinds of material: the object’s current state that can be confirmed; direct changes that can be confirmed after the event; channels, capacities, and clocks that can carry later changes; and mechanisms and external disturbances not yet distinguished. Use a state’s exit conditions to propose candidate paths and observed changes to propose candidate mechanisms. Then check the enabling conditions, stages of propagation, and minimum evidence item by item. The order serves only to locate candidates. It does not indicate that a candidate found earlier is more likely to be true.

When using a candidate, retain competing candidates and simple baselines that can explain the same phenomenon. Return signals should be distinguished from learning. Local overload should be distinguished from temporary rises in demand, declining capacity, and common external causes. Cross-circle cascades should be distinguished from synchronized exposure. Counterexamples and exclusions specified in the relevant full contract must not be omitted because the search has already found a match.

For each candidate path, state separately the conditions under which it continues to hold, the observable changes the mechanism should produce first, signals to the contrary, and the stopping point. Without evidence of enabling conditions, a candidate may still be used to arrange observations that distinguish the alternatives, but it must not be promoted to an identified mechanism. An early signal must not be just a scaled-down restatement of the outcome. When paths share evidence, citing it again does not add independent support.

The search order may be adjusted in light of missed cases, misclassifications, and the cost of use. Adjustments must preserve the original limits of applicability for the mechanisms and be compared with simpler search methods. Ease of search, a complete set of cards, or the use of the same order by several analysts does not establish that a real-world mechanism holds.

[^p07-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

[^p07-tn-circle]: Translator's note: *Circle* renders 圈层. Circles may be parallel, nested, overlapping, bridged, or competing, and may also form only temporarily around particular events; the term does not imply a hierarchy.

<a id="part_7"></a>

# Part 7 The Human Structured World

This Part does not insert human experience directly into the universal core. Instead, it adds H-World, a human adapter, as a layer over the universal structural grammar. Universal primitives describe broadly conceived empirical structures. The human adapter alone handles intent, meaning, power, institutions, authorization, responsibility, and value. The 11 human variables and U01–U11 are managed separately. Similar names or functions, or an equal number of terms, do not establish one-to-one equivalence. Judgments specific to the human domain must not be applied back to nature as general rules.

All 11 variables are interfaces with `claim_type=H` and `contract_role=human_variable_interface`. Formal references use `human_variable:HV01` through `human_variable:HV11`. GC, GS, GE, CV, RS, IS, IM, and IB are definitions of strong types with `contract_role=strong_type_definition`. Formal references use `human_strong_type:<ID>`. The interfaces and type definitions carry no empirical truth on their own. Formal G/H instance support must come from a G-instance that satisfies `root_instance_contract` or from an H1-instance, H4-instance, or H5-instance that satisfies `human_empirical_instance_contract`. Existing domain research still supports limited conclusions according to its own objects, designs, and evidence. That support must not be revoked wholesale on the ground that the research lacks instance status under this framework. H2, H3, H6, variable interfaces, and type definitions must not be presented as empirical support on the strength of complete fields or successful classification.

Applying this Part requires first completing the D0 object contract[^p08-tn-contract] and registering the scale. Any judgment in the human domain must specify the effective object, comparison conditions, time window, and observation position. It must also specify evidence coverage, affected positions, and authorization on the J axis. Technologies, crises, resources, institutional conditions, and statistical trends do not acquire intent by having impacts. Visibility, disagreement, or proximity to a disturbance does not automatically make a person, group, or dissenter a mechanism, source, or responsible party.

## 7.1 Role Boundaries for H1–H6 Human-Domain Propositions

H1–H6 are not six facts of the same type. Three are empirical hypotheses that require instance testing, two are operational or classification rules, and one is a normative boundary. How each proposition can hold depends on its role.

| Proposition | Role | Permitted Registration | Limits on Inference |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H1 Meaning and Coordination | Empirical hypothesis | Register a conditional orientation anchor if a candidate meaning arrangement specified in advance produces a difference beyond the threshold in resource, action, or coordination outcomes relative to the comparison condition | Do not infer a unified inner state, genuine consent, or a common will from slogans, shared language, or an interpreter’s projections |
| H2 Action Uptake and Classification of Responsibility | Operational rule | Record CV, bearers of costs of the same type, beneficiaries, the right to stop, and RS separately | Classification alone does not establish intertemporal reproduction, nor does it turn the ability to take up the work into an obligation to do so |
| H3 Institutional Write-Back | Classification rule | Register institutional write-back if an appeal, audit, or feedback changes records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions and actual execution is documented | Intake does not amount to write-back. A single write-back does not amount to learning, and a single update does not amount to long-term repair |
| H4 Power, Mediation, and Reflexivity | Empirical hypothesis | If position, mediation, disclosure conditions, outcome measure, comparison conditions, and threshold are all specified in advance, register channels of masking, amplification, or reflexivity that the evidence supports | Labels such as “power,” “platform,” “metric,” or “AI” must not substitute for a mechanism chain and a counterfactual. Silence does not amount to consent |
| H5 Historical Trace Carriers | Empirical hypothesis | Register only a candidate trace if the historical event, candidate carrier, baseline, trace measure, threshold, and durability window are all specified in advance and a durable difference exceeds the threshold | A trace does not amount to a path effect. Obtain separate evidence under §4.5 for the subsequent effects of the current carrier. Only a claim about an additional historical increment requires a G3-instance. Repair or protective action separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| H6 Open-Ended Undertaking | Normative boundary | Describe actual costs, voluntariness, direction, alternative explanations, structural consequences, and the right to stop | Do not diagnose whether a person “has love,” treat sacrifice as proof of love, or conscript one party into continuing to bear the burden |

The outputs of H1–H6 are limited to empirical descriptions, classification results, gaps in evidence, or normative requirements. These outputs do not themselves authorize real-world adjustments. Classification under H2 does not authorize redistribution or attribution of responsibility. Classifying write-back under H3 does not authorize changing records or rules. The normative boundary under H6 does not itself implement protection or stopping measures. Any real-world action must separately pass C12, rely on explicit N premises at runtime, obtain J authorization, and follow the O procedures.

The evidence status of H1, H4, and H5 is `requires_instance_evidence`. They follow the same structure as a G-instance for two-stage contracts, the four outcome statuses, the positive-support check, the null-conclusion check, disclosure of deviations, evidence references, and the discipline governing analysis artifacts, but their identity field is `claim_id`, and it may contain only H1, H4, or H5. An H1-instance must preselect exactly one outcome criterion among resource allocation, action choice, and coordination outcomes. An H4-instance must preselect exactly one criterion among evidence coverage, safety of expression, object behavior, and reflexive response. An H5-instance must preselect exactly one `carrier_family_id`, one nonempty atomic carrier reference consistent with that family, and one durability criterion. A list of multiple carriers or a family mismatch makes the instance ineligible. Unselected criteria must not be used to rescue a test after the results. Failure at the positive-support check does not automatically support the null model. An H5-instance establishes only a trace within the specified carrier and window. Evidence for future effects of the current carrier must be obtained separately under §4.5. A historical increment beyond the current representation requires a separate, independent G3-instance. Internal definitions, concept labels, and the framework’s own statements do not count as external empirical support.

## 7.2 Three Sets of Strong Types That Must Be Kept Distinct

Separating the types keeps the subsequent questions, evidence requirements, and limits on responsibility distinct. The types specify what materials an instance must supply. Their definitions do not assert that the corresponding facts about the world already hold. Each claim requires grounds directly relevant to it. A single record can support multiple distinct facts, but their dependence on that source must be retained and the record must not be counted repeatedly as independent validation. A single case may have several types registered at once. The establishment of one type alone must not be used to settle the others. Formal independence relations among strong types use `human_strong_type:<ID>`. Bare IDs, Chinese display names, and labels for the group must not be used in place of such references.

### 7.2.1 GC Generative Condition

GC asks “What conditions make generation possible or more likely?” Resources, technologies, institutions, relationships, trauma, crises, and windows of opportunity can all be candidate conditions, but conditions have no intent. The condition inventory first records when the conditions arose, changed, or were removed, and what the actual exposure was. A claim that a condition makes generation more likely also requires a comparison suited to that claim, a difference in outcomes, a candidate channel, and competing explanations. GC, GS, and GE are tested separately. Observing a condition neither identifies the generative subject nor establishes that a generative event has occurred.

### 7.2.2 GS Generative Subject

GS asks “Who can understand and carry out acts of initiation, translation, or handover?” The identity of the subject, capability, resources, actual actions, and authorization status must be verified separately. Technologies, crises, platforms, metrics, and resource windows must not be registered as subjects. Evidence of subject status must not be inferred backward solely from the fact that an event has occurred. GS is not automatically the same as the full set of responsible parties.

### 7.2.3 GE Generative Event

GE asks “What state transition took shape, and when?” First use the same measurement contract to record the state before generation, the time of the event, the state afterward, and the criteria used. Competing events during the same period and a bridge to the mechanism are used to explain further how the event formed. The absence of an identified mechanism must not be written up as nonoccurrence of an observed event. GE can result from action by a subject or from emergence without a subject. A generative subject must not be invented for emergence without a subject. The event must not be personified.

### 7.2.4 CV Uptake Carrier

CV asks “Who or what actually executes, transmits, maintains, records, provides care, or repairs?” Personnel, positions, procedures, facilities, and technical systems can all be carriers. Evidence comes from task logs, flows of resources and capacity, costs, rights to stop, and changes in function after carrier replacement. CV ≠ RS. Facilities and procedures perform functions. Doing so does not by itself make them morally or legally responsible parties. Whether an organization can bear institutional or legal responsibility must be judged separately according to its standing as a subject and the specific rules. Organizations must not be put in the same category as equipment.

### 7.2.5 RS Responsible Party

RS asks “Who bears what responsibility for what, and on what basis?” Responsibilities arising from conduct, decisions, authorization, oversight, and benefit, and responsibility for remedy, must be registered separately, and knowledge, control, scope, period, appeal, and remedy must be specified. Evidence for RS must be independent of who is most visible, who actually takes up the work, and who bears the consequences. An implementer with low permissions must not, by virtue of being the most visible, become the default primary responsible party.

### 7.2.6 IS Source of Destabilization

IS asks “Where does a disturbance enter or accumulate?” The record must include where the disturbance first appeared or accumulated, its point of entry, temporal order, differences in exposure, and alternative sources. The existence of a source does not amount to malice, a mechanism, or full responsibility. A dissenter’s status must not be registered directly as a source.

### 7.2.7 IM Mechanism of Destabilization

IM asks “Through what causal path does a disturbance change a state, boundary, feedback, rhythm, or orientation?” The evidence must show, at each step, the mediating links, temporal order, evidence for the bridge, conditions under which the claim can fail, and competing mechanisms. People, groups, and dissenters must not be directly labeled as mechanisms. A story about a mechanism must not substitute for causal evidence.

### 7.2.8 IB Behavior of Destabilization

IB asks “What actions, instances of inaction, or outputs were actually observed?” Original records, logs, or observations from multiple sources must retain the subject or carrier, context, scale, and time window. Observing behavior is not the same as identifying its source or mechanism, and malice or full responsibility cannot be inferred directly from it. For silence and absence, nonoccurrence, unobservability, and concealment for protection must also be distinguished.

IS, IM, and IB are not attachments exclusive to HV09 but a cross-cutting classification of destabilization. They all apply to HV02 Boundaries and Interfaces, HV06 Drive–Uptake Chain, HV07 Feedback Write-Back, HV08 Conditional Potential Field, HV09 Structural Load, and HV10 Evolutionary Phase. A case may separately register source, mechanism, and behavior at these interfaces, but independent evidence must be submitted for each. The source must not be inferred backward from visible behavior. A person or dissenter must not be labeled a mechanism. Malice and full responsibility must not be inferred directly from the location of a source.

## 7.3 A Unified Interface for the 11 Variables

All 11 variables are defined as H-World interfaces with no claim to be a universal ontology. The formal interface card retains 39 fields. For a specific application, fill in the required material according to the selected conclusion route. Mark the remaining fields as not applicable, unknown, or unavailable for reasons of protection, and give the reasons. Completing the fields does not require unlimited collection of personal information. Nor is it a prerequisite for ordinary understanding, seeking help, or accessing lawful services. The 39 fields are `id`, `qualified_id`, `name`, `proposition`, `scope`, `claim_type`, `contract_role`, `pause_condition`, `allowed_inference`, `prohibited_leap`, `inferential_requires`, `protocol_requires`, `specializes`, `applies_to`, `conditional_support_routes`, `scale_profile`, `effective_object`, `state`, `observables`, `evidence`, `input_dependencies`, `output_effects`, `carrier`, `responsible_subject`, `time_window_and_lag`, `uncertainty`, `local_exclusion_zone`, `affected_positions`, `normative_status`, `scale_invariants`, `required_scale_additions`, `changing_semantics`, `non_applicable_objects`, `forbidden_elevation`, `judgment_ceiling`, `action_ceiling`, `counterexamples`, `appeal`, and `rollback`.

The four kinds of formal dependency must be kept separate. `inferential_requires` records only formal premises indispensable at every conclusion level. `protocol_requires` records only epistemic, normative, or procedural checks that every level must pass. `specializes` records qualifying relations involving definitions, classification rules, or normative boundaries. `applies_to` records only the objects to which the interface applies. Conditions needed at only a particular level go in `conditional_support_routes`. Each route must record all of `route_id`, `claim_level`, `when`, `additional_inferential_requires`, `additional_protocol_requires`, `allowed_conclusion`, and `result_ceiling`. This separates candidate descriptions, classifications, conditional effects, mechanism explanations, and intertemporal explanations. As a result, missing prerequisites for a higher level do not wrongly block lower levels, and complete fields at a lower level must not be used to smuggle in a higher level. A G-instance, an H-instance, a human variable, or a CM conditional mechanism may enter only as an inferential premise and must not be presented as a protocol. Protocol fields may record only E, `CAUSAL`, `EVIDENCE`, `ANALOGY`, `SOURCE`, or an explicit N/O check. H2, H3, and H6 provide no empirical support. They remain in `specializes` as classification or normative qualifications. Human variables and strong types use `human_variable:*` and `human_strong_type:*`. Scale axes, operators, and universal primitives use `scale_axis:*`, `scale_operator:*`, and `universal_primitive:*`. A bare O must not appear in a dependency field. Organizational level must be written `scale_axis:O`, and operational procedures must be specified by their individual identifiers O1–O4. `input_dependencies` and `output_effects` record only interface content and variable flows. They do not substitute for formal dependencies between propositions.

See Appendix A for the complete interface cards for the 11 variables.

### 7.3.1 HV01 Structural Domain

D0 declares only a candidate object. It does not establish that a structural domain holds. A limited effective structural domain may be registered within the scope of a pre-registered G1-instance only if that instance shows that a candidate grouping achieves an out-of-sample or extrapolation gain beyond the threshold relative to a matched N0, under the preselected outcome, model class, sample or extrapolation unit, and threshold. Observable signals include differences in relations and constraints inside and outside the boundary, entry and exit records, repeated co-occurrence of common problems or institutional rules, and whether object identification remains stable after changing the grouping rules. Without a passing G1-instance, HV01 may still supply HV02–HV11 with a reference to a candidate object that carries its boundary, time window, and uncertainty, for factual description, applicability checks, and judgments in the relevant domain. It must not label the grouping as an effective structural domain with a gain supported by G1. The existence of a legally constituted organization, a traceable relationship, or a specific service recipient can have separate documentary and observational grounds. The grouping’s out-of-sample value must be compared separately. Naming does not create a community, and shared circumstances do not establish a common will.

### 7.3.2 HV02 Boundaries and Interfaces

The effective object is a boundary that selectively affects flows of resources, information, rights, or responsibilities. Membership status, admission and exit, cross-boundary flows, gatekeeping nodes, grounds for refusal, and discrepancies between formal and actual boundaries must be observed together. The basic route depends only on the object interface of HV01. It may register an inventory of boundaries, accessibility, and the risk of exclusion, with no strict G1 or G2 requirement. The selective-effect route may register the selective effect of a boundary for a specified channel, window, and position only if it also has an eligible G2-instance and passes the causal check. A subset of members, formal representatives, or highly visible positions must not be taken directly as representative of everyone affected.

### 7.3.3 HV03 Orientation Anchor

The effective object is a goal, identity, memory, commitment, fear, or common problem that may change outcomes involving resources, action, or coordination. The candidate route has no strict dependency on H1. It may first register materials on meaning, heterogeneous expressions, disputes about representativeness, and the need for further evidence. The effective anchor route requires an H1-instance with a `supported` result for its single preselected criterion among resource allocation, action choice, and coordination outcomes. An outcome family that was not selected must not be used to rescue the claim after the results. Expression alone does not constitute a structural anchor. A few affirmative expressions do not establish that the group shares the meaning. HV03 provides no authorization to impose uniformity of meaning.

Meaning acts through more than an individual’s private beliefs. Forms of address, narratives, rituals, commitments, and classifications can provide publicly recognizable forms of action. They allow people to tell whether a particular expression is a request for help, a refusal, an act of entering an agreement, an act of mourning, or a challenge to eligibility. The analysis should separately identify the context in which the parties understand the form, who has authority to interpret it in a way that carries institutional consequences, and what changes in action or resources occur through that interpretation. An interpretation can be reasonable while its causal effect remains undetermined. Coordination can occur without participants sharing ultimate beliefs.

Translating concepts from different traditions into the framework should preserve the questions they answer in their original practice, the authority to interpret them, the obligations, and the disputes, rather than only seek similar names. Religious identities, sources of norms, legal qualifications, and personal beliefs sometimes share the same translated term and can still perform different functions. Comparison may yield partial comparability, an inability to align some local features, or insufficient material. There is no need to conclude that entire traditions are closed off from one another. Assessing task-relevant loss of meaning in translation must take into account both the analyst’s task and the practical questions faced by those affected. A classification can suffice for an administrative count without necessarily explaining who can draw water, inherit, speak, or refuse.

### 7.3.4 HV04 Generative Node

A generative node must be split into three independent contracts for GC, GS, and GE. Each records the nine-axis scale of the conditions, subject, or event, respectively. The type classification route has no strict dependency on HV03. Nor does it claim causal generation on the strength of complete strong-type fields. The mechanism route may register a candidate mechanism of generation within the specified channel only with an additional eligible G2-instance. Observable signals include changes in conditions, the subject’s capability and actual actions, the state transition from before to after the event, and the bridge from local to aggregate levels in emergence without a subject. Technologies, crises, and windows of resources may serve only as candidates for GC and acquire no intent. Generative events and generative subjects must not be substituted for one another.

### 7.3.5 HV05 Action Uptake Layer

The effective object is a person, position, procedure, facility, or institutional arrangement that actually executes, transmits, maintains, records, provides care, or repairs. Basic classification has no strict dependency on HV04. Under H2, task logs, resource capacity, costs, rights to stop, and alternative arrangements must be observed separately, as must the bases for decisions, authorization, oversight, benefit, and remedy. A functional effect of the carrier requires a separate G2-instance. Intertemporal reproduction through a carrier also requires a G2-instance covering subsequent outcomes and windows. It further requires evidence of maintenance, replenishment, or replacement across rounds and of actual continuation. The route for historical-increment reproduction additionally requires a G3-instance. Historical traces in carriers follow a separate route. A durable trace within the specified carrier and window may be registered, and then submitted as a candidate historical variable to a G3-instance, only when an H5-instance returns `supported` for its one preselected specific atomic carrier reference, whose family matches the subtype, and its one preselected durability criterion. An H5-instance does not by itself establish future path effects. Facilities and procedures do not become responsible parties by virtue of executing a function. Organizational responsibility is assessed separately according to the organization’s institutional qualifications. The capability to take up work creates no unlimited obligation to do so.

Uptake of care work requires distinguishing the execution of tasks from the management work of identifying needs, scheduling, reminding, checking, and being on call. Records of these burdens are added only if the current objective involves them. Someone else completing one task can be genuine help, but that still must not be taken as grounds for asserting that all the management work has been taken over. The resources and support available to those taking over and the conditions under which they may refuse must also be included in the same evaluation. A heavy burden may result from shared scarcity; malicious conscription must not be presumed from the burden alone. The scope and duration of existing duties, responsibility for continuing the work, and the safety of those harmed should be argued separately.

### 7.3.6 HV06 Drive–Uptake Chain

The effective object is a traceable chain that converts orientation and generation into sustained action. Local segments may be registered independently first. Only the full candidate chain route requires interface records for HV03, HV04, and HV05 together, linked segment by segment in a common mapping of object, scale, window, and quantities. The effective channel route additionally requires an eligible G2-instance. Every segment must record inputs, outputs, delay, loss, points of interruption, resources, and costs. A claim that “there is drive” must not fill a gap in uptake. One pass through the whole chain does not establish intertemporal reproduction. An intertemporal historical increment must be tested separately in a G3-instance. Historical carriers may be put forward as candidates only by an H5-instance.

An effect at each segment is still insufficient to establish an effect for the chain as a whole. Assessment of the whole chain must retain correspondence within the same case, the shared scope of support, joint response, temporal order, capacity, and the endpoint outcome. Edge means obtained separately must not be multiplied directly. See §11.6 for a formal counterexample with two positive mean effects and zero effect for the chain as a whole. This restriction applies to unconditional combination. It does not invalidate a qualified total effect already supported by a design that meets the requirements.

### 7.3.7 HV07 Feedback Write-Back

The effective object is a return channel capable of changing a subsequent institutional state or transition. Basic write-back classification relies on D2 and H3 and has no strict dependency on HV05. Evidence of submission, evidence of intake, the field versions before and after, actual execution, entry into effect, and duration must be observed separately. None of the following may be called effective write-back: signal arrival alone, intake alone, a statement of position alone, or field changes without execution. The causal feedback route also requires a G2-instance. The route for learning through a carrier additionally requires an update that can be retained, repeated use, a prespecified task comparison, and corresponding causal evidence. Only historical-increment learning adds a G3-instance. A single write-back does not amount to learning.

The number of decisions changed does not define the quality of review. Upholding the original decision for stated reasons can fulfill the relevant procedural responsibility and may improve explanations, records, or subsequent handling. These changes should be registered under the actual object. If the target state does not change, they are not registered as effective write-back for that target, but the value of hearing the parties and of fair procedure is not denied on that account. An erroneous change of decision can likewise constitute write-back without constituting a correction.

### 7.3.8 HV08 Conditional Potential Field

The effective object is a set of conditions that change the probability of action, safety of expression, or distribution of evidence through testable channels. The basic inventory of conditions is constrained only by the observation position and evidence discipline. It has no strict H4 prerequisite. It may register only candidate conditions, heterogeneous positions, blind spots, and the need for further evidence. The route for position or mediation effects requires a `supported` result on the single criterion preselected for an H4-instance among evidence coverage, safety of expression, and object behavior. For the reflexive response route, E3 must also pass. The route must establish that observation, naming, scoring, or publication reaches the object through an actual channel. For a sustained reflexive response, it must also be verified that the response enters a current carrier, is retained across windows, and continues to have an effect. A sustained response must not be extrapolated from a single response. An additional historical residual is tested separately under G3. The conditional potential field is not a subject with a will. It does not “want,” “choose,” or “punish.” Nor may the labels “power” or “platform” be used in place of a mechanism.

“Potential field” here is a working name for a set of conditions. It presupposes no common unit, conserved quantity, potential function, or additive forces. These mathematical structures may be used only in a specific model that supplies variables, units, mappings, and predictions that can fail. The name itself provides no physical mechanism.

The effects of power extend beyond masking existing facts or suppressing existing wishes. Eligibility classifications, examinations and records, task training, spatial arrangements, and rewards and penalties can change which options are actually feasible, which capabilities are recognized, what behavior is treated as normal, and how actors understand themselves. The proposal of categories, adoption by institutions, allocation of resources or opportunities, responses by those concerned, and subsequent feedback must be traced step by step. The existence of a category does not establish that it has been applied. Its application does not establish that everyone accepts it.

A person can accept, use strategically, conceal, reinterpret, or reject a given status. Researchers must not infer that a person identifies with that status directly from public compliance. Nor may they prejudge classifications across the board as false or harmful. Capability, communication, legal validity, belief in legitimacy, and normative justification each require separate evidence. An institutional arrangement may provide services and a status with recourse to appeal. It may also create exclusion. The specific channels, alternative arrangements, and consequences at different positions should be compared. These are questions about candidate mechanisms for H4 and the relevant interfaces. They must not replace the conditions for the empirical support of that proposition and those interfaces.

### 7.3.9 HV09 Structural Load

The effective object is the relation of tasks and coordination demands to capacity and recovery margin within a given window. The instantaneous task–capacity route has no strict requirement for G2, CM-LOAD, HV05, or HV06. It registers margins, backlogs, local gaps, and recovery status only under the same window, position, and type mapping. Only the overload mechanism route requires both CM-LOAD and an eligible G2-instance. For cumulative damage or delayed recovery through a carrier, use §4.5 to add the current damage/capacity state, its formation process, and comparisons across windows. The route for historical increments beyond the current representation additionally requires a G3-instance. Observations must cover task volume, backlog, delays, errors, staffing resources, hidden labor, exit, and recovery curves. Local overload masked by averages must still be recorded. Load here is specified by a task–capacity contract. It does not generalize “entropy” into one additive entity shared across social, psychological, and institutional domains.

Task duration, simultaneous activities, being on call, cognitive management, sleep loss, and future opportunities differ in their units and how they overlap. Comparisons of burdens must specify what can be added and what would be double-counted. A mean must not be attributed to each bearer as that person’s actual load. Reducing a burden at one position may shift it to other households, posts, or future periods. Conclusions about the net burden should stay within the scope of the present question.

### 7.3.10 HV10 Evolutionary Phase

S0–S6 and X0 apply only to human intentional collectives that are taking shape or operating. Meaning, generation, uptake, and the formation of institutions are aspects to be checked. They need not all be mature before description can begin. Populations for which there is no evidence of intentionality, technical systems, and categories drawn solely from similarities do not directly qualify for these prototypes. The component interfaces of HV03, HV04, HV05, and HV07 are shared inputs to the checks for all routes. The dependency is on four kinds of auditable assessment record, not on the four components having already been established empirically. The status of a record may be `missing`, `not_applicable`, or `unsupported`. Accordingly, the basic route determines only applicability, inapplicability, or whether components are missing. For descriptions under the S0–S6 prototypes, directly verify the entry conditions, observations, counterexamples, and falsification materials under §8.2–8.3. No prior observation of a second range of operation is required. Check the multiple intervals and thresholds required by the mechanism only for a further claim of a phase pattern and transition that meet CM-PHASE. A causal phase transition separately requires a G2-instance. Obtain separate evidence under §4.5 for phase continuation or delayed recovery sustained by the current carrier. Hysteresis or path differences of the historical-increment kind require a separate G3-instance. Phase descriptions allow skipping phases, parallelism, mixing, moving back, splitting, merging, dormancy, absorption, and functional transfer. X0 is a conversion path, not a “lower” stage. Naming a phase does not authorize advancement, elimination, or moral ranking.

### 7.3.11 HV11 Open-Ended Undertaking Action

The effective object is a human undertaking arrangement in which action, cost, direction, voluntariness, conditions for refusal, and consequences must be reviewed separately. HV11 has no strict dependency on HV05. R0 may first register the facts of the action, costs, direction, risk of coercion, and gaps in the right to stop. The R1 description of bounded voluntary undertaking requires evidence of the action and its costs and a clear direction. It also requires separate support for voluntariness, the genuine ability to refuse, and conditions for exit or negotiation, and a check of competing explanations such as dependence, fear, interests, and acting for show. The outcome must be recorded according to what happened as realized, unrealized, harmful, or unknown. Structural success is not a prerequisite for R1. R2’s causal judgment about structural consequences additionally requires a G2-instance and corresponding causal evidence. A causal effect does not establish voluntariness retroactively, and R1 does not guarantee R2 in advance. HV11 is constrained by H6 and N4 and does not authorize any protective measure on its own. It must not be used to diagnose personality or love, or to elevate an individual undertaking to a group obligation.

Open-ended undertaking action is a limited category for reviewing particular actions and arrangements for undertaking. It does not define love, detect it, or rank personalities. Relaxed companionship, expressions of concern by people whose capabilities are limited, unsuccessful acts of creation, private mourning, and refusals that produce no new structure retain their experiential significance and value even without high costs or detectable structural consequences. Conversely, high costs, sustained contributions, and statements of good intent do not automatically establish free consent, fair distribution, or justified obligations. When materials are insufficient, labels applied from outside should be stated less strongly, without removing the parties’ standing to be heard, to be protected, and to refuse.

## 7.4 Elevation, Evidence, and Action Ceilings

When human variables are transferred across scales, SP0 and SP1 must be compared axis by axis. What is preserved, changed, and lost must be stated, together with positions with low visibility and local exclusion zones. The following elevations in inference are all prohibited. Individual experience must not directly represent the state of a group, and a local mechanism must not directly represent the aggregate. Undertaking by members must not be turned directly into a group obligation, and a carrier’s function must not be turned directly into responsibility. Behavior must not be treated directly as a source or mechanism, and a stage or a load must not directly become a moral rank.
