It does not substitute for legal validity under real-world institutions, official authority, or consent of those concerned. Matters that applicable law or the protection floor makes nonwaivable must not be overridden by the procedures above. Where no justified decision rule exists, the procedure record itself must not fabricate one either. Emergencies require separately specified temporary permissions, the narrowest scope, expiry, and subsequent review. Emergency power must not be obtained on the ground that “objections have been answered.” The existence of opposition, reasons for opposition that remain unaddressed, and failure of protection conditions should be registered separately. 〔Studies 14.06, 19.02, 21.04, 25.02〕

## 14.6 The J Axis, O1–O4, and Bounded Authorization

The J axis specifies who may decide, under what source of authority, for which object and action, and within which territory and validity period. Authorization must be atomic. One and the same valid J tuple must cover the deciding subject, target object, single action, territory, and entire validity period. One tuple covering the subject and another covering the action must not be combined to create authorization. It also must not be assumed that jurisdiction expands because the scope of observation, the scope of influence, or technical capability has expanded.

`jurisdiction_axis` fixes the source, scope, and validity period. Each option’s `territory` must equal that scope, and its `validity_interval` must fall within that period. To proceed to external action, the choice record, selected option, `selected_action`, and J tuple must match on each corresponding field. The existence of a source of authorization does not amount to legal validity, and legal validity does not amount to normative justification. O3 must register them separately.

O1–O4 are not optional attachments. O1 prevents facts, thresholds, and evidence levels from being changed after the options have been seen. O2 keeps value premises, those who bear the costs, and conflicts visible. O3 prevents omission of no action, conflicts of interest, and the limits of authorization. O4 prevents a bounded decision from expanding during execution into an indefinite, irreversible action for which no one is responsible. If any step is missing, the record can go no further than `under_review` or `paused`.

To enter `authorized`, the N premises must also be resolvable and must not consist of N1 alone. Conflicts of value and objections must be recorded. Every PF item must pass, and all affected and low-power positions must be recorded. The selected option must be a feasible `external_action`. Least harm and proportionality must be explicitly selected. A valid atomic J tuple must cover the action, and O1–O4 must cross-reference one another. Stopping, appeal, independent review, rollback, remedy, and expiration must all be capable of changing the status.

Atomic authorization specifies sufficient coverage for a particular action. It does not deny that actual institutions consist of multiple actions, each taken with the requisite authority. Intake, adjudication, preservation measures, service of process, and enforcement may be carried out in succession by different subjects under the law. Each action’s own authorization and prerequisites are checked separately, and it is then explained how the preceding result serves as valid input to the next step. Several incomplete tuples must not be combined to create authority for the same action. An entire lawful procedure with multiple steps also must not be declared invalid because no one person holds all the relevant authority.

Personal consent, private appointment, public authority, and an organization’s legally established standing to make a request can also be valid separately. When a public action is lawful without a private appointment, that does not mean that the person concerned has consented, nor does it permit expansion into other matters. The framework’s internal issuance status determines only the scope of use it is willing to certify. The legal validity of external procedures, normative disputes, and actual effects of protection must still be assessed separately on their respective evidence.

## 14.7 Status, Action Ceilings, and the Correction Loop

A choice record has only seven statuses: `draft`, `under_review`, `authorized`, `paused`, `stopped`, `rolled_back`, and `closed`. Every transition must append the previous status, time, responsible party, and grounds. It must not overwrite history.

`draft` permits organizing materials and comparing options within existing valid permissions. It grants no new authority for real-world intervention. `under_review` means that O1–O3, normative conflicts, protection, or authorization are still under review. A need for preservation measures may be raised, but actual protection, freezing of resources, collection of further evidence, or reversible trials still require applicable, independently valid authorization. Actions under existing statutory duties or emergency authority are recorded within their own scope. Review status confers no additional permissions. Only `SEL-AGT` and `SEL-GOV` may enter `authorized`, and the action ceiling covers only the specific option selected and authorized.

A record enters `paused` when a key fact, protection, authorization, procedure, or monitoring condition ceases to hold, and the part of implementation affected by that failure stops. Review or suspension status by itself does not authorize freezing resources, restricting access, collecting information, or continuing intervention. Action needed to avoid immediate harm requires a separately valid duty or authorization, a specified scope, and conditions for stopping and review. A record enters `stopped` when a stop trigger is reached, authorization is withdrawn, the validity period expires, or a prohibited action is taken; the affected actions are then terminated under the applicable stopping procedures, and safe wind-down and any remedial duties that remain independently valid are verified, without waiting for new theoretical grounds. A record enters `rolled_back` after the prescribed recovery has been completed and verifiable remedy and version write-back have been initiated. A record may enter `closed` only when outstanding harm, appeals, remedies, and responsibilities for records have been settled or formally handed off. Any later new action requires a new record.

For each action, its maximum level, prohibited actions, stop triggers, and the responsible party with authority to stop it must be registered in advance. The final action ceiling is the narrowest intersection of the descriptive ceiling, normative principles, protection floor, selected option, J tuple, O4 trial scope, reversibility, and expiry. Tightening any item tightens the overall ceiling. A failure or unknown in any item must not be offset by “high scores” elsewhere.

Appeal must be accessible, have a time limit, and be capable of changing a record or an action. It must lead to external independent review in high-impact cases or when there is a conflict of interest. Anti-retaliation mechanisms protect submission of evidence, objections, refusal, stopping, appeal, and exit. Rollback goes beyond withdrawing an order. It includes recovery to an acceptable state, correction of authoritative records, synchronization of downstream versions, remedy of real harm, and tracking of residual harm. Every authorization has an `expiry`. It lapses automatically at expiry. Continued execution must go through authorization again rather than being renewed by default.

Correction has four distinct endpoints: views are heard safely; relevant evidence is understood; an authority with jurisdiction actually changes the erroneous decision; and the recovery of resources and rights reaches the people concerned. An earlier step can have independent value, but it must not be used to close the case for a later one. A higher rate of overturned decisions is not automatically better. It should be checked whether substantiated errors can be corrected, who could not enter the process, and what actually reaches people after correction. A rollback record cannot erase harm that occurred, opportunities that were missed, or costs incurred by others. 〔Studies 21.04, 21.08, 28.04〕

## 14.8 How PF-1–PF-10 Connect to the Choice Record

PF-1–PF-10 remain a complete protection floor. Scale elevation, object aggregation, option comparison, and convenience of execution do not reduce it. A choice record must have exactly ten `protection_floor_bindings`, with each PF ID appearing once and all IDs covered. Each entry records applicability status, rationale, evidence, mechanism, responsible party, stop trigger, appeal, review, rollback, remedy, and expiry. `rights_floor` must equal the set of PF items that are currently `active`. It must not consist only of a general statement that “protection has been considered.”

Each `mechanism_ref` must resolve to a mechanism in the same record for safe submission, anti-retaliation, appeal, independent review, rollback, remedy, residual harm tracking, or version write-back. The responsible party must match the owner of the mechanism. Bare strings, references that resolve to nothing, and self-reported independence do not count as protection. Human-specific PF items may be marked inapplicable only if the enclosing object and the downstream use together establish exclusively nonhuman impacts. If the enclosing context involves humans, sentient subjects, mixed objects, or effects on rights or resources, rewriting `impact_domain` as `nonhuman_only` does not lower the protection floor.

PF-1 enters every affected position and low-power position in the options and the distribution ledger.

PF-2 provides anonymous submission, submission through a representative, or low-exposure submission, so a high-risk channel requiring a real name is not the only route.

PF-3 makes refusal and stopping genuinely effective within the process. Past consent does not amount to permanent consent.

PF-4 provides effective appeal with a time limit and the ability to change the outcome.

PF-5 protects submission of evidence, objections, refusal, stopping, appeal, and exit against retaliation.

PF-6 allows evidence to be added, disputes to be marked, source records to be corrected, and downstream versions to be synchronized.

PF-7 provides external independent review when impacts are high, conflicts of interest exist, or internal correction fails.

PF-8 requires actual capability for withdrawal, rollback, repair, and write-back to the authoritative version.

PF-9 provides genuine exit and transfer. Where exit is impossible, it provides representation and alternative uptake.

PF-10 freezes the action ceiling, prohibited actions, stop triggers, responsible party, and review time.

When the objects affected are humans, sentient subjects, mixed objects, or unknown objects, every item in PF-1–PF-10 must be `active`. Any `pending`, `failed`, or `not_applicable` item blocks entry into `authorized`. Human-specific mechanisms may be marked `not_applicable` with reasons only when the evidence limits the object and downstream use to purely nonhuman processes, but PF-10 and the J, responsibility, stopping, review, rollback, and expiry checks for real-world action always remain in force. When the use changes, the review must be reopened, and the previous inapplicability status must not be inherited.

The rule for the protection floor is no downgrading: when moving from a local to a wider scale, from the short to the long term, or from a single relation to an institution or platform, protection may only be maintained or strengthened. If a wider scale hides low-power positions, the correct response is to recover the local record, narrow the action, and strengthen protection, rather than to cancel local rights in the name of aggregate benefits.

Hence the question of how the world “ought to choose” is no longer reduced to a single unified objective function. What the framework provides is a traceable structure of choice. It first acknowledges the differences among systemic selection, decision-making by a subject, and collective governance. It then records facts, values, rights, options, consequences, distribution, authorization, and procedure separately. Finally, the narrowest action ceiling, execution that can be stopped, and actual repair keep any decision bounded, open to challenge and correction, and subject to expiry.

The protection floor provides minimum procedural requirements and limits on burdens. It does not exhaust substantive rights. For each specific matter, the relevant rights, claimants, duty bearers, applicable grounds, conflicts, and remedies must still be stated. The ten mechanisms do not automatically establish that a distribution of income, eligibility, care, or property is justified. Conversely, a right with independent grounds does not come into existence only when the framework’s forms are used.

## 14.9 Distribution Across Circles and Responsibility for Inaction

Multi-circle options must record local benefits, cross-boundary spillovers, and costs in different time windows separately. Better metrics in the primary circle must not be used to offset harm to rights in another circle. An overall average must not be used to cover people unable to exit. Positions where memberships overlap may bear costs more than once. Bridging parties may bear the risks of translation and conflict. Responsibilities and histories can still remain after a temporary circle dissolves.

No action is also an option: which rules and allocations of resources does it retain, who continues to benefit or suffer harm, which clocks keep advancing, when is evaluation reopened, and who bears responsibility for observation and protection? If there is ongoing harm, a statutory duty, irreversible lock-in, or a position from which exit is impossible, “waiting for more evidence” must not be extended indefinitely. Even without the main action, minimal protection, appeal, disclosure, or temporary uptake may be needed.

Option comparison must not be completed automatically by the prediction layer. Foresight provides conditional consequences, the normative layer discloses conflicts of value and the protection floor, and the authorization layer determines who may act. Predictive accuracy, complexity, and technical capability do not confer a qualification to rule.

## 14.10 Resources, Standing, Representation, and Non-Domination

A normative evaluation may ask about actual control of resources, equal standing within institutions, and rights over agendas and representation at the same time. An account of how these reinforce each other must explain whether resources make it possible to refuse dependence, whether standing changes rules of admission and evidence, and whether representatives can change budgets and execution. These can also be out of sync. Greater benefits can come with humiliating barriers to access. An identity can be affirmed while resources remain inaccessible. Representation by people with the same identity can increase while dissent within the group remains excluded. An improvement in any one of them must not be used to close the case for the other two.

The absence of actual interference does not necessarily mean the absence of domination. If one party retains the power to change another party’s key options arbitrarily, the other may have to appease them in advance, remain silent, or limit their own actions. When non-domination is adopted as the principle for the current evaluation, it must be stated which powers can be constrained and how, whether decisions can be challenged, and how those imposing constraints are themselves held accountable. This normative choice must not be inferred automatically from a low rate of conflict or present satisfaction. Nor does it require treating all interference by law as domination.

The scope of those affected, the scope of those subject to a governance structure, legal membership, and the scope of formal representation can fail to coincide. The first two help identify overlooked problems and needs for protection. The latter two must be determined under the specific institution and authorization. Full voting rights or jurisdiction must not be generated automatically from any of them on the strength of a single principle alone. The review checks the framework itself adopts are its contract for bounded action, not the only justified system of decision-making for every community. How ordinary disagreements are resolved, which rights must not be canceled by a majority, and when emergency procedures end should be stated publicly and be open to review.

[^p15-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

<a id="part_15"></a>

# Part 15 Intervention and Applications

This Part puts a chain of constrained conversions between “finding a problem” and “changing reality.” It first separates four distinct tasks, then assigns approved candidate actions to T0–T4, and finally turns to nine domain modules. This preserves the framework’s explanatory power while preventing its terms from crossing authorization boundaries and acquiring authority to act.

This Part sets out the theoretical meaning, protection requirements, and limits of evidence for intervention levels and application modules. Structured contracts[^p16-tn-contract] can carry registration and validation, but their IDs, names, fields, and rules must be checked separately against the versions actually in use. A file’s existence or a passing format check does not establish that the implementation matches the meaning of the text.

## 15.1 The Four Tasks Must Be Kept Separate

The conversion chain has a fixed order.
Explanation → Diagnosis → Normative Choice → Intervention.

Explanation asks how the object operates under a specified boundary, scale, time window, and K. It produces descriptive or explanatory propositions, evidential status, a null model, alternative explanations, and the action ceiling warranted by the descriptive account. It must not produce adjudications of responsibility, value priorities, or commands to act.

Diagnosis[^p16-tn-diagnosis] asks which problem most warrants attention under the current evidence, what competing explanations exist, and what evidence would overturn the judgment. It must pass through the tool workflow. For a strong judgment it must also preserve contrary conditions, a repair window, evidence requirements, appeal, anti-retaliation, supplementation of evidence, external review, and withdrawal. Diagnosis itself confers no authorization.

Normative choice asks who may choose what from which options, under what jurisdiction, validity period, and value premises. Evaluations, recommendations, and draft institutional arrangements for deliberation must explicitly register N premises and conflicts, affected positions, the price to be paid, and an option to take no action. Actual adoption requires running SEL-AGT or SEL-GOV and passing C12 and O1–O3. Legal validity, normative justification, and J-axis authorization must be verified separately. A majority, stability, persistence, efficiency, and model confidence must not substitute for them.

Intervention uses only choices and authorization that already exist. It does not create authorization. Real-world action must be linked to a unique `selected_action` and must have, at the same time, a passing 10-component C12 bridge record, explicit N premises at runtime, O1–O4, the complete protection floor, and currently valid, independently reviewed atomic J authorization. The J tuple must cover the same deciding subject, object, action, and territory, as well as the entire validity period. An authorization that is valid but unrelated must not endorse the proposed action.

If any link required for real-world action is missing, execution must stop, and the related action requirements may go no further than no action, a need for more evidence, a need for preservation, continued deliberation, or a recommendation for external referral. Any real-world action among these still requires applicable, independently valid permission. “We have explained it clearly,” “the problem is serious,” and “the objective is well-intentioned” all skip required steps if given as grounds to act.

## 15.2 What T0–T4 Have in Common

T0–T4 are types of intervention task. They do not indicate maturity, a developmental ladder, the relative merit of objects, or a status label. Each level fixes `classification_kind` as `intervention_task_type` and fixes `maturity_rank` and `mandatory_sequence` as `false`. The numbers fix only the order of reading, not a mandatory sequence: if safety conditions fail, T4 may be assessed directly without any prior T level, and an object that already has reliable evidence of safety, minimum order, and structure need not perform each level in turn for the sake of formal completeness. “Directly” here means only that a T-level route does not require prior levels. It never means that authorization is obtained directly from diagnosis. None of the four tasks of explanation, diagnosis, normative choice, and intervention may be skipped, nor may C12, J, or O1–O4. The task level must be selected again whenever the object, scale, K, time window, jurisdiction, affected positions, or action changes substantially.

No level may generate authorization on its own. Each must record entry conditions, prerequisites, permitted and prohibited actions, evidence, C12, N at runtime, O1–O4, atomic J authorization, PF-1 through PF-10, the action ceiling, stopping, appeal, rollback, failure, and rules for switching levels. Weak signals, low power, inability to exit, and anti-retaliation are not appendices but hard checks on whether action may proceed.

Every record other than `draft` must retain an `execution_state_log` that cannot be erased. State events are appended in order, each linked through the preceding event’s hash and its own hash. An external `anchor` records the event count, head hash, and current state. Each event retains the complete choice at the time and references to `selected_action`, C12, the single J authorization, and O1–O4. Stopping, rollback, completion, expiry, and withdrawal are no grounds for deleting the historical authorization chain. `rolled_back`, `completed`, `expired`, and `withdrawn` are terminal states for the record. A record in one of these states must not re-enter `active` by recomputing hashes in the same package. The only course is to create new choice, authorization, and execution records. The current execution state must also agree with the state of the complete 17-field choice record.

A hash chain can check links between stored contents and changes to their bytes. It cannot by itself establish that the whole log was never rewritten, that events actually occurred, or that the anchor is independent. The external anchor’s identity, time of acquisition, and control relationships must also be verifiable. Execution outcomes are confirmed separately with evidence from the relevant systems and the world. Recording requirements serve traceability, correction, and protection; they do not substitute cryptographic format for factual investigation.

### 15.2.1 T0 | Carriers of Safety and Trust

T0 first makes it possible for evidence, counterexamples, refusal, and requests for help to enter safely. This is neither an exercise in psychological reassurance nor a requirement that affected people stay in dialogue. T0 should come first in cases of complex trauma, closed systems of concentrated power, sustained betrayal, inability to exit, or appeals mediated by the same chain of power. The same applies when diagnosis itself could locate, label, or punish the people concerned.

Permitted actions include anonymous or encrypted submission, submission through a representative, and phased disclosure; separating identity from factual material; recording refusal, withdrawal, emergency stops, and independent review; testing receiving carriers for sustained, consistent, predictable, and noncoercive conduct; and preserving necessary evidence and connecting people to external professional, safety, or statutory support.

Forced dialogue, mediation, reconciliation, and full disclosure are prohibited. Silence, delayed expression, anonymity, or submission through a representative must not be treated as unreliable. Helping the party whose power is being questioned locate, label, or punish people affected is prohibited. Invoking love, responsibility, maturity, or the larger interests at stake to demand continued exposure is prohibited. Without a minimally safe way to receive material, only low-exposure preservation and external referral are permissible, and T0 must not be represented as complete.

T0 must verify that the channel can actually be reached, access to identities is controlled, withdrawal and stopping take effect, and external review is independent. It must also establish a baseline for adverse changes after feedback. Weak signals enter a protected area pending review. Low-power positions and dependencies are listed separately. People unable to exit receive representation, local buffers, alternative uptake, and room for future choices. Loss of power, isolation, stigma, punishment, and changes in resources after feedback trigger anti-retaliation procedures.

If an identity is leaked, retaliation follows submission, the receiving party loses independence, continued contact increases immediate risk, or authorization ceases to hold, contact and propagation must stop immediately, unnecessary access must be revoked, anonymous channels or submission through a representative must be reinstated, new harm must be remedied, and the carrier must be migrated. Appeals allow anonymity, representatives, independent third parties, and emergency stops. Once safety is established, the case may remain at T0 or move to T1/T2. If safety cannot be established, it may move directly to T4.

### 15.2.2 T1 | Reversible Minimum Order

T1 applies when harm is spreading and basic functions are breaking down while full diagnosis still takes time. Its objectives are limited to stopping identifiable harm, preserving evidence and key supports, recovering minimal communication and essential services, and opening a new observation window.

Permitted actions include safe separation, reducing coupling, preserving evidence, protecting key points of support, and establishing temporary order with a short automatic expiry. All actions must be low-risk, observable, and reversible through rollback. In-depth diagnoses of personality or motives, forced mediation, discipline, permanent assignments of responsibility, removal of eligibility, public stigmatization, and irreversible structural changes are prohibited. Temporary measures must not quietly become ordinary rules through repeated renewals.

To enter T1, the immediate harm, consequences of taking no action, key variables for protection, a reversal path for each measure, and side effects by group must be specified. Minimum order must not shift the costs of maintaining function onto positions with the fewest permissions, the fewest resources, or care responsibilities. Near misses, silence, and anomalies at the margins remain weak signals. People unable to exit must not be presumed to consent because they stay. Reporting risk, refusing a temporary measure, or requesting review must not lead to retaliation affecting schedules, resources, services, or reputation.

The measures must stop if they produce greater harm, reversibility is lost, the deadline is extended without authorization, costs rise at low-power positions, appeal is suppressed, or J authorization ceases to hold. Rollback must restore the original rights, services, resources, and records and remedy harm caused by the temporary measures. Once harm has stabilized and structural evidence is sufficient, T2 may be assessed. If safety deteriorates, the case returns to T0. If safe repair is infeasible or irreversible risks increase, it enters T4.

### 15.2.3 T2 | Repair of the Uptake–Return Flow Chain

What T2 addresses is not abstract “poor relations” or “poor execution” but breaks, already located by evidence, in cost-bearing, return flows of resources, transmission of implementation, write-back of bad news, uptake at intermediate levels, responsibility, and bounded authorization.

Before entering T2, the following must be in place: a return-flow map for costs and benefits, a chain of responsibility and authorization, carriers for uptake at intermediate levels and channels for bad news, a plan to operate alternative channels in parallel, distributional impacts, and a repair baseline. Dismantling old channels must not outpace the establishment of alternatives. Permitted actions include repairing oversight and feedback, supporting uptake at intermediate levels, replacing nodes within limits or reconfiguring them gradually, testing return flows with the smallest verifiable change, and reallocating resources, responsibilities, and authorization within an explicit scope.

Dismantling old channels before alternatives operate is prohibited. Contact, understanding, slogans, and project completion must not be treated as structural repair. Authorization for reform or dissolution must not be indefinite. Repair costs must not be concentrated on people with little power, people unable to exit, or people already exhausted. Short-term aggregate metrics and accounts from the center must not be used to establish that return flows close the loop.

Evidence must include chain nodes, direction of flow, delay, effects by group, resources that actually reach the downstream end, capacity at intermediate levels, pre-registered criteria for success and failure, and side effects. Workarounds at the downstream end, near misses, samples from the margins, and material omitted from the central summary enter as weak signals. Aggregate gains must not obscure net losses at low-power positions. People unable to exit must have participation through a representative, alternative uptake, and local buffers. Those who give feedback, object, or stop a trial are protected against retaliation.

The repair must stop if alternative channels fail to operate, benefits do not return to positions under pressure, bad news cannot change the plan, margins decline at intermediate levels and the downstream end, harm to a group exceeds a threshold, or authorization ceases to hold. Rollback includes stopping expansion, restoring old channels or running them in parallel, reversing unauthorized reallocations, correcting eligibility and records, compensating for harm, and comparing options again. T3 may be assessed only when there is a stable closed loop and long-term uptake is in place. The case enters T4 when repair is infeasible, power blocks it, or net harm grows.

### 15.2.4 T3 | Deep-Time Maintenance and Higher-Order Updating

T3 may be used only when there is already sustained evidence of safety, minimum order, and key return-flow chains. It addresses maintenance debt, breaks in succession, dependence on a single center, slow variables, institutional aging, and recovery margins. It does not declare that the system has entered an “advanced stage.”

Permitted actions include establishing deep-time early warnings, monitoring slow variables, developing successors and allowing bounded trial and error, creating uptake across multiple centers, maintaining redundancy and alternative paths, conducting periodic reviews and vulnerability exercises, and writing back counterexamples and versions. Diverting T1–T2 resources for safety, minimum order, or repair in the name of building the system is prohibited. A higher-order steady state must not be recorded as a final state, a level of maturity, or a superior status. Succession, exercises, and early warning must not be used to expand monitoring or make permanent eligibility judgments.

Evidence for entry includes data by group across cycles, maintenance debt, succession and substitution outcomes, recovery exercises, independent audits of vulnerabilities, adoption of counterexamples, and changes in versions. A narrative of long-term stability must not turn near misses, successors’ objections, or deviations in slow variables into noise. Maintenance costs are accounted for by generation, territory, role, and resource position. People unable to exit have local exemptions, representation, and alternative uptake. Reporting an institutional vulnerability must not affect eligibility for succession, resources, or reputation.

T3 must stop when T0–T2 resources are squeezed, monitoring becomes unauthorized control, succession becomes reproduction of power, costs are shifted to low-power or future positions, or counterexamples cannot change rules over a sustained period. Rollback includes suspending long-term mechanisms, minimizing monitoring data or shutting off the data flow, restoring basic resources, withdrawing obsolete rules, and starting retirement or replacement. T3 may return to any task of protection or repair or move to T4. It is not a common endpoint for all objects.

### 15.2.5 T4 | Exit, Transfer, and Preservation of Evolutionary Memory

T4 applies when staying or repairing is no longer safe, feasible, or justified. T4 may be assessed directly as a candidate without any prior T level if T0 cannot be established, anti-retaliation and appeal repeatedly fail, repair is blocked by power, the object’s anchor has effectively been lost, the net harm of staying exceeds the price of transfer, or external uptake is safer. Actual exit, transfer, or handling of memory still requires normative choice, C12, J, and O1–O4.

Permitted actions include designing safe exit, local buffers, staged transfer, and alternative uptake; preserving, with consent and to the minimum extent necessary, evidence, skills, lessons from failure, and attempts at repair; settling care, compensation, and outstanding responsibilities; protecting those who stay and those unable to exit; and verifying the new uptake system while retaining an option for a second transfer. A strategic action that carries a price may enter consideration as a candidate only if the subject acts voluntarily and with knowledge, the price can be borne, the experience is explicitly recorded, and the action does not exhaust all remaining strength.

Exit must not be romanticized as courageous, loyal, mature, or the only right course. Helping responsible parties use exit to evade compensation, care, or legal duties is prohibited. People affected must not be required to publicly prove their circumstances. Individual material must not be entered into public memory without consent. It must not be assumed that the new system is inherently safe. Others’ love and sacrifice must not be conscripted.

For people unable to exit, the goal is not to “complete an ideal exit” but to reduce harm, preserve subjecthood, recover minimal choices, and establish local buffers and a future way out. The wish to leave, hesitation, wariness about the new carrier, and anomalies among those who stay are weak signals. T4 must not become a unilateral escape for those with more resources. If exiting, refusing a transfer, preserving evidence, or appealing is followed by withdrawal of supplies, tracking, stigma, or retaliation against those who stay, this must trigger a protection review and the halting of the related steps that increase danger.

When risks of the new uptake rise, the subject withdraws, exit provokes retaliation, memory is used beyond the permitted purpose, obligations are evaded, or authorization ceases to hold, the affected steps of transfer, contact, or data handling must be suspended or redirected. Suspension does not mean requiring the subject to remain exposed to danger; necessary support continues on independently valid grounds, recoverable rights and resources are recovered, data uses outside the permitted scope are revoked, records are corrected/deleted, and harm to the subject and to those who stay is remedied. On entry to the new system, registration must start again from the new object’s T0, scale, and K, and the old authorization must not be carried over.

Exit is not the deletion of an object from a table. Ending an organization, changing jobs, separating within a family, relocating, and retiring a tool can leave responsibilities for wages, care, debts, compensation, knowledge handover, and data custody. Separate evidence must be obtained that the new arrangements actually operate, dependents receive support, those who leave are protected against retaliation, and historical responsibilities have been handled. The name of an alternative or a handover document must not substitute for this evidence. 〔Studies 16.08–16.09, 23.04, 24.06, 28.08, 29.08〕

Assessing success or failure in T0–T4 first requires fixing the function and conditions of support at issue. The ability to express oneself with continuing assistance, availability of a service with a temporary power supply, or continuation of a task by replacement staff can all be accurately specified outcomes. If the goal includes independent completion, stability over the whole period, or full recovery of living conditions, what happens after support is withdrawn, what resources are available afterward, and whether the relevant endpoints are reached are checked separately. A narrower goal already achieved must not be canceled because a broader goal has not yet been completed, and completion of the narrower goal must not be extrapolated into full recovery.

Continuity after an exit should distinguish who carries out tasks, who arranges and verifies them, who is on call, and who bears the costs. If the person previously responsible still arranges and checks tasks after handover, the reduction in execution work may be acknowledged while the coordination burden that has not been transferred is retained. Safe exit does not require affected people to supply a perfect replacement first. Where bounded responsibilities still have independent grounds, parties with authority and resources must state the scope and duration of the uptake and the gaps that remain. The need for continuity must not be rewritten as a requirement that the original party stay indefinitely.

## 15.3 Common Checks for the Application Modules

A domain name is not an object contract. Each application first registers the D0 object, nine-axis scale SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>, K, time window, sources, and affected and low-power positions, and then enters the seven-gate tool workflow. An application module may change only the questions asked, risk checks, and action ceiling. It must not substitute for domain investigation, professional judgment, legal procedures, or authorization.

All nine modules retain PF-1 through PF-10. They register affected and low-power positions, provide anonymous submission, submission through a representative, and low-exposure submission, and preserve refusal and stopping. They also require effective appeal, anti-retaliation, evidence supplementation and record correction, and external independent review. The remaining requirements are withdrawal, rollback, repair and version write-back, exit and transfer with protection through representatives when exit is impossible, and the action ceiling, prohibited actions, and stop triggers.

Each module below specifies the scope of domain evidence and how to downgrade outputs for gaps. It states what judgments the available materials can support, what facts and verification are still missing, and how those gaps limit the output. Each supported proposition has its own `claim_id`, material references in `source_refs`, and a value for `max_inference` from the closed set. A module title or list of materials must not be used to endorse a whole passage of conclusions.

“Partial coverage” is not the same as uselessness. It means only that the output ceiling must fall in line with the gaps. Every `source_gap_effects` entry must select from the closed enumerations “lower confidence, suspend the proposition, require external verification, or record only a gap in the inventory” and “no external action, require a domain protocol, narrow authorization, or suspend intervention.” It must not be `none` or `ignore`. The effects of gaps propagate along the dependencies of each proposition. A gap in one item must not be extrapolated into the invalidity of all analysis. Argument evaluations, procedural critiques, and conditional recommendations under explicit premises that already have independent grounds are each retained.

### 15.3.1 APP-REL | Relationships

The object is the interaction, shared commitments, boundaries, division of responsibility, and return flows within a particular time window, not the personality of either party. A relationship, an individual’s psychology, family institutions, and events involving violence or coercion are distinct objects. Once the object undergoes conversion, scale and K are registered again.

Major risks include romanticizing one-sided effort as love, assuming both parties want repair, exposing people again through forced dialogue, underestimating dependence and the cost of exit, and weaponizing the framework’s terms. A lightweight entry asks first about felt safety, whether responsibility runs only one way, whether effort is reciprocated, whether both parties want to work toward repair, and whether boundaries can be respected. In the presence of violence, coercion, sustained humiliation, control of resources, isolation, continuing deception, or inability to exit freely, the joint diagnosis must stop and the work must turn to safety and external support.

Weak signals include hesitation, silence, avoidance, informal requests for help, and repeated boundary crossings. Low-power positions are registered in terms of dependence on housing, resources, care, status, and reputation. People unable to exit may submit through a representative, disclose in stages, and use local buffers. Threats and changes in resources after setting a boundary, seeking help, refusing dialogue, or expressing a wish to leave enter anti-retaliation monitoring.

The tool may produce at most plain-language observations, escalation of risk, needs for external support, and candidates for T0–T4. It authorizes no mediation, disclosure, separation, or disposition of property. It does not diagnose personality or illness or substitute for psychological, medical, or legal judgment.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (partial coverage). The framework’s rules support a lightweight entry into relationship analysis, protection in informal settings, and protection for people unable to exit. Independent causal contracts for relationships, systematic cases, and evidence of intervention effects are still missing. The output may therefore include only observations, escalation of risk, and needs for support. Personality, causal responsibility, or the effectiveness of intervention must not be determined on that basis. Where violence, coercion, or consequences for property, residence, or guardianship are involved, the matter must be handed to the appropriate professional procedure, and the action ceiling must be narrowed.

### 15.3.2 APP-FAM | Families

The family module checks co-residence, recognition of kinship, affection, property, care, labor, and legal eligibility separately. It assumes no membership boundary that assigns all rights and duties at once. People who live together do not necessarily control resources jointly. Those who actually provide care do not necessarily acquire inheritance rights. Legally recognized family members can also live apart for long periods. The nuclear family can be a particular statistical category and must not be assumed to be the only real form or the normative standard. A family as a whole, a relationship between two people, a chain of care, an individual safety event, and an external institution are distinct objects. Birth, death, illness, migration, and changes in guardianship or economic dependence may require rechecking the corresponding boundaries and K.

Major risks include recording children and dependents as consenting when they cannot exit, low visibility of care work and health costs, treating control of resources and isolation as private matters, overriding individual rights in the name of the family as a whole, and conscripting love, filial duty, responsibility, and sacrifice. Children, older people, patients, people with disabilities, economically dependent people, and primary caregivers must be listed separately.

Weak signals may be expressed in behavior, indirectly, through a representative, or after a delay. Members with little power must not bear the costs of maintaining the appearance of family stability. When people cannot leave, temporary safe places, backup resources, external consultation, and the option to refuse direct dialogue are provided. Withdrawal of supplies, threats, monitoring, and stigma after seeking help trigger anti-retaliation and stopping procedures.

The module may generate only gaps in care or resources, safety boundaries, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize entering a household, contacting children or dependents, changing property or care arrangements, or making decisions about guardianship. It does not substitute for procedures for child protection, medical care, guardianship, or domestic violence.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (partial coverage). The framework’s rules can identify shared living arrangements, the privatization of costs at major life transitions, and protection needs where exit is impossible, but the evidence and protocols for subdomains such as children, older people, people with disabilities, and economically dependent people are incomplete. If materials are insufficient, only risks and facts to verify are recorded, and presuming consent, assigning care responsibilities, or proposing guardianship or disposition of resources is prohibited. Child protection, medical, guardianship, or domestic violence procedures must provide further verification.

### 15.3.3 APP-ORG | Organizations and Projects

The object is the chain of objectives, resources, positions, execution, feedback, and responsibility in a specified organization or project. The organization, project, department, position, and individual must be registered separately. When the object of analysis changes among individual performance, the function of a position, a process, a project, and the organization’s institutional arrangements, the K mapping must be rebuilt.

Key risks include metrics displacing objectives, distortion in summaries at intermediate levels, pushing responsibility downward, invisibility of maintenance and care work, retaliation through performance evaluation against people who give feedback, outsourcing long-term costs through temporary projects, and using framework labels in personnel decisions. Beyond basic diagnosis, midsize organizations must add the chains of return flow and transmission, implementation incentives, carriers for uptake at intermediate levels, frequent small return flows, and low-disruption interfaces for disputes.

Central summaries must not silence frontline workarounds, near misses, maintenance debt, informal complaints, or signs that people may leave. Positions weak in contract terms, rank, resources, evaluation, or ability to exit are listed separately. Where exit is blocked by the industry, income, visas, career history, or care responsibilities, representation and external review are provided. Changes in performance ratings, schedules, promotion, contracts, or resources after dissent enter anti-retaliation monitoring.

The module may identify risks, breaks in uptake or return flow, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize scoring, appointment, discipline, layoffs, contract changes, or financial changes. Once diagnosis is used in a personnel decision, the corresponding action must be put on hold, people who provided feedback must be protected, and independent appeal and rollback must be available.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (partial coverage). The framework’s rules support checks on translation across organizational roles and levels, return flows, uptake at intermediate levels, responsibility chains, and concentrations of power. Evidence about the specific industry, position, labor relations, and project cycle still needs external supplementation. If on-site records, data by group, or independent review are missing, findings of fact, causal attributions of responsibility, and eligibility for real-world adoption that depend on them cannot be established. One may propose audit questions, recommendations for reversible trials, and assessments of texts, procedural critiques, and conditional plans that have independent grounds, but these must not be used as a basis for scoring, appointment, discipline, layoffs, or changes to contracts.

### 15.3.4 APP-PLT | Platforms

The platform object is not a published rule but the chain of written rules, parameter rules, interface rules, models, distribution, behavioral responses, and data write-back at a frozen version. The platform, model, product interface, content network, user groups, and external social impacts are registered separately. K must be revalidated when the objective function, parameters, entry point, or model version changes.

Risks include recommendation loops that train on the world they shape, popularity bias, declining diversity, capture by proxy objectives, dual ledgers across text, parameters, and interfaces, automation bias, gates on visibility and eligibility, and control of appeals by the same chain of power. An audit checks the objective function, parameters, default entry points, human review, behavioral feedback, and effects by group that the current claim requires. Claims about internal mechanisms require the corresponding version and implementation material. Assessing a limited total effect supported by a comparison that meets the requirements does not require access to all internal parameters. Training write-back is checked only when model retraining is actually involved, and fixed distribution rules can also form feedback.

Material from low-traffic positions, minority groups, people wrongly excluded, and people who left must not be downweighted by count. Platform workers, small creators, people without an alternative platform, and people subject to automated decisions are listed separately. Where platform lock-in exists, data migration, appeal through a representative, and human review are provided. Throttling, bans, or changes in income after appealing, refusing a data use, or reporting an error are signals for anti-retaliation checks.

The module produces only audit questions about parameters or interfaces, effects by group, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize data access, post deletion, throttling, account bans, changes to models or parameters, or decisions on eligibility for work. When the object version changes, harm to a group exceeds a threshold, human review is inaccessible, or rollback capability is lost, expansion must stop and the version must be rolled back.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (substantial coverage). The rules specify where to check networks, diffusion, conditional potential fields, parameters, recommendation loops, and automated governance. Specific platform mechanisms and effects still require independent evidence.

Research on recommendations should distinguish behavioral records, preference representations within the system, independently measured preferences, and actual consequences. Drift in representations during simulation cannot by itself establish changes in people’s internal preferences or real-world harm to groups (see Mansoury et al., 2020). Changes supported by other independent materials should still be retained. The existence of feedback, amplification of bias, and lock-in that is difficult to correct also require separate evidence.

An assertion about a parameter or internal mechanism is suspended if evidence on the relevant version, parameters, or implementation is insufficient. However, randomized or quasi-experimental studies with clearly defined interventions and valid comparisons can support limited effects within their samples, treatments, outcomes, and time windows, even if researchers cannot obtain all internal parameters. Effects of the entire configuration must not be recorded as independent contributions of its parameters or extrapolated to other versions. Without evidence about effects by group and effective review, fairness, safety, or eligibility for broader action is not certified.

### 15.3.5 APP-INS | Institutions and Public Governance

The object is the structure of institutional rules, execution, resources, eligibility, feedback, responsibility, and public commitments within a frozen jurisdiction and time window. Legal texts, policies, implementing institutions, budgets, informal rules, public objects, and affected groups are registered separately. A state, institution, organization, and policy are distinct objects.

Major risks include shared sources for key channels, closure around concentrated power, filtering of bad news, backlash after appeals, pushing responsibility downward, oversight captured by beneficiaries of a rule, paper compliance standing in for reality, public commitments extracting sacrifice, majorities and stability overriding rights, and romanticizing multiple centers or autonomy. Legal validity and normative justification must be kept separate. Public benefits must be broken down by group, region, generation, and ability to exit.

Aggregate metrics must not silence downstream positions, minorities, regions at the margins, informal practices, and absence signals. Positions weak in rights, resources, eligibility, information, or ability to exit are listed separately. People unable to exit a public institution or essential service need representation, alternative services, and external review. Administrative, occupational, service, eligibility, and reputational changes after reporting wrongdoing, mounting a defense, or appealing enter anti-retaliation audits.

The module may produce institutional risks, distributional impacts, gaps in procedure, stress tests, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize legislation, law enforcement, budgets, eligibility decisions, regulation, or disposition of public resources. If appeals and review are controlled by the same chain of power, rights floors are breached, temporary measures become permanent, or the framework is used as a veneer of legitimacy, the related action must stop and be rolled back.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (substantial coverage). The rules cover checks on concentrations of power, production and distribution, public commitments, multiple centers, bureaucracy, and misuse. Specific institutions still require case registration, comparison across jurisdictions, and causal evaluation. If evidence about applicable law, implementation, budgets, or affected groups is missing, each legal finding, certification of effects, or real-world adoption that depends on the missing material must be stopped item by item. The framework must not be used to prove a political position or to issue public dispositions. Independently grounded critiques of texts, institutional evaluations under explicit normative premises, and complete drafts of charters and plans for review can still stand, but each must state, item by item, its conditions of applicability, the price to be paid, and the matters still awaiting review.

### 15.3.6 APP-OPI | Public Opinion

The object is a process of public interpretation with the event, issue, groups, propagation network, platform conditions, and time window frozen. Facts, evidence, recognition, interpretation, responsibility, identity, and action must be kept at separate levels. Popularity is not the public, nor is it representativeness.

Major risks include substituting popularity for facts and agreement, creating false agreement by deleting comments or throttling, labeling critics as mechanisms of destabilization, substituting apologies for compensation, platform rewards for conflict, simple diffusion producing wide attention with little uptake, and forcing people affected to prove their circumstances publicly. The purpose of responding is to reduce factual confusion, obscured responsibility, loss of trust, and gaps in repair. It does not aim to suppress attention.

Low-attention material, expression from the margins, deleted material, silence, and absence enter as weak signals. Positions without agenda-setting or public relations resources and those bearing identity exposure are listed separately. People unable to exit public exposure receive representation for speaking, reduced exposure, and correction of records. Online abuse, throttling, and changes in employment or resources after speaking up enter anti-retaliation monitoring.

The module may generate only levels of clarification, gaps in evidence, questions about repair, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize deleting comments, throttling, controlling comments, publishing individual material, or classifying the public as a whole. When a factual version changes, the response increases exposure, a platform action exceeds authority, or corrections cannot be written back into the propagation chain, the response must stop and a traceable correction must be published.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (partial coverage). Material eligible to enter the candidate content set, actual presentation, reading or contact, private judgments, public expression, and verifiable actions should be recorded separately. Deletion and editing, media choice, shared events, and existing relations can all change the visible distribution. A change in exposure with no detected change in attitudes does not cancel the effect on exposure or establish permanent ineffectiveness. Popularity and participation counts use denominators for platform behavior. Public opinion requires its own population and sampling. Public justification also requires independent normative and institutional grounds. If materials are insufficient, conclusions about representativeness, mechanisms, or effects that depend on the gap are suspended. Text evaluations, local facts, and conditional recommendations that already have grounds may be retained, and traceable correction is provided.

### 15.3.7 APP-SPC | Space and Channels

The object is a network of space and channels with coordinates, functional meanings, population/users, time window, and layers of dependency frozen. Physical locations, functional networks, administrative boundaries, service areas, and infrastructure layers are registered separately. A connection on a map does not amount to adequate capacity, substitutability, or actual access.

Risks include concentration of key nodes, overload at the center and disconnection at the margins, infrastructure failures with common causes, inadequate capacity and alternative routes, and changes to space shifting exposure and relocation costs. The continuous path from actual points of departure to use of a service must be verified: road connections, times of day, fares, eligibility, physical and informational accessibility, and conditions for remaining at or using the site. Counts of facilities or geometric distance must not substitute for this. Original residents before the change, current residents, those who moved out, new arrivals, and those waiting to enter are tracked separately. A rise in an area average may result from population replacement and must not be retroactively counted as gains for original households. Plans, conditions on site, and lived experience are checked against one another. For local participation, it must also be checked who is absent and who can change the decision.

Breaks at the margins, maintenance workarounds, local congestion, and near misses are weak signals. Positions with limited mobility, health, income, digital access, or ability to speak are listed separately. People unable to relocate or take a different route receive minimum services, alternative channels, and local safe places. Reporting infrastructure risks and refusing unsafe passage are protected against retaliation.

The module produces only requirements for maps, questions about protecting key channels, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize road closures, population relocation, land requisition, allocation of public resources, or engineering changes. If on-site conditions conflict with the map, alternative routes are not operating, or the changes increase harm at the margins, the changes must stop, and channels and services must be recovered.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (substantial coverage). The rules cover the spatial expression of potential fields, bottlenecks, centers and margins, layers of infrastructure, and the geography of recovery. Engineering safety, capacity thresholds, and on-site usability still require appropriate professional evidence. If a key parameter is unknown, any judgment about channel safety or engineering feasibility that depends on it is suspended, and the site investigation and measurements needed are specified. This does not cancel other distributional facts, disputes over rights, or procedural evaluations that already have independent grounds. A map must not be used to issue permissions for closure, relocation, or resource allocation, and on-site measurements alone do not determine the justified objectives of a change.

For urban development, housing, or reconstruction after a disaster, protected links should be established among pre-disaster addresses, current locations, and destinations, and the following should be recorded separately: building safety and renewed exposure, services households actually receive, the same residents’ relationship networks, local memory, and whether there is adequate information, feasible choice, and access to appeal and remedy. A well-kept streetscape, open facilities, signs of mail delivery, occupied plots, or an area average must not be taken directly as a substitute for the return of original residents, affordable housing, recovery of relationships, or authorization to represent others.

Comparisons across locations must group original residents, people who moved out, newcomers, renters, owners, caregivers, people waiting, and people unable to move, and must record costs, times, capacity, alternative routes, risks at the destination, and who can change the plan. Connections on a map do not authorize relocation, land acquisition, closures, or resource allocation. If key on-site parameters are missing, only requirements for site investigation and further evidence may be formed, and conclusions about channel safety or engineering feasibility must not be generated.

### 15.3.8 APP-EMG | Emergencies and High-Pressure Situations

The object is a system under high pressure within a specified incident phase, with defined hazards, critical functions, the command chain, information chain, and resource chain, affected areas, and a recovery window. The hazard, incident system, response organization, critical functions, and residents’ living conditions are registered separately. Rescue is judged by whether time-sensitive protection of life and against serious harm reaches people. Stabilization is judged by the spread of harm and minimum services. Repair is judged by whether specified functions are usable under explicit conditions. Reconstruction separately checks longer-term facilities, livelihoods, and renewed exposure. Tasks can run in parallel or recur, but they must not share a single success criterion, deadline, or authorization.

In the recovery phase, rescue, stabilization, functional repair, reconstruction, and long-term recovery of living conditions must be registered separately. Building completion, occupancy of an area, and facility operation are distinct endpoints from service availability, housing burden, relationship networks, local memory, and residents’ decision-making authority. “Partial recovery” describes only an observable combination in which some facilities or housing have improved while services, affordability, social connections, or rights are still lacking. It must not be described as a feedback trap without evidence across periods and consideration of competing explanations.

Time pressure in an emergency does not cancel protection of people affected, nor does it give the model on-site authority to act. External statutory duties and professional procedure cards determine life-saving, evacuation, dispatch, and engineering actions. The framework only records phases, delays, people unable to evacuate, and gaps in care and appeal. When action exceeds authority, exceptional powers expire, or on-site information overturns assumptions, the framework stops expansion and returns to ordinary procedures.

Risks include cascades from complex interactions and tight coupling, alert fatigue, accumulation of workarounds and near misses, drift into failure under pressure for efficiency, multiple chains of command, indefinite extension of emergency powers, shifting the costs of narrowing functions, and breaks in the chain of recovery after rescue. The framework recognizes professional emergency authority first. Structural analysis must not delay life-saving action.

Near misses, on-site dissent, low-level alerts, and interruptions of supply at the margins must not be ignored because disaster has yet to occur. People who cannot decide whether to evacuate, people with impaired mobility, people with limited information, and frontline positions with low permissions are registered first. People unable to evacuate receive minimum supplies, alternative uptake, and representation. Reporting danger, refusing unsafe orders, and appealing afterward are protected against retaliation.

The module can support recording risks, narrowing functions, and continuity of recovery. It does not substitute for medical, firefighting, engineering, or on-site command procedures, or confer authority to evacuate, dispatch, or take action. Temporary centralized coordination can align objectives, information, and resource interfaces, while on-site discretion still requires an explicit scope of delegation, professional boundaries, and feedback on anomalies. The handover of command, winding down of coordinating bodies, termination of each statutory exceptional power, continuity of essential services, and long-term recovery of living conditions are checked separately. Completion of an earlier item does not automatically establish completion of a later one, and unfinished recovery does not automatically extend all exceptional powers. When professional information from the site overturns the framework’s assumptions, the framework should step back to recording and after-action review. Expiry, actions beyond authority, and the return to ordinary procedures must be handled individually under the corresponding powers and tasks.

Recovery can reach different endpoints in phases. If a temporary teaching site, transportation, teachers, and the term budget are all in place, education for this term can genuinely be recovered. That the original school building has not yet been rebuilt does not make this outcome false. But if no one is in place to take over the site rent and care work next month, opening classes today does not establish stability for the entire term. A later shock also does not automatically negate an earlier limited outcome. “False recovery” first describes a claim exceeding its evidence. It does not presume a motive to deceive. 〔Studies 31.10–31.11, teaching scenario〕

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (substantial coverage). The rules cover normal accidents, near misses, drift into failure, narrowing of functions, command, and chains of recovery. Specific disaster types, danger thresholds, statutory command, and professional operating cards must come from the relevant domain systems. Without on-site confirmation, a responsible professional, or valid emergency authorization, the framework only records information and flags risks, and it must not delay life-saving action, substitute for command, or generate operational orders.

### 15.3.9 APP-ULS | Ultra-Large Scale

The object is a nested system with boundaries, levels, jurisdiction, time window, key subsystems, resources/transmission/return flows, and external responses frozen. The whole, regions, groups, organizational levels, infrastructure, and external systems are registered separately. Each move from local to aggregate, short-term to deep-time, or organizational to institutional requires reassessing object conversion, the nine-axis partial order, and K. An increase in scope of influence on the I axis does not expand authority to act on the J axis.

Major risks include using structural language to overestimate actual execution, insufficient resources and competing objectives, distortion of downstream transmission and feedback, obstruction by vested interests, reflexivity of metrics, fast risks crowding out slow variables, local failures spreading across levels, capture of governance, and large-scale irreversible side effects. A complete plan does not amount to resources being in place. Intentions at the center do not amount to action at the downstream end. Short-term stability does not amount to healthy slow variables.

Large-scale spatial and institutional options must also check whether aggregation mixes parcels, households, areas, and destinations into one denominator. Aggregate improvement can coincide with rising burdens on original residents, renters, people who moved out, or regions at the margins. Relationships, memory, service eligibility, and actual choices must not be backfilled with counts of new construction. For positions unable to exit or take another route, representation, minimum services, alternative uptake, and local options that can be rolled back are retained.

Anomalous regions, minority groups, samples from the downstream end, nontextual experience, and absence signals must be brought back into the record through independent sampling. Impacts are disaggregated by region, group, level, generation, and ability to exit. People unable to exit an institution, territory, or basic system receive representation, local exemptions, and minimum services. Changes in budgets, eligibility, employment, or services after submitting bad news enter anti-retaliation monitoring.

The module may produce only areas where judgment remains open, stress tests, trials at different levels, independent review, and candidates for T0–T4. It does not authorize a blanket prescription for the whole system or governance across domains. If harm spreads across groups, resources and implementation capacity fall below the premises, metrics systematically conflict with field conditions, local appeals cannot be written back, or rollback capability is insufficient, expansion must stop and withdrawal must proceed level by level.

Scope of domain evidence and downgrading for gaps (substantial coverage). The rules cover blind spots, applicability switches, stress tests, and intervention priorities. Unified data, case registration, causal identification, representativeness, and methods of governance across domains still need to be established for the particular object. If stratified samples, evidence of resource capacity, the execution chain, or evidence of rollback is missing, the analysis must not extrapolate from the local to the aggregate, infer execution from intent, or propose a blanket option, and the output is downgraded to areas where judgment remains open, a plan to obtain more evidence, and proposals for small-scale reversible trials.

## 15.4 Minimum Output for an Application Record

Each application must retain at least the module ID, object, scale, K, and time window. It must record every supported proposition and its external evidence, coverage status and effects of gaps, material and tool inputs, domain risks, and affected, low-power, weak-signal, and unable-to-exit positions. It must include the protection floor, diagnosis and action ceiling, and how evidence gaps lower confidence and the diagnostic level. Where a choice is needed, it must retain the normative choice or a comparison with no action; where intervention is needed, the candidate T level; and for inapplicable items, the reasons. If the application proceeds to real-world action, it must attach the complete 17-field choice record wrapper, structured `selected_action`, C12, explicit N premises, O1–O4, the single atomic J authorization, stopping, anonymous appeals or appeals through a representative, anti-retaliation monitoring, rollback, and the execution state log.

In all module records, including those used only for diagnosis, references to evidence, positions, channels, and responsible parties must resolve to the runtime registry. When context is omitted, summary placeholders such as `GHOST`, `none`, `self`, or `self:*` must not substitute for external records. The parties responsible for appeal, independent review, and rollback must be distinct from the deciding subject.

A module output missing these fields may be treated only as a reading aid and must not proceed to real-world action. An application proposed for real-world intervention must retain a bounded chain of action that can be changed by counterexamples, input from safe positions, and real-world consequences. A task limited to explanation or diagnosis ends at its corresponding evidential boundary.

## 15.5 Starting Dynamic Analysis in the Application Modules

When dynamic analysis is needed, all nine application modules must additionally declare the main actor, primary circle, neighboring circles, relation types, M/Ψ conditions, five kinds of clock, event, simple baseline, and outcome write-back. The relationship and family modules focus on role activation, inability to exit, and slow, intermediate, and fast states. The organization and platform modules focus on metrics, bridging, propagation, and institutional write-back. The public governance, public opinion, and ultra-large-scale modules focus on representation, positions with low visibility, losses in aggregation across levels, and calibration drift. The space and emergency modules focus on material channels, capacity, thresholds, delay, and emergency authorization.

No module may bypass an event’s factual status because “the situation is urgent,” or bypass privacy and protection of people affected because “the model is only making a suggestion.” An emergency permits only minimum protective action under external emergency rules, and review, appeal, and rollback must be reinstated as soon as possible. Scenarios generated by the model still retain `hypothetical` or `simulated` status and must not be written back as `observed`.

If an application only needs explanation, multi-circle foresight is not mandatory. If a simple baseline is already sufficient, a simpler module record is retained. If foresight lacks registration or calibration, the output stops at path prompts. If authorization is incomplete, real-world execution stops, and requirements for action, analytical recommendations that confer no execution permission, and drafts of institutions and plans are retained. The existence of a complex workflow must not create pressure to take real-world action.

[^p16-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

[^p16-tn-diagnosis]: Translator's note: Diagnosis here is an assessment of an object against stated criteria within this framework.

<a id="part_16"></a>

# Part 16 Governance

Using the framework to explain other objects does not exempt the framework itself from structural problems. Documentation can grow unwieldy, concepts can harden, tools can be captured by power, and users can come to depend for their identity on language they have invested in. The governance layer is therefore not a set of decorative principles. It is a set of practical interfaces through which concepts can be downgraded, assertions suspended, publication blocked, versions rolled back, and the framework replaced.

This Part governs four distinct objects: the current text and templates, the relations among concepts, the actual practices of users, and the community of maintainers, critics, people who provide counterexamples, and people affected. The four must not be collapsed into a single “framework”: a self-consistent document does not amount to the absence of misuse in practice, and an active community does not amount to validation of the empirical question families.

The framework cannot use its own language to establish that it is safe. Internal consistency, passing stress tests, passing validation, and complete reports establish only that certain outputs of a process are complete. They cannot establish real-world safety, effective repair, or consent from affected people, still less confer authorization to act in reality.

Tool implementations should turn these boundaries into verifiable governance constraints: internal statuses must not establish by their own declaration what holds in reality; governance records do not by themselves generate authorization for real-world action; terminal records do not erase history; and formal adoption must rest on commensurate grounds in external review or approval. Merely substituting aliases, self-reporting independence, or citing records circularly cannot satisfy these requirements. Whether the requirements have been implemented in a particular piece of software and actually run must be verified separately; the requirements in the text do not in themselves constitute evidence of implementation.

This Part’s status and permission rules apply to the framework’s own text, modules, formal assertions, governance changes, and related high-impact uses. The rules do not claim to replace legal procedures in different jurisdictions. Nor do they directly judge an external institution to be nonexistent or invalid because it has not yet adopted these record formats. The legal validity, normative justification, and actual protection of an external institution, and whether the framework is willing to endorse its use, are still judged separately.

## 16.1 GOV-01 Framework Self-Diagnosis[^p17-tn-diagnosis] and Separation of Objects

The first step of self-diagnosis is not to give the whole framework a blanket judgment of “healthy” but to specify what is being examined.

For the document, check whether the text, table of contents, definitions, templates, and rules contradict one another.

For the concepts, check whether the empirical question families, universal variables, human adapter, normative choice, or procedures for action exceed their scope.

For practice, check how users actually diagnose, manage, teach, make public statements, and use AI.

For the community, check who controls naming, merging, interpretation, and publication, and who bears the consequences of misuse.
