Each self-diagnosis must record at least the object type, version, rights holders, route for submitting counterexamples, harm observed, falsification conditions, and judgment ceiling. If dependence on jargon, cognitive lock-in, an expert monopoly, accommodation of commercial interests, or a veneer of AI compliance has already caused harm, the affected module may be restricted, suspended, or assessed for replacement.

## 16.2 GOV-02 Consensus Procedure and Version Permissions

“Everyone agreed in the end” is not effective consensus. For consensus to be effective, differing views must be able to enter safely, be recorded and answered, and materially change the text and practice. The procedure has at least seven steps.

Register the issue. Introductions of concepts, major rewrites, adjustments to empirical question families, misuse cases, and counterexamples must first become traceable issues.

Disclose the material. List cases, counterexamples, user feedback, external research, failures in practice, and ethical risks.

Identify what is affected. Specify the users, people being diagnosed, low-power subjects, and settings that the change would affect.

Provide access for dissent. Users with little power, people being diagnosed, nonspecialist readers, and external critics must all be able to submit objections.

Distinguish decision levels. Minor revisions, changes to core concepts, and changes to empirical question families do not share the same permissions.

Preserve minority views. Objections that are not accepted must not be deleted. They enter the record of retained counterexamples and unresolved questions.

Write back to the version record. State what was introduced, removed, or downgraded, the reasons, and what evidence would lead to withdrawal of the change.

If the institutional arrangements rule out the possibility that dissent could trigger a necessary correction, the procedure may be recorded only as `nominal_only`. It must not be used to reinstate a suspended module. Selection of participants, terms of office, time limits, and statutory authority must be pre-registered separately for the particular organization, jurisdiction, or community. Uniform numbers of any kind must not be copied directly without regard to the organization, jurisdiction, risk, and resources.

Each objection must have a `disposition`, grounds for how it was handled, evidence used, and its effects on the text, procedure, or action ceiling. An accepted objection’s effect must link to the actual `change_id`. Objections that are declined for stated reasons must remain on record. `applied` does not require token acceptance of at least one objection. It requires that reasons capable of changing a key judgment, protection, or permission all be addressed in a way suited to them. The affected adoption must be suspended if any such objection remains unresolved, required independent review is missing, or any requirement of the rights floor or any nonwaivable requirement of applicable law is unmet. Whether an objection is decisive is determined under criteria published in advance, open to appeal, and independently reviewable. That determination must not be self-certified unilaterally by the proposer or by a majority.

`applied` indicates only that the governance change meets the framework’s conditions for internal adoption. It does not establish that the change works in reality or confer permission for external action. Upholding a correct decision for stated reasons can constitute an adequate review. If correction is needed and does not actually occur, a record of responses must not be substituted for completion. The value of participation in general, causal feedback, actual change to the text, eligibility for internal adoption, and real-world protection are judged separately. Emergency duties still operate under their independent authority, scope, validity period, and review conditions.

## 16.3 GOV-03 Correcting Empirical Assertions and Choosing Which Question Families to Keep

Correction starts by distinguishing three objects. A proposition about a specific instance can be overturned by counterevidence suited to it. A generalization across instances or a claim about coverage can be limited by counterexamples within its scope. G1–G4 as a classification of questions have no independent empirical truth value. Whether they are kept, are restricted, or exit should be decided by whether they omit important questions, whether they can reduce confusion, the burden of using them, and their net benefit relative to a domain baseline or a simpler baseline. Positive results in several instances do not establish that a question family exhausts reality or that the classification is optimal.

Question families and their application modules may use the following five management statuses. These statuses govern use and maintenance and do not replace the evidential status of any specific proposition.

`normal_use`. Continue use for stated purposes. Each instance claim is still judged on its own evidence. Frequent use does not establish that the classification adds value.

`boundary_restricted`. Restrict use where the classification misses important distinctions or adds burdens for particular objects, tasks, scales, or institutional settings.

`suspended`. Suspend affected uses and retain existing independent facts when the classification or module continues to produce identifiable misjudgments or high-risk misuse.

`replacement_competition`. Compare domain methods, simpler methods, and alternative question classifications on the same tasks and materials with resources suited to the comparison. Assess judgment quality, omissions, unwarranted abstention, and total burden separately.

`retired_from_core`. The question classification no longer constrains the shared core. Material that remains useful may be retained as a domain tool or a historical record; known results from its specific instances are preserved separately according to their evidence.

Public assertions have the progression `internal_draft → open_assertion → publishable`, but at any stage they may move to `downgraded`, `suspended`, `publication_blocked`, `withdrawn`, or `replaced`. The framework as a whole likewise can operate only among `active`, `boundary_restricted`, `suspended`, `replacement_competition`, `replaced`, and `retired`.

`retired_from_core`, `withdrawn`, `replaced`, and `retired` are terminal states. They have no outgoing transition to `active` or another in-service state, and rollback must not be used to restore old authorization. A return to service under a successor method requires a new identifiable identity, fresh external authorization, and preservation of the history of the old terminal state.

Repeated counterexamples, consistent misjudgment of a kind of subject, ever-expanding scope of explanation, and evasion of refutation by saying only “the other party is not a system,” “there has not been enough time,” or “the evidence is not deep enough” all trigger a suspension review. Counterexamples must be handled in a way suited to their role in the argument: a valid local counterexample changes the corresponding boundary; repeated systemic failure triggers comparison with alternatives; misuse causing serious harm suspends the affected use; and if review finds that a counterexample does not refute the original claim, the reasons and the conditions under which those reasons would cease to hold should be preserved. A correct claim must not be forced into revision to show responsiveness, and counterexamples must not be placed outside the theory wholesale.

The consequences of a counterexample should match the claim it actually bears on. First check the object, measurement, auxiliary conditions, mechanism, and scope of extrapolation. Then decide whether to make a local correction or replace the core. Anomalies must not be attributed to the core wholesale, nor reclassified wholesale as falling outside it. A local revision requires independent grounds and new testable consequences. Conditions must not be added after the fact merely to explain a known failure.

## 16.4 GOV-04 Tracking Cases, Counterexamples, Assertions, and Forecasts

The five kinds of authoritative record are linked to one another but do not substitute for one another.

`COUNTEREXAMPLE-REGISTRY`. Record the object of the counterexample, scale and window, observable facts, reasons it is not noise, original and alternative explanations, requirements for handling it, and subsequent signals.

`CASE-REGISTRY`. Preserve successes, failures, early dispersals, unfinished processes, counterexamples, indeterminate cases, and excluded cases. For excluded cases, record “whether the insufficiency of material is itself a blind spot of the framework.”

When using a set of cases to compare mechanisms of formation, failure, or dispersal, or to estimate the proportion of a result, state the object, outcome, spatial and temporal window, and entry rule. Preserve actions and processes that had a relevant opportunity to occur but did not. Do not fold unrelated nonoccurrences into the same denominator. Assess the possibility of occurrence and the conditions for detecting it in the material separately. Lack of a record that can be checked must not be entered directly as zero, and a theoretical expectation must not be the sole reason to exclude a real counterexample either. If a boundary is disputed, preserve the judgments under different reasonable scopes and how they change. Interpreting the text of a single case need not, for this purpose, establish a population for frequency estimates. When a professional case-selection record can bear the same responsibility, it may be cited directly (Mahoney and Goertz, 2004, authors’ manuscript).

`CLAIM-REGISTRY`. Record the assertion’s text, type, object, scale, affected positions, evidence, counterevidence, alternative explanations, withdrawal conditions, action ceiling, and publication status.

`FORECAST-REGISTRY`. Record the prediction, time window, triggers, early signals, contrary signals, competing predictions, and outcome at expiry. It is conditional foresight, not a verdict on anyone’s fate.

`VERSION-LOG`. Enter counterexamples, narrowed boundaries, downgrades, suspensions, blocks on publication, withdrawals, replacements, retirements, rollbacks, and remedies in a public ledger.

A formal judgment made in the framework’s name must have a corresponding `CLAIM-REGISTRY` record to qualify for publication or high-impact use under the framework. Ordinary discussion, independent domain research, and well-grounded local opinions bear responsibility for sources, correction, and use according to their own tasks. They do not lose their standing as knowledge for lack of a framework ID. More cases on record do not amount to broader applicability, nor do internally selected cases amount to external validation. Strong-judgment and authorization requirements still apply when the output enters organizational action or automated decisions.

Governance changes have seven additional runtime registries: canonical actor, evidence, relationships of interest, authorization, external review, objections, and the structured `VERSION-LOG`. Every `evidence_ref` must resolve to an evidence record with integrity status `verified`. Reviewers, members who make the decision, and subjects of authorization must use a `canonical actor ID`, and each one’s relationship to the proposer must be checked pair by pair for funding, evaluation, identity, promotion, control, and commercial interests. Aliases may be used only for retrieval and must not substitute for identity. The issuer of authorization must not also be its holder or the proposer.

## 16.5 GOV-05 The Pre-Concept Gate and Ceiling on Conceptual Validity

The framework must not force something into the category of “structural domain” and then use its own concept to prove that classification. Before formal mapping, answer in plain language. Can the parts be observed together? Is there a minimum boundary? Do actions within it repeatedly affect one another? Is there a shared problem, direction, risk, or constraint? Who bears the costs of maintaining it? Can results or bad news change subsequent practice?

If the pre-concept gate fails, the output is limited to describing phenomena, registering risks, or analyzing narratives. Even after it passes, the concept may be used at only one of five levels of strength.

Expressive level. Helps only to state feelings or similarities clearly.

Exploratory level. May generate only questions and signals to observe.

Diagnostic level. External observable material permits judgments with low to moderate confidence.

Strong-judgment level. The object, evidence, scale, responsibility, and observation checks have all passed.

Action level. Additionally requires external review, appeal, anti-retaliation, compensation, and withdrawal, and still requires normative choice under Part 14, J authorization, and O procedures.

## 16.6 GOV-06 Absence Signals, Protective Opacity, and Suppressive Opacity

Invisibility is not the absence of information. Absence of an anchor may mean no shared orientation ever formed. Absence of feedback may mean there is no channel or people are afraid to speak. Absence of conflict may come from fear, dependence, or punishment. Absence of repair may mean there is no interface for it. Absence of exit may mean the price of leaving is unbearable. Absence of language may mean people lack the means to express themselves.

Opacity has three kinds.

`protected`. Protects vulnerable subjects, privacy, witness safety, experience that is still taking shape, and feedback from people with little power.

`functional`. Reduces information overload and preserves room for necessary professional judgment, ritual, buffering, or undisturbed decision-making, but must be subject to subsequent review and tracing of responsibility.

`suppressive`. Obscures responsibility, blocks feedback, evades review, produces fear, preserves a monopoly on power, or prevents people who were harmed from proving the harm.

Classification must answer four questions. Who is protected? Who benefits? Who can review independently? When is the opacity lifted? Opacity must not be recorded as a healthy boundary if the four answers, independent review, time limits, or conditions for lifting it are incomplete. Suppressive opacity directly triggers measures against capture, external review, and a block on public release.

The time limits and conditions for lifting opacity govern continued review of the grounds for confidentiality. They do not mean that sensitive material must be disclosed once a particular date arrives. Review may continue protection or disclose only what is sufficient for a limited judgment. Being unable as yet to confirm that opacity is justified is not the same as having shown that suppression exists. Conversely, a confidentiality label must not be used to conceal falsified records or evasion of responsibility, or to block credible verification.

## 16.7 GOV-07 Operating Limits of Stage 6 and Stopping Observational Recursion

The qualified reference for this module is `governance.module.GOV-07 → hsp.domain.human_intentional_collective → hsp.state.S6`. It applies only to objects that independent runtime records confirm are human intentional collectives and whose status includes `hsp.state.S6`. It does not apply to individual humans, nonhuman systems, or human collectives that have yet to meet S6. Stage 6 here is no universal level of the world broadly conceived.

Stage 6 does not transcend entropy increase or describe a perfect system. For a specified function, object, and limited window, it requires evidence of problem detection, correction of errors, recovery of function, and continuation by those who take up the work. It must also meet the conditions of applicability in Part 8. Record detection lag, repair lag, recurrence rates, resource margins, and load distribution separately. Processes measured in different units must not be combined into a single speed. Current operation that is going well can support a judgment about the corresponding function. It cannot by itself certify Stage 6. Genuine recovery under continuing support does not automatically cease to count because support has yet to be removed.

Observational recursion may expand only when a new response changes boundaries, identity, resources, channels for action, uptake relationships, feedback write-back, risk, or public memory, or supplies a new signal that is costly to produce. Recursion must end when two consecutive rounds yield only the same kind of response, the object begins manipulating diagnostic rules, continued tracking would increase exposure, the informational gain is less than the disruption to the structure, or the diagnostician can no longer distinguish the object’s state from the products of observation.

After stopping, do not keep asking “what the object really is.” State only this: under current observation conditions, a particular response pattern can be confirmed; further judgment requires external review or new external evidence that distinguishes the alternatives.

## 16.8 GOV-08 Malicious Compliance and Auditing Selective Evidence

Malicious compliance is not open violation. It is the selective completion of forms, evidence chains, reviews, and appeal procedures while sources continue to be manipulated, weak signals filtered, inaction dressed up, or responsibility whitewashed. Ten signals must each be checked: a single source; counterexamples dismissed as noise or emotion; open assertions turned into labels; appeals that never change outcomes; supposedly independent parties controlled by the same chain of power; AI reports without original materials and external verification; diagnostic language used for not acting or not holding anyone accountable; potential fields, entropy increase, and stages used to dress up inaction; love, mission, the larger interest, or professional expertise invoked to continue extracting from people with little power; and people who appeal recast as irrational because they appealed.

Three or more signals block strong judgments. At five or more signals, record a high risk of malicious compliance. Only risk registration, a request for external review, and exploratory steps for protection may then be output. The audited party must not itself confirm the reviewer’s independence.

Three signals and five signals are procedural thresholds for starting protection and review. They are neither constants read from nature nor calibrated probabilities of malice. A trigger means that a strong judgment should not proceed under present conditions. It does not amount to a finding that anyone deliberately deceived others. Review must state dependencies among the signals, the price of false alarms and of misses, and the particular circumstances. If these thresholds are to be changed, the change should disclose its grounds through the version procedure and should not be made on the spot after a case outcome appears.

## 16.9 GOV-09 AI Boundaries and the Non-Probative Status of Process Outputs

AI may organize materials and verify their identity, propose and compare mechanisms, form grounded judgments and critiques that can be refuted, recommend options under disclosed objectives, normative premises, and constraints, and draft institutional arrangements, plans, and commentary for deliberation. Outputs should state the current choice and its key grounds, the price to be paid, the strongest opposing case, and the conditions sufficient to change it. They should also present in full the substantive analysis already conducted. AI must not invent sources or protection conditions. AI must not use fluent text, internal scores, or the completion of a procedure to establish the commissioning party’s good faith, effective anti-retaliation, or actual repair, and must not substitute for independent review or for the bearing of responsibility. AI must not independently make a high-risk real-world decision, and must not substitute a claim that a procedure has been passed for the decision of a subject with authority. An analytical recommendation does not amount to actual adoption; where high-risk decisions and real-world execution are involved, the applicable procedures for strong judgments, protection, normative choice, and authorization must still be completed.

AI outputs should check original facts, testimony from affected positions, counterexamples, feedback from people with little power, external review, records of appeals changing outcomes, who controls the evidence, and AI’s selection and rewriting of material, according to the task and proposed conclusion. For each, state how it relates to the conclusion, whether it is available, and what limits follow from its absence. The count of missing items alone does not determine the ceiling for the whole output. Where a factual finding lacks decisive evidence, that finding must not be made; where causal identification lacks discriminating evidence, competing explanations must be retained; where protection and review conditions are inadequate, actual safety, consent, fairness, repair, or eligibility for high-impact adoption must not be certified. Evaluations of arguments in texts, local factual judgments, and conditional recommendations under explicit premises that are unaffected by the gaps can stand separately. A finding of inapplicability must state its reasons and must not be used in place of an unknown or to bypass a real risk. Outputs still governed by strong-judgment and real-world action provisions remain subject to their requirements.

Process outputs are not proof of what holds in reality. Fluent prose, automatic scoring, internal stress tests, consistency matrices, validation passes, report counts, and complete forms cannot establish repair, consent, safety, or effectiveness.

## 16.10 GOV-10 Six Components of Weak-Signal Protection

Weak signals must not depend only on a diagnostician being “willing to listen.” `WEAK-SIGNAL-PROTECTION` must register each of the following items.

WS1 Anonymous or low-exposure submission. People need not disclose their identity before submitting facts.

WS2 Independent parties to receive and take up the material. It must not go only to the chain of power being questioned.

WS3 Anti-retaliation records. Record loss of power, isolation, stigma, punishment, and changes in resources after feedback.

WS4 Calibration by group. Overall satisfaction or average performance must not hide deterioration among groups at the margins.

WS5 Delayed disclosure. When safety is at risk, protect those providing signals before discussing disclosure.

WS6 Evidence of write-back. After a signal is accepted, actual changes to rules, resources, responsibilities, or authoritative records must be visible.

If any component is missing, the output may say only that “the risk to weak signals has been recognized.” It must not claim that “weak signals have been protected.” A single weak signal does not directly establish a conclusion, but neither does it automatically disappear because it is infrequent, anonymous, emotional, or incomplete in form.

## 16.11 GOV-11 An Intermediate Path Without Institutional Infrastructure

Families, intimate relationships, friendships, small teams, and temporary communities often lack independent reviewers, stable appeal procedures, and complete evidence chains. They can still involve ongoing harm, control of information, relational dependence, and risks to reputation or safety. `NO-INFRASTRUCTURE-PATH` does not simulate institutions that are absent, require people affected to take risks gathering evidence, or equate “cannot be reviewed” with “no facts can be recorded.”

It may output only a protective open assertion: one that makes explicit the current material, the continuing pattern that most warrants concern, prohibited uses, escalation signals, withdrawal signals, and a low-risk next step. The objective moves from “proving who is wrong” to reducing exposure, preserving records, recovering support, seeking an outside perspective, and observing subsequent signals. It must not be used for punishment, dossiers, eligibility decisions, public classification, or deprivation of resources.

## 16.12 GOV-12 Protection of Subjects Unable to Exit

Children, people with severe dependencies, people whose income sources are controlled, incarcerated people, people bound to care arrangements, or people with extremely limited resources may be unable to exit. Remaining does not amount to consent. `INABILITY-TO-EXIT-PROTECTION` requires six safeguards: priority for safety; trusted representatives; recovery of minimal choices, information, records, and connections to support; records with low exposure; local buffers and alternative ways out; and a prohibition on romanticizing inability to exit as love, loyalty, maturity, sacrifice, or service to the larger interest.

People should not have to remain exposed to prove their circumstances. Submission through a representative, delayed expression, phased disclosure, uptake by outside parties, preservation of evidence, and refusal of direct dialogue are permitted. The objective of protection is not to force an ideal exit but, under conditions that prevent exit, to reduce harm, preserve subjecthood, and expand future choices.

The tasks taken on by a trusted representative must be specified. Record the grounds and scope separately for assisting understanding and expression, submitting materials for someone, handling a matter delegated by the person, and making a decision on the person’s behalf. A representative’s statement must not be recorded automatically as the person’s own expression or consent. Preserve means of communication the person can use, conflicts of interest, conditions for refusing or changing support, review of objections, and continuity of necessary support. If the person’s wishes remain unclear, state the support efforts made, grounds for interpretation, and uncertainty. Substitute decisions and emergency measures in records or institutions also require separate checks on their institutional basis and normative disputes. Kinship, professional qualifications, or convenience of representation do not establish full standing to represent. Recording requirements must not be used to interrupt necessary support or force disclosure. See the Committee on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, General Comment No. 1 (2014) and its 2018 corrigendum. For representation and authorization, see M07 and D.21.

## 16.13 GOV-13 Complex Trauma and the Absence of a Healthy Baseline

Some structures have never had a healthy state. First distinguish structures that departed from a healthier state, structures with a nominal anchor that has hollowed out in practice, the newly formed type that never developed a healthy anchor, and the trauma-built type, whose rules, identities, boundaries, and relations were built out of trauma from the start.

For the latter two, `TRAUMA-NO-HEALTHY-BASELINE` asks what minimum safety means now, whether the subject is receiving predictable support for the first time, whether they can express themselves without punishment, whether small instances of trust can be repeated, which survival strategies previously protected them but are becoming constraints, and whether a new anchor can be found or must be built together. It does not ask “how to get back to the original state.”

Treatment, clinical care, caregiving, child protection, or legal tasks requiring particular qualifications should be handled under the corresponding professional procedures. Expertise and credentials do not confer authority over the whole of someone’s life. Goals the person values, communication they can understand, objections, and refusal must still be addressed in the relevant procedure. Emergency duties and authority over particular matters require independent grounds that apply to the case. They are not generated by inference from the framework. State scope, validity period, review, and continuity of support separately. The framework must not use its own theory to invent a healthy past. See §1.3, §2.8, and §14.2 for limits on authority, and §15.3.8 for emergency tasks.

“Trauma-built” is a candidate description of the formation history of some structures. It does not designate complex post-traumatic stress disorder or another clinical diagnosis. Harm that calls for protection now may be recorded first, whereas the formation history, particular psychological mechanisms, and professional classification each need evidence. Present harm must not be used to infer that every relationship was formed by trauma. People do not have to establish that an ideal past once existed to receive support.

## 16.14 GOV-14 Keeping Open-Ended Undertaking, Normative Premises, and Metaphorical Drift Separate

Love is not “the remainder that structural explanation cannot account for,” nor is it a favored label for a motive. A description of bounded voluntary undertaking uses `HV11-R1-voluntary-limited-action`. First check the action, identifiable costs, direction, voluntariness, and genuine conditions for refusal, exit, or negotiation. Also check competing explanations such as interests, fear, benefits of identity, inertia, dependence, and coercion. The result can be achieved, unrealized, harmful, or unknown. Successful structural consequences are not an entry requirement for R1. Failed attempts must not be allowed to disappear by definition.

A causal judgment about structural consequences uses `HV11-R2-structural-consequence`. Whether the action changes safety, the ability to refuse, appeal, resources, or later possibilities for affected positions must be judged separately under the relevant G2/causal contract. A beneficial result must not be used to establish voluntariness retroactively, and sincerity or a high price paid must not substitute for evidence of effects either. Normative approval of the action also requires disclosure of the N premises adopted, the protection floor, and costs to others affected. Whether the description holds, whether the effect holds, and whether the action deserves approval are three questions that can each be answered affirmatively or negatively. Risks identified by R0 may generate requirements for protection directly; they need not wait for R1 or R2 to receive support.

These routes constrain the framework’s judgments about particular undertakings and their consequences. They do not exhaust the meaning of love, care, or goodwill in general. Kindness at low cost, private companionship, creative attempts that did not succeed, and the value of ordinary relationships can hold in the appropriate context. Failure to meet a route’s conditions does not negate the actor’s inner life or personality. Voluntarily bearing a limited cost does not create an unlimited duty to continue. Necessary arrangements for others to take over and release from responsibility must be justified separately for the matter at hand.

A broad utility representation can incorporate concern for others, identity, or commitments, but representing a choice as optimal does not by itself identify the reasons for acting or replace a judgment about its normative standing (see Sen, 1977). If benefit terms are merely filled in to match choices already observed, so that any outcome becomes optimal, competing explanations cannot be distinguished on that basis. Exploratory extensions may be retained. A claim of new explanatory capability must state which outcomes the new model can distinguish relative to the old one, on what grounds, and what materials would make the explanation fail. See §2.4 and D.15.

Normative premises must be disclosed and referred to Part 14. Protecting subjects with little power, rejecting uses of structural language that erase responsibility, and favoring openness to appeal and withdrawal and the reproduction of successors able to take up the work are value boundaries to which the framework openly commits. They are not neutral conclusions derived automatically from facts.

The status of a concept used across domains must be marked as metaphor, analogy, candidate mechanism, corroborating empirical evidence, formal model, or verified rule. “Resembles” must not be rewritten as “follows.” Cases from physics, biology, or animal behavior do not directly establish conclusions about human organizations or institutions. Each metaphor must specify similarities, differences, risks of misuse, and evidence that would lead to stopping its use.

## 16.15 GOV-15 Knowledge Lineage and Source Transparency

For each concept that can be traced, record its intellectual affinities, specific source, version, what was retained, adapted, or abandoned, cross-cultural and cross-domain risks, and what the source can and cannot support. Acknowledging intellectual affinities does not weaken the framework; on the contrary, it can reduce mystification and dogmatism.

Theoretical material cannot substitute for case evidence, a single case for a universal rule, an internal summary for original records, or an AI summary for a traceable source. The framework establishes interfaces for interoperability with other theories. It does not present itself as an overarching framework that absorbs them all.

Connections among bodies of knowledge should preserve where disagreements arise. Some theories estimate different outcomes. Some propose competing mechanisms for the same outcome. Some adopt different value premises. Common naming must not erase these differences. When retaining a method, state what distinguishable content it adds to the current task. If a domain method already suffices, using only that method should be permitted.

## 16.16 GOV-16 Usage-Threshold Debt and Accessibility Audits

The more complex the framework, the more likely it is to prevent crude application, and the more easily it creates six kinds of usage-threshold debt: learning requires time and cognitive resources; translation between plain language and terminology loses information; evidence can be difficult to collect safely; external review may be unavailable; the more people invest, the harder it can be to exit; and the status of someone who “knows the framework” can bring interpretive authority and social capital.

Accessibility requires more than adding a shorter version to the complete text. The following must be provided: plain language, ceilings for different levels of use, expression through a representative or with assistance, ways to submit nontextual material, equal weight for appeals, and a way for people to protect themselves before learning the whole framework. At appropriate times it must be said explicitly: “You do not need to learn the framework to protect yourself first, record the facts, and seek support.”

If an equivalent route for appeal, protection, or withdrawal is missing, the framework must not be used for substantively high-impact purposes or embedded in automated decisions. Public dissemination requires closer scrutiny in light of the object, factual allegations, identifiability, and consequences. Ordinary analysis still requires accessible means of correction and withdrawal. Mastery of terminology, complete reading, internal certification, or paid training does not constitute external eligibility.

Usage-threshold debt also includes the labor of maintainers and collaborators. More fields, charts, or complete reading do not automatically produce understanding and fairness. Their value must show up in clearer questions, counterexamples that are addressed, better limits on transfer, and the actual ability of people affected to correct results. People who need assistance expressing themselves should not first have to demonstrate independently the full ability to express themselves before receiving support that makes expression possible.

## 16.17 GOV-17 Tool Use, Commercialization, Weaponization, and Risks of Forking

Courses, consulting, AI tools, organizational software, scorecards, review forms, and public standards that use the framework must retain evidence tracing, downgrading of judgments, protection for low-power positions, access to appeal, anti-retaliation, human review, stopping, and withdrawal. They must not present a lightweight template as a complete diagnosis, internal certification as external review, or AI output as an independent investigation. They also must not treat customer satisfaction as evidence of effectiveness, force a strong diagnosis for the sake of delivery, let the paying party unilaterally control evidence, feedback, and public framing, or let “framework certification” become a new monopoly on eligibility.

Users with sustained disagreements over core commitments may create a fork. A fork does not amount to betrayal. Running different frameworks under the same name without acknowledging a fork creates a greater risk. Each fork must state which empirical question families it retains, which boundaries it abandons, which concepts it changes, and what new risks of misuse arise.

## 16.18 GOV-18 Exit of Open Assertions Captured by Power

An open assertion is only a target for correction under uncertainty. It must exit if it is repeatedly cited as a conclusion, enters performance evaluation, records, discipline, eligibility, resource allocation, or public memory, or is used to suppress appeal. Exit is also required if media, organizations, or AI reduce it to a label, if it becomes a basis for prolonged exclusion or deprivation of resources under the name of “observe first,” or if the judgment is retained while its withdrawal conditions are lost.

After exit, it must be stated explicitly that the assertion must no longer be acted on; the evidence limits, alternative explanations, and withdrawal conditions must be restated; and records that have already been misused must be deleted or flagged. Where harm has already occurred, the matter proceeds to repair, compensation, and examination of the chain of responsibility. If misuse cannot be stopped, the framework must decline to endorse the use.

## 16.19 GOV-19 Version Write-Back and the Feedback Loop

The version log is the authoritative record for writing back counterexamples and governance decisions. It is not an appendix to release notes. For every counterexample, narrowed boundary, downgraded judgment, suspension, block, withdrawal, replacement, retirement, rollback, or remedy, record the event, time, responsible party, affected IDs, previous and new statuses, evidence, counterexamples, consequences for the text, consequences for the procedure, consequences for the action ceiling, and downstream write-back.

Archiving counterexamples without effective review does not complete a correction loop. A valid counterexample should change the affected text, procedure, or action ceiling. A counterexample that does not hold may be declined for reasons, but those grounds and a way to review them must be preserved. A prolonged absence of actual handling of feedback may trigger a review of whether the channel has failed. Absence of changes in the log establishes neither perfection nor, by itself, failure. Correct the log with new entries and retain the old entries and traces of failure.

The runtime `VERSION-LOG` is not a set of free-text references. Every entry must resolve as structured data and precisely bind the governance `record_id`, `subject`, source/target version, `transition`, previous status, new status, and event type. Only standardized numeric versions are accepted. `source_version` must equal the `schema_version` of the framework currently being validated. `target_version` must strictly increase. Material in the same package must not establish its own validity through cross-references among `none`, aliases, or fabricated versions. `applied` must also include at least one actual change to the text, procedure, or action ceiling and use different `before_hash` and `after_hash` values to show actual write-back. Review, the effects of objections, and downstream repair must all point to the same log entry.

Text identity, theory version, structured contract version, software version, and the version actually consumed at runtime must be registered separately, with links that resolve between them. Renaming the theory or moving to a new major version does not reset the software version automatically or establish that installed content has been migrated. Writing back the text, building the software, using the recorded version at runtime, producing answers that meet requirements, and benefiting a real task have distinct points of verification.

The endpoint of governance feedback should match the problem. A typo correction concerns the authoritative text and its propagation. Correcting a numerical value in the source also requires checking the calculations that depend on it. Use beyond authority must be traced to actual execution and remedy. Upholding a correct decision for stated reasons can constitute adequate review. If an erroneous decision is overturned but the change has yet to be executed, it can be confirmed only that the decision has been changed. The version log and hashes locate changes. They cannot substitute for the meaning or real effects of those changes.

## 16.20 GOV-20 External Review Packages and Gates for Public Release

Internal stress testing is only an internal wind tunnel for finding misuse of terms, misjudgment of paths, and inconsistencies. It must not be used to establish that the framework is reliable in reality or better than alternatives, or for marketing or certification.

An external review package includes the assertions to review, a traceability matrix, publication status, known defects, review questions and criteria, reviewer identities and conflicts of interest, feedback write-back, and protection for reviewers. Review types include source tracing, review by people in affected positions, review by domain experts, red-team review for misuse, review of public language, and review of publication gates. If a kind of review remains absent for a long time, especially review by people in affected positions, the gap itself blocks publication.

Reviewers must not establish their external status by declaring it themselves. Reviewers, decision members, decision-making bodies, authorization issuers, scope of authorization, subjects, target versions, validity periods, and relationships of interest must all resolve in the runtime registries. The external approval record must also bind precisely to the current governance record, `subject`, target version, `transition`, and list of objections. `applied` may use only an external review with an `approved` decision. An alias with the same name, an internal stamp, or self-issued authorization must not substitute for it.

Every strong judgment for public release must pass nine checks individually: complete traceability, source quality, handling of counterexamples, affected positions, shifts in burden, adversarial testing for misuse, public language, external review, and the strong-judgment kit. If any item fails, the judgment must not be published as a strong judgment. Passing all of them yields only `publishable`. It still confers no authorization to act.

### 16.20.1 Organizing Methods and Reviewing a Sample

Instructions for use and reference entries organized from the original text may be reviewed by sampling within a stated scope. Before sampling, state the population, categories, selection basis, and question to be judged. Cover different mechanisms, objects, and output types. Include entries concerning high-impact use, conversion across scales, or theoretical boundaries. A small convenience sample cannot represent categories it does not cover.

If review finds omitted concepts, widened scope of application, or confusion between the original and derived material, expand review of the relevant categories and trace the methods and conclusions that depend on the entry. Sampling results must distinguish entries actually reviewed from those not reviewed individually. Give the grounds and scope of a decision to adopt the entire batch separately. Permission to sample, passing procedural checks, or finding no errors so far in the sample does not amount to item-by-item confirmation by the author. These also must not substitute for substantive review of real-world high-impact judgments.

An empirical judgment offered by the author that serves as a theoretical method should be entered in the relevant part of the original text with its applicable objects, grounds, counterexamples, and stopping conditions. Editorial suggestions that have not entered the original text remain candidates. Supplementary method entries must not create a separate theory attributed to the author. Entry into the original text changes its textual status and does not automatically turn personal experience into a universal empirical proposition or proof of effectiveness.

## 16.21 GOV-21 Benign Demise of the Framework and Interfaces for Replacement

A framework’s maturity lies not in explaining everything but in knowing when to exit. Benign demise does not necessarily mean that the entire text disappears; it can also be a concept, module, or empirical question family leaving the core. There are five kinds of trigger: a module is persistently superseded by a simpler, more falsifiable method with less unintended harm; a concept only increases the cost of understanding without increasing diagnostic capability; an empirical question family continually relies on exception patches in a key domain; users suppress outside theories or real counterexamples to preserve the framework; and gains from the framework’s identity, commercial value, or power exceed gains in judgment quality.

Replacement must state how far this framework can judge, where it starts to fail, what the successor method takes over, how the two cross-check each other, what records and unresolved harms must be handed off, who is responsible for the handoff, and when it takes effect. The decision may be `restrict`, `suspend`, `compete`, `replace`, `retire`, or `handoff`. The framework’s creator, maintainers, or commercial beneficiaries must not veto replacement unilaterally or perpetuate themselves by adding concepts without limit.

The general principle of governance is this: the stronger the explanatory power, the more withdrawal must be preserved; the greater the publication capability, the more counterexamples from people with little power must be protected; and the higher the degree of automation, the more AI output must be downgraded to auxiliary material. A structural diagnosis, whatever it is, must not erase a person’s particular circumstances, and must not dissolve responsibility into complexity, stages, potential fields, entropy increase, or love.

## 16.22 GOV-22 Multi-Circle Complexity and Governance of Foresight

Multi-circle capability creates five kinds of governance debt. Object debt arises when circles are treated as entities because they are named. Variable debt arises when candidates from residuals are automatically filled in as facts. Complexity debt arises when variables and paths grow without out-of-sample gains. Calibration debt arises when forecasts are published without registering results. Power debt arises when predictions are used for screening, monitoring, punishment, or manipulation. When any debt exceeds its threshold set in advance, the framework should reduce the model, downgrade the output, suspend high-impact use, or retire the relevant module.

The forecast registry must retain successes, failures, unresolved results, invalidated targets, and results that cannot be evaluated. Publishing only the hits is not permitted. External reviewers can check model versions, input cutoffs, simple baselines, metrics, and outcome write-back. A claim of foresight capability is automatically downgraded if long-term calibration deteriorates, errors concentrate in particular groups, the complex model ceases to outperform the baseline, or results continue to depend on variables added after the fact.

## 16.23 GOV-23 Governing Personality, Privacy, and Overreach by Automation

Personality hypotheses carry a high risk of misuse. Governance must check collection purposes, authorization, privacy level, permitted uses, cross-circle propagation, appeals by people concerned, deletion or correction, model versions, and downstream impacts. Prohibited uses include clinical diagnosis, adjudicating the essence of a personality, discriminatory screening, unauthorized risk scoring, and using silence as evidence.

AI may propose and compare variable candidates and paths, verify the identity of materials, form judgments that can be refuted, and offer recommendations under publicly stated normative premises. It must not certify reality through its own generated content, diagnose personality, decide values for others, or issue permissions. Any automated system that promotes `candidate` to `observed`, promotes `simulated` to a real-world event, or promotes foresight results to authorization must block the run and leave an audit record. Human review is effective only when reviewers can see evidence, counterexamples, and uncertainty, have the relevant capability, time available in advance, and a manageable workload, and can actually refuse, modify, or suspend an action. Those signals must be able to reach the executor in time and change what it does; they must not merely leave a single click in an interface.

The effectiveness of human oversight should be assessed on all of the following together: whether correct recommendations can be adopted appropriately, whether incorrect ones can be intercepted, whether problems that were not flagged can be detected, and whether affected people can correct results. More overrides are not necessarily better, and complete compliance does not establish reliability. For narrowly scoped actions that run faster than humans can judge them one at a time, machine-checkable constraints and minimum permissions may be studied, but responsibility for choosing goals, omissions in constraints, taking over in exceptional cases, and remedies must still be explicit, and system-wide risk must not be shifted wholesale onto the person who clicks last.

## 16.24 GOV-24 Model Degradation, Retirement, and Replacement

The framework must have mechanisms for retirement and replacement. If a simpler method consistently performs better in explanation, foresight, protection, and accessibility, cases, contracts, records of failure, and appeals should be handed off through the replacement interface; preserving the existing framework is not the aim. At retirement, necessary source chains, records of responsibility, and remedies for harm must be retained; the retired model must no longer be used for new decisions; and it must be disclosed which conclusions remain valid, which are downgraded, and which cannot be transferred.

Replacement comparisons must assess at least judgment quality and total burden together. First fix the task, materials, input cutoff, resources, and method of evaluation. Retain a sufficiently strong domain method and accurate references back to sources for comparison. Then check misjudgments, omissions, unwarranted abstention, time, disclosure, and maintenance labor separately. Entry of a candidate into the text establishes only editorial adoption. If candidates from the same source share one change, the gains from adoption must not be counted once per candidate. Line breaks, character counts, and file counts can be verified, but those counts cannot stand in for measures of reader costs or work outcomes.

Without an actual comparison, do not claim that the framework is better than alternatives, that they have been shown to be equivalent, or that the framework is useless. Preserve historical comparisons according to their actual versions, tasks, and denominators for failure. Local improvements can hold while regressions in other dimensions and delivery failures still have to be shown. When theory, software, and materials all change together, a comparison can evaluate the combination. Differences between combinations must not be attributed wholesale to one theoretical statement or one form of packaging.

## 16.25 Context-Specific Parameters and Missing Information

The following parameters must be determined by external procedures in the specific setting: participants in consensus and time limits; the observation windows, samples, and decision makers needed to reinstate empirical question families; safe channels, custodians, and remedial institutions for weak signals; risk weights among gaps in AI outputs; and criteria for comparisons with alternatives across disciplines, cultures, and jurisdictions. Registration must state the applicable organization, jurisdiction, object, risk level, evidence base, validity period, and review conditions. Numbers from one setting must not be copied as universal thresholds. If these fields are missing, the conclusion remains downgraded, suspended, or subject to competition. The framework must not fill them in itself.

A missing parameter blocks only judgments and uses that depend on it. Other conclusions with independent grounds are retained within their own scope. Unknowns kept for protection, lack of the ability to observe, genuine inapplicability, and evidence still to be supplied must continue to be kept separate. In actual use, also assess proportionality by comparing whether extra recording can change the judgment and reduce harm with the delays, exposure, and maintenance costs it creates.

[^p17-tn-diagnosis]: Translator's note: Diagnosis here is an assessment of a specified object against stated criteria within this framework.

<a id="part_17"></a>

# Appendix A Human Variable Interface Cards

This appendix contains the complete interface cards for the 11 human variables (HV01–HV11) in Part 7. Each card is organized into five sections, A–E. A covers identity, proposition, and scope. B covers formal dependencies and limits on conclusions. C covers the nine-axis scale and object contract.[^p18-tn-contract] D covers state, evidence, and variable flows. E covers uptake, responsibility, normative status, ceilings, and correction.

The 11 cards below set out all 39 formal fields for each variable. The fields specify the structure of the contract. Collect specific evidence as required by the selected route, taking each item’s relevance to the task into account. Record inapplicability, unknowns, material not yet collected, and material unavailable for reasons of protection separately. Completing the form is no prerequisite for ordinary services or understanding. An empty dependency set is explicitly written as “None (empty set).” It means only that nothing is registered for that kind of dependency. It does not establish that evidence is sufficient or that an empirical claim holds. Each conditional support route must be read as a whole, one route at a time. Favorable conditions from different routes must not be combined after the fact.

## A.1 HV01 Structural Domain (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Interface ID (`id`) | HV01 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV01` |
| Name (`name`) | Structural Domain |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | D0 declares only a candidate human object. A limited effective structural domain is registered within the instance’s scope only if a pre-registered G1-instance shows that the candidate grouping achieves an out-of-sample or extrapolation gain above the threshold on the preselected outcome relative to a matched N0. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Relationships, teams, organizations, institutions, and public issues |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | The object, boundary, scale, time window, or object identity is insufficient to support the selected route; when formal G1 support is sought, the corresponding null model or instance contract is incomplete |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | 1. D0 |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. E1<br>2. EVIDENCE<br>3. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | None (empty set) |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV01-R0-candidate-object;<br>claim_level=candidate_description;<br>when=The D0 object contract is complete, but there is not yet an eligible G1-instance with result_state=supported;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate human object, the set of materials, boundary disputes, and the need for further G1 evidence;<br>result_ceiling=Candidate object description only. A reference to the object with its boundary and uncertainty may be supplied to HV02–HV11, but this reference must not be treated as an effective structural domain supported by G1.<br>2. route_id=HV01-R1-effective-domain;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=A pre-registered G1-instance for the same object, scale, window, K, and extrapolation unit is supported;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G1-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register the limited effective structural domain, strength of object identification, boundary credibility, and window of applicability within the scope of that instance;<br>result_ceiling=Limited to the pre-registered SP/T/K and generalization_unit. Judgments about the object’s ultimate nature, a unified will, or authorization must not be made. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. R0 may register a candidate object, boundary disputes, and limited descriptive references<br>2. The limited effective structural domain and identification strength supported by the gain may be registered only within the G1-instance’s pre-registered object, scale, window, outcome, and extrapolation unit |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Inferring an objective community from naming it. 2. Inferring a common will from shared circumstances |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers relational or event units, the candidate member population, distributions inside and outside the boundary, and grouping rules. X=Spatial extent covers common locations, organizational boundaries, digital platforms, and external environments across domains. T=Time span covers the identification window, cycles of membership change, and boundary history. O=Organizational level covers roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers raw events, interaction mechanisms, meso-level relational structures, and institutional and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw interactions, event sequences, individual member cases, boundary distributions, metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct members, people excluded, people indirectly affected, second-order spillovers, and positions across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers membership relations, boundary connections, isolated points, and bridging across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for naming the object, adopting the boundary, and subsequent disposition. For an inapplicable axis, record the reason for `not_applicable` |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | A candidate object declared by D0. It is registered as a limited effective structural domain within the instance’s scope only if the pre-registered G1-instance passes the threshold test relative to a matched N0 |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Object contract. 2. Participating and affected positions |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Units and population<br>2. Representativeness<br>3. J axis<br>4. Positions with low visibility |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Effective membership, relationships, and object identity can change with scale |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman systems without intentional, institutional, or responsibility interfaces |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating a local group as directly representative of all affected people |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Candidate. 2. Identifiable. 3. Boundary disputed. 4. Does not hold |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Differences in relationship density and constraints inside and outside the boundary<br>2. Records of members entering, exiting, or being excluded<br>3. Repeated co-occurrence of common problems, resource channels, or institutional rules<br>4. Whether object identification remains stable after changing the grouping rule |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. D0 candidate object and object identity records<br>2. The G1-instance pre-registration and matched N0<br>3. Training results and out-of-sample or extrapolation results<br>4. Comparison of gains for the candidate and competing groupings |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. D0 provides only candidate object fields, which do not constitute evidence that the structure holds<br>2. Pre-registered G1-instance, matched N0, threshold, model class, and sample or extrapolation unit<br>3. Observation positions, competing groupings, and the E1 protocol |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. R0 supplies the other variables with a candidate object reference, boundary, time window, and uncertainty. R1 additionally supplies the limited effective scope supported by G1<br>2. Preserve grounds for both the object’s existence and domain facts separately from grouping gains |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register the identification window, boundary changes, and lags in membership changes |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record boundary disputes, absent members, and observation coverage |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | The aggregate must not represent positions where people cannot express themselves safely or that were not sampled |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Members. 2. People excluded. 3. Cost bearers outside the boundary |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Relationship network. 2. Organizational boundary. 3. Institutional records |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. The analyst who proposes the structural domain judgment<br>2. The decision maker who uses the judgment |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | A descriptive H-World interface. It confers no normative justification |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | A limited effective object at the level of explanation may be registered only if the G1-instance passes, and only for the pre-registered object, scale, window, outcome, and extrapolation unit. Otherwise it remains only a candidate object or a set of materials |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of candidate structural domains, boundary disputes, and needs for more evidence. It does not authorize inclusion, exclusion, or disposition. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. People repeatedly present in the same place lack stable relationships or common constraints<br>2. A group defined by the analyst disappears as soon as the grouping rule changes |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, members and people at affected positions may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, challenge the boundary, representativeness, and the identity criterion, and trigger review independent of the original naming or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke the structural domain registration, remove its effect on the scope of downstream objects, and restore the record to a set of materials; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

A=Aggregation level covers individual cross-boundary events, cases of interface use, populations by membership category, distributions of admission and refusal, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers physical points of entry, organizational boundaries, digital interfaces, legal jurisdictions, and cross-domain channels. O=Organizational level covers user roles, gatekeeping teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers cross-boundary events, mechanisms of gatekeeping interaction, interface structure, institutional rules, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw admission and refusal records, sequences of use, individual cases, flow distributions, service metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct users, people excluded, positions where benefits or costs occur indirectly, second-order spillovers, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers interface nodes, gatekeeping bottlenecks, alternative paths, and cross-domain connections. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for inclusion, exclusion, interface changes, and adjustments of rights and responsibilities. Scale elevation requires differences to be recorded axis by axis |

| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman boundaries without concepts of membership, rights, or responsibility |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating greater spatial or organizational scope as automatically conferring jurisdiction |

Gatekeeping nodes, wait times, and reasons for refusal<br>4. Differences between formal boundaries and boundaries of actual passage |

| Field | What to Record |

X=Spatial extent covers sites of relationships, organizational spaces, digital public spaces, and the reach of propagation across domains. C=Causal level covers events of expression, mechanisms of interaction between meaning and action, meso-level coordination structures, institutional arrangements, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw expressions and actions, event sequences, individual cases, distributions of stances, coordination metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct participants, dissenters, indirectly affected people, second-order consequences for coordination, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers propagation of expressions, links for coordination, heterogeneous clusters, and bridging nodes. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for naming the anchor, adopting a judgment of representativeness, and requiring coordination or uniformity. Diverse anchors must be preserved |

| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Rules of representation<br>2. Heterogeneity<br>3. Distribution of costs and benefits<br>4. J axis |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. The content and strength of an anchor and the subjects who carry it can change |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman systems without intentionality, meaning, or capability for commitment |

Changes in action choices, rates of completion in collaboration, or conflict patterns<br>3. Acceptance, rejection, and alternative expressions of the anchor at different positions<br>4. Whether the outcome difference under the comparison conditions exceeds the predetermined threshold |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Resource adjustments<br>2. Sequences of action<br>3. Commitment and exit records<br>4. Evidence of conflict |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Positions of participation. 2. Safety of expression. 3. Data on resources and actions |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Generative events. 2. Mobilization of uptake. 3. Agendas for normative choice |

I=Scope of influence covers direct participants in generation, people taking up the work, indirectly affected people, second-order consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers propagation of conditions, collaboration among subjects, diffusion of events, and emergent connections. Register scales separately for GC, GS, and GE |

The pre-registered state transition from before to after the generative event<br>4. Records of the bridge from local interaction to aggregate outcome in emergence without a subject |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Records of initiation<br>2. Windows for conditions<br>3. Actions by subjects<br>4. Mechanisms of interaction without a subject |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Orientation anchor. 2. Resources and institutional conditions. 3. Causal contract |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Need for uptake. 2. State transitions. 3. The start of a chain of responsibility |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register accumulation of conditions, triggering events, and lags in formation |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record joint generation, emergence without a subject, and subjects that cannot be identified |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Overlooked informal initiators and affected positions |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Initiators<br>2. People taking up the work<br>3. Beneficiaries<br>4. People affected |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Initiators<br>2. Procedures<br>3. Technical facilities<br>4. Relationship networks |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Those who actually act. 2. Decision makers. 3. Parties granting authorization |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | The fact of generation does not establish normative justification or a complete attribution of responsibility |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Up to the level of explanation when the mechanism chain is complete |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only candidate classifications for GC, GS, and GE, descriptions of state transitions, and needs for more evidence. It does not authorize starting, spreading, or stopping a generative process. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. A technological condition is incorrectly described as a generative subject with a goal<br>2. An emergent process without a unified initiator is forced into an attribution to one visible person |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people classified as generative subjects may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, challenge attributions of intent, role, and authorization, and trigger review independent of the original classification or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline correct the GC, GS, and GE types and actually remove erroneous attributions of intent or responsibility and their downstream effects; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

[^p18-tn-contract]: Translator's note: *Contract* renders 合同 in an analytical sense: a specification registered and frozen before results are known, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

## A.5 HV05 Action Uptake Layer (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
