| Interface ID (`id`) | HV05 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV05` |
| Name (`name`) | Action Uptake Layer |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | Uptake carriers CV that execute, transmit, maintain, record, provide care, or repair must be recorded separately from responsible parties RS, cost bearers, beneficiaries, and rights to stop. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Human structures requiring sustained action, maintenance, care, or execution |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | People who execute with limited permissions are classified by default as primarily responsible, or the capability to take up work is treated as an obligation |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | None (empty set) |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. EVIDENCE<br>2. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | 1. H2 |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV05-R0-carrier-responsibility-split;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=CV, bearers of costs of the same type, beneficiaries, rights to stop, RS, resources, and capacity can be recorded separately under H2;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Register current uptake carriers, tasks, costs, capacity, rights to stop, types of responsibility, and gaps in uptake;<br>result_ceiling=Only the current classification and description of gaps. The capability to take up work does not become an obligation, and CV does not become RS.<br>2. route_id=HV05-R1-functional-carrier-effect;<br>claim_level=conditional_effect;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance shows that replacing, interrupting, replenishing, or reducing load on the specified carrier has a channel effect above the threshold on the preselected functional outcome;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register the candidate uptake effect of the specified carrier on the measured dimensions of function, capacity, delay, or loss;<br>result_ceiling=Unmeasured dimensions remain unknown. Functional effects must not directly generate responsibility, a duty to make sacrifices, or authorization to reallocate resources.<br>3. route_id=HV05-R2-intertemporal-reproduction;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=The current uptake channel already has support from a G2-instance, and a G3-instance shows that its historical variables provide a conditional increment for subsequent uptake or reproduction outcomes;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance, G3-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for intertemporal uptake or reproduction with a historical increment beyond the current state, within the specified window and carrier;<br>result_ceiling=Historical inevitability, irreversibility, attribution of responsibility, or a duty to keep bearing the work must not be inferred.<br>4. route_id=HV05-R3-historical-carrier-trace;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=An H5-instance returns supported for the specific carrier and durability criterion, with exactly one of each selected in advance;<br>additional_inferential_requires=H5-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register durable traces in the human domain within the specified carrier, trace observables, and window, and submit predefined candidate historical variables to a G3-instance;<br>result_ceiling=An H5-instance does not establish future path effects, intertemporal reproduction, a repair window, responsibility, or action. Evidence for these conclusions must be obtained separately under the carrier or historical-increment routes, outcome contracts, and normative procedures.<br>5. route_id=HV05-R4-carried-reproduction;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance supports the effect of the specified uptake channel on the declared subsequent outcomes within the stated window, and the current carriers of capabilities, rules, resources, or relationships, retention or replenishment across several cycles, actual continuity, and discriminating causal materials are verified under §4.5;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for intertemporal uptake or reproduction through carrier transmission within the specified carriers, cycles, and outcomes;<br>result_ceiling=A historical increment beyond the current state must not be registered, and long-term effects must not be extrapolated from a single functional effect. The fact of uptake must not generate a duty or authorization to keep bearing the work. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Gaps in uptake. 2. Mismatches between tasks and resources. 3. Distinctions among responsibilities |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Treating the most visible person as primarily responsible<br>2. Inferring that someone ought to bear the work because they can<br>3. Assigning responsibility solely because facilities or procedures execute a function. Organizational responsibility is assessed separately under the organization’s standing as a subject and the specific rules |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual tasks or uptake events, cases involving carriers, the uptake population, distributions of costs and capacity, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers work sites, team or organizational boundaries, digital systems, and the reach of services across domains. T=Time span covers task cycles, maintenance windows, recovery lags, and the period in which responsibility applies. O=Organizational level covers execution roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers events of execution, mechanisms of interaction between tasks and resources, meso-level uptake structures, institutional arrangements of responsibility, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw task logs, sequences of uptake, cases involving carriers, distributions of costs and capacity, performance metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers those directly taking up the work, people dependent on services, positions that indirectly receive benefits or bear costs, second-order spillovers, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers dependencies in uptake, alternative paths, single-point bottlenecks, and service networks across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for task assignment, stopping, resource adjustments, attribution of responsibility, and remedy. Register scales separately for CV and RS |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | People, positions, procedures, facilities, or institutions that actually execute, transmit, maintain, record, provide care, or repair |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. CV does not amount to RS<br>2. Costs and benefits are recorded separately<br>3. Rights to stop |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Aggregation of tasks<br>2. Representation and delegation<br>3. Six kinds of responsibility<br>4. External costs |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Uptake carriers and responsible parties can change with the level |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman processes without action by a subject, responsibility, or maintenance requirements |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Equating uptake by an individual directly with organizational responsibility |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Adequate<br>2. Fragile<br>3. Overloaded<br>4. Disrupted<br>5. Replaced |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Logs of actual task execution, maintenance, recording, and repair<br>2. Flows of resources, capacity, time, and costs<br>3. Records of rights to stop, alternative arrangements, and transfer of uptake<br>4. Grounds for decisions, authorization, oversight, benefit, and remedy |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Task flows<br>2. Working hours and resources<br>3. Maintenance records<br>4. Records of stopping and refusal<br>5. If the objective concerns overall burden, check management, being on call, conditions for those taking over, and continuity separately. A judgment about one task need not collect all of these |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Demand arising from generation. 2. Resources. 3. Authorization. 4. Roles |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Realization of state transitions. 2. Cost distribution. 3. Structural load |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register schedules, maintenance cycles, backlogs, and recovery lags |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record hidden labor, informal care, and costs outside the boundary |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | People taking up work with limited permissions, informally, or without the ability to exit |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. People taking up the work<br>2. Beneficiaries<br>3. People served<br>4. Replacements |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Personnel<br>2. Positions<br>3. Procedures<br>4. Facilities<br>5. Institutions |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Parties responsible for conduct<br>2. Parties responsible for decisions<br>3. Parties responsible for authorization<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight<br>5. Parties responsible as beneficiaries<br>6. Parties responsible for remedy |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | The fact of uptake does not generate a duty to keep bearing the work |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 is limited to classification of uptake and responsibility. R1 is limited to identified functional effects of carriers. R3 is limited to durable traces supported by H5. R4 explains reproduction through carriers under §4.5 and with channel evidence covering subsequent windows. R2 explains historical increments beyond the current representation only with additional G3. Each route is limited by its own materials and window. No route may substitute for the others, and none creates an unlimited duty to bear the work. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of CV, RS, costs, capacity, rights to stop, and gaps in uptake, along with needs to reduce load, add resources, or reallocate them. It does not authorize changes in tasks, resource allocation, attribution of responsibility, or protective action. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. The most visible executor with limited permissions is not the party responsible for decisions or authorization, or responsible as a beneficiary<br>2. An automated facility bears transmission tasks but cannot bear moral or legal responsibility |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people taking up the work may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, challenge tasks, resources, costs, restrictions on the right to stop, and attribution of responsibility, and trigger review independent of the original chain of task assignment or attribution |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke erroneous task, resource, or responsibility statuses, actually restore previous task and record states, and carry out authorized remedies; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

Complete interface records do not amount to causal efficacy for each segment.<br>3. route_id=HV06-R2-effective-channel;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=The complete candidate chain has been assembled and an eligible G2-instance supports the specified channel. Correspondence within the same case, shared scope of support, joint response, temporal order, capacity, and the endpoint outcome must also suffice to support the whole-chain effect in question. The average effects on the edges must not simply be multiplied;<br>additional_inferential_requires=human_variable:HV03, human_variable:HV04, human_variable:HV05, G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register the effective drive–uptake channel, losses, and mechanisms of interruption for the tested segments and window;<br>result_ceiling=Intertemporal reproduction, responsibility, normative justification, or authorization to act must not be inferred from one pass through the chain. |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers events at individual links, cases involving chains, the population of similar chains, distributions of inputs and outputs, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of action, project or organizational boundaries, digital spaces for collaboration, and spillovers across domains. T=Time span covers windows for initiation, maintenance, interruption, and recovery, and cycles across periods. O=Organizational level covers initiating roles, execution teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers events at links, mechanisms of interaction among drive, resources, and tasks, meso-level uptake chains, institutional arrangements, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw records of tasks and resources, sequences of links, cases involving chains, loss distributions, performance metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers positions of direct initiation and uptake, positions of indirect benefit or cost, second-order spillovers, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers chains of dependency, alternative paths, bottlenecks, feedback connections, and bridges across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for adopting goals, assigning tasks, committing resources, stopping, and trials. Mark positions across the axes for each segment |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | A traceable chain that converts orientation and generation into sustained action |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. The chain of orientation, generation, uptake, resources, costs, and responsibility |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Bridges across levels<br>2. Aggregation loss<br>3. Carryover of responsibility<br>4. Protection floor |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Nodes, channels, and bottlenecks can change with organizational scale |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman processes without intentional drive or human uptake |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating local drive or a single node as directly representative of the complete chain |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Connected end to end<br>2. Lagging<br>3. Overloaded<br>4. Disrupted<br>5. Replaced |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Records of the anchor being translated into tasks, budgets, rules, or schedules<br>2. Inputs, outputs, delay, loss, and points of interruption for each segment<br>3. Capacity of people taking up the work, stopping, and changes in alternative paths<br>4. Actual contribution of chain outputs to the target outcome |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Resource flows<br>2. Task and maintenance records<br>3. Repayment and costs<br>4. Time lags |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Orientation anchor. 2. Generative node. 3. Uptake layer |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Action outcomes. 2. Load. 3. Feedback and traces of evolution |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register lags in initiation, transmission, maintenance, and repayment segment by segment |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record breaks, alternative channels, and repayment outside the boundary |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Informal, low-visibility, and cross-organizational uptake positions |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Initiators<br>2. People taking up the work<br>3. Beneficiaries<br>4. Cost bearers |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Personnel and positions<br>2. Procedures<br>3. Budgets<br>4. Infrastructure |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Those responsible at each node for conduct, decisions, authorization, oversight, and remedy |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Effectiveness of the chain does not establish that its goal is normatively justified |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Up to the level of explanation or diagnosis when evidence for the whole chain is sufficient |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of chain connectivity, time lags, losses, interruptions, costs, and uptake needs. It does not authorize load reduction, resource adjustments, or trials. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. A compelling vision and concentrated mobilization lack sustained resources, maintenance, or repayment<br>2. A chain that appears connected end to end shifts key costs to people taking up work outside its boundary |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people taking up work along the chain or at affected positions may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, challenge resources, costs, and attribution to the chain, and trigger review independent of the original judgment about the chain or the decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke attribution to the chain as a whole and cancel the downstream effect of that attribution, and restore descriptions at the level of individual nodes and the unresolved status; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

## A.7 HV07 Feedback Write-Back (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Interface ID (`id`) | HV07 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV07` |
| Name (`name`) | Feedback Write-Back |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | An appeal, audit, or piece of feedback constitutes institutional write-back in a human structure only if it changes records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions and the corresponding change has actually been executed. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Human structures with feedback, appeals, audits, or governance procedures |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | The claim that institutional write-back holds is suspended if there is no state update and only a receipt, statement of position, or publication, or if field changes have not actually been executed. Limited statuses such as submitted, intake complete, and fields changed are still classified under R0. |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | 1. D2 |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. EVIDENCE<br>2. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | 1. H3 |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV07-R0-writeback-classification;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=Input channels, receipts, field versions before and after, execution records, time of entry into effect, duration, and stopping or rollback status can be checked separately;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Distinguish the write-back statuses of not submitted, submitted, intake complete, fields changed, executed, continuing, or no longer in effect;<br>result_ceiling=Only field changes that are actually executed count as institutional write-back. A single write-back does not count as learning, nor does write-back amount to correcting an error correctly. The procedural value of upholding a decision for reasons, and any other changes that have occurred, are retained according to their own evidence.<br>2. route_id=HV07-R1-causal-feedback;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance shows that an institutional return channel changes the preselected subsequent state or transition relative to a condition without a return or with the channel blocked;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register effective feedback and effects of institutional write-back within the specified fields, channel, and window;<br>result_ceiling=Learning, long-term repair, normative justification, or broader authorization must not be inferred from the presence of feedback.<br>3. route_id=HV07-R2-feedback-mediated-learning;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=Effective feedback already has support from a G2-instance, and a G3-instance shows that a retainable update provides a historical conditional increment for a predetermined task over repeated rounds;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance, G3-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for feedback-mediated learning with a historical increment beyond the current state, within the specified task, rounds, and window;<br>result_ceiling=It must not be claimed that the institution as a whole has learned, that repair is complete, or that its value direction is right.<br>4. route_id=HV07-R3-carried-learning;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance already supports effective feedback. The current carrier of the update, retention and actual use across rounds, a comparison for the predetermined task, and the causal basis for that comparison are verified under C3-carried-learning and §4.5. An instance of immediate feedback does not automatically cover later task outcomes;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for feedback-mediated learning through carrier transmission within the specified update carrier, task, and rounds;<br>result_ceiling=This does not qualify as a historical increment under CM-LEARNING-history or G3. Training, archiving, write-back, or a single success must not be called learning directly, and it must not be claimed that the institution as a whole has improved. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Effective write-back, blocked write-back, and feedback in appearance only |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Inferring learning from the presence of a channel<br>2. Treating one update as long-term repair<br>3. Treating silence as consent |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual feedback events or appeals, cases of write-back, the feedback population, distributions of intake and execution, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers submission channels, organizational or platform boundaries, institutional jurisdictions, and the reach of appeals across domains. T=Time span covers submission, intake, field changes, execution, duration, and review lags. O=Organizational level covers feedback roles, intake teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers feedback events, mechanisms of interaction in write-back, meso-level procedural structures, institutional rules, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw feedback, processing sequences, cases of write-back, outcome distributions, timeliness metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers people directly appealing or taking up the work, indirectly affected people, second-order institutional consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers feedback channels, intake nodes, review paths, blockages, and connections across levels. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for intake, field changes, execution, stopping, rollback, and remedy. Register paths across levels |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | A return channel that changes a subsequent institutional state or transition |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Source and channel of feedback, write-back fields, and subsequent changes |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Representativeness of feedback<br>2. Write-back paths across levels<br>3. Aggregation loss<br>4. External review |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Write-back carriers, lags, and responsible parties can change |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Processes without records, rules, resources, roles, or stopping conditions |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating feedback from an individual case as directly representative of the population’s views |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Arrival<br>2. Intake<br>3. Write-back<br>4. Blockage<br>5. Distortion |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Evidence of submission and intake of feedback or an appeal<br>2. Differences between versions of records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, or stopping conditions<br>3. Execution records, time of entry into effect, and duration of the change<br>4. Review, reversal, remedy, and subsequent state changes |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Original feedback<br>2. Intake trail<br>3. Versions of fields<br>4. Subsequent changes to rules or resources |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Feedback sources<br>2. Safe channels<br>3. Responsible parties<br>4. Review procedures |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Updates to records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register time limits for submission, intake, decision, execution, and further review |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record feedback that did not arrive, anonymity for protection, and handling that cannot be observed |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Positions without safe submission or digital access, or under threat of retaliation |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. People submitting material<br>2. People evaluated<br>3. People carrying out the action<br>4. Beneficiaries of the institution |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Appeal systems<br>2. Audit procedures<br>3. Meeting records<br>4. Repositories of rules<br>5. Chains of responsibility |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Parties responsible for intake<br>2. Decision makers<br>3. Parties who execute write-back<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Whether feedback is effective and whether its contents are normatively justified are judged separately |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 judges how far the write-back process has actually gone. R1 judges the effect of the return channel with corresponding support from G2. R3 judges feedback-mediated learning through a carrier from retention of the update, its use across rounds, and a task comparison. R2 judges learning with a historical increment only with additional G3. A single write-back, learning in general, transfer, and net benefits are assessed separately. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of intake, field changes, execution, duration, and gaps in write-back, along with needs for review or procedural repair. It does not authorize rewriting records or rules, carrying out repair, or closing appeals. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. An appeal gets a receipt, but records, rules, resources, and stopping conditions all remain unchanged<br>2. An audit report is published without a responsible party, deadline, or subsequent state update |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people who submit feedback may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, request its status, time limit, responsible party, and write-back outcome, and trigger review independent of the original intake, write-back, or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke erroneous updates and actually restore the previous states of records, rules, resources, roles, or stopping conditions; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

## A.8 HV08 Conditional Potential Field (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Interface ID (`id`) | HV08 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV08` |
| Name (`name`) | Conditional Potential Field |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | Resources, institutions, relationships, power, safety, metrics, platforms, and historical conditions enter a conditional explanation for an outcome family only if, through a detectable mechanism, they change the single outcome selected in advance for the corresponding route (evidence coverage, safety of expression, object behavior, or reflexive response) and receive support from the corresponding H4-instance. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Settings in which human action is affected by context, institutions, and positions of power |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | The potential field is treated as an all-purpose background, a subject with intent, or a moral label |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | None (empty set) |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. E2<br>2. EVIDENCE<br>3. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | None (empty set) |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV08-R0-condition-inventory;<br>claim_level=candidate_description;<br>when=Resources, rules, positions, safety, metrics, platforms, AI mediation, or historical accumulations can be listed by position, scale, and time window, but there is not yet an eligible H4-instance;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Register candidate conditions, differences across positions, blind spots in observation, competing explanations, and needs for more evidence;<br>result_ceiling=The output may be called only an inventory of conditions or a candidate channel. The conditions must not be personified, and it must not be claimed that power, mediation, or reflexive effects have been established.<br>2. route_id=HV08-R1-position-or-mediation-effect;<br>claim_level=conditional_effect;<br>when=An H4-instance returns supported on the single success criterion selected in advance among evidence coverage, safety of expression, and object behavior;<br>additional_inferential_requires=H4-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register channels of masking, amplification, or behavioral response within the instance’s position, mediation, outcome family, and window;<br>result_ceiling=The result must not be extrapolated to unselected outcome families, and malice, responsibility, or automatic disposition must not be inferred from a position or mediation effect.<br>3. route_id=HV08-R2-reflexive-response;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=An H4-instance has reflexive response as its single preselected criterion. Observation, naming, scoring, or publication reaches the object through an actual channel, and the instance returns supported;<br>additional_inferential_requires=H4-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=E3, CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register reflexive response within the specified observation or publication channel and window;<br>result_ceiling=One response does not count as persistent reflexivity, and the result must not be used to conceal observation, suppress expression, or broaden authorization.<br>4. route_id=HV08-R3-carried-reflexive-persistence;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=An H4-instance returns supported on its single preselected reflexive-response criterion within the stated scope across windows, and under §4.5 it is verified, by comparison with repeated stimuli, continuing observation, shared changes, and material supporting other explanations, that the response enters a current carrier, is retained or updated, and has a sustained effect;<br>additional_inferential_requires=H4-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=E3, CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register sustained reflexive response within the specified observation or publication channel, current carrier, and window;<br>result_ceiling=Persistence must not be extrapolated from an immediate H4 effect. This does not qualify as a historical increment under G3, and the result must not be used to conceal observation, suppress expression, or broaden authorization. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Conditional opportunities, constraints, masking, and amplification |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Assuming conditions determine individual behavior<br>2. Inferring malice from a position of power<br>3. Attributing intent to an environment |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual exposure or evaluation events, cases at particular positions, the population subject to the conditions, distributions of responses, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of interaction, organizational or platform boundaries, public digital spaces, and environments across domains. T=Time span covers windows for accumulation of exposure, response, reflexive change, and fading. O=Organizational level covers roles in action and evaluation, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers exposure events, mechanisms of interaction between conditions and behavior, meso-level power structures, institutional rules, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw exposures, expressions, and behaviors, time series, cases at particular positions, response distributions, platform metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers people directly evaluated, indirectly affected people, second-order reflexive consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers links of power and information, mediation nodes, areas of masking, amplification paths, and propagation across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for setting rules, using metrics, public evaluation, human review, and disposition. Compare differences across positions |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | R0 registers a set of potentially relevant conditions. R1 and R2 judge their effects on the preselected outcomes based on the corresponding evidence. The term “potential field” presupposes no potential function, common unit, or physical conservation law. |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Mechanism chain from conditions to behavior or evidence |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Distribution of positions<br>2. Heterogeneity of conditions<br>3. Externalities across domains<br>4. J axis |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Key conditions and the strength of effects can change with scale |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman systems without intentional action, power, or institutional positions |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Generalizing local conditions directly into the motives of all subjects |

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Support<br>2. Constraint<br>3. Masking<br>4. Amplification<br>5. Mixed |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Differences in behavior before and after changes in resources, rules, platforms, or conditions of disclosure<br>2. Safety of expression, evidence coverage, and absence rates at different positions<br>3. Changes in visibility and disposition before and after metrics, scoring, or AI mediation<br>4. Whether the candidate channel effect exceeds the predetermined threshold under the comparison conditions |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Resources and rules<br>2. Differences across positions<br>3. Changes in platforms or metrics<br>4. Behavioral responses |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Boundaries and interfaces. 2. Observation positions. 3. Causal contract |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Feasible paths. 2. Expression and evidence. 3. Generation and destabilization |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register accumulation of conditions, responses, and lags in fading |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record unobservable conditions, collinearity, and reflexive changes |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Positions invisible due to barriers involving safety, identity, or platforms |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Advantaged positions<br>2. Low-power positions<br>3. Mediators<br>4. People evaluated |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Institutions<br>2. Resource allocation<br>3. Platforms<br>4. Metrics<br>5. Relationship networks<br>6. Historical accumulations |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Rule makers<br>2. Platform operators<br>3. Resource allocators<br>4. Decision makers for action |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Advantages conferred by conditions, or the results of screening, do not constitute normative justification |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 only lists candidate conditions. R1 and R2 form judgments about position-related effects or reflexive responses within the selected H4 outcome and window. R3 also requires evidence of a current carrier, retention across windows, and a sustained effect. Immediate effects must not be extrapolated into persistence. Establishing a channel does not establish malice, responsibility, or authorization. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of candidate channels through conditions, differences across positions, masking of evidence, and needs to reduce risk. It does not authorize changing rules or platforms, allocating resources, evaluating subjects, or making dispositions concerning them. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. Under the same institutional conditions, people at positions with different safety and power take opposite actions<br>2. Using a label such as “power” or “platform” in place of an actual causal channel leaves the state change unexplained |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people at different positions may, through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection, submit differences in mechanisms, absence signals, and impacts on safety, and trigger review independent of the original judgment about the potential field or the decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke the attribution to a potential field that does not hold, remove labels on positions and their downstream effects on evaluation and disposition, and restore the unresolved status; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |

Stopping, absence, exit, and recovery curves<br>4. Differences between average load and its distribution at key local uptake positions |

R1 uses G2 and CM-LOAD for channel effects. R3 checks the current state, its formation, and effects across windows under §4.5 for accumulation through carriers. R2 tests the additional historical conditional increment separately under G3. H5 traces cannot substitute for evidence of later effects or testing under G3.<br>6. Lack of formal mechanism support for a route does not cancel findings of general harm, continuing burdens, or historical causation already supported by independent domain evidence. Register conclusions separately according to their own grounds. |

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Personnel<br>2. Positions<br>3. Procedures<br>4. Facilities<br>5. Budgets |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Task assigners<br>2. Resource allocators<br>3. Parties granting authorization<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight<br>5. Parties responsible for remedy |

R3 uses §4.5 to judge cumulative damage or delayed recovery through transmission by a current carrier. R2 judges historical increments beyond the current representation only with additional G3. Persistent correlation, a temporary overload, or an overall average must not be used as a substitute for the corresponding evidence. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of load, capacity, recovery, local overload, and needs to reduce load or add resources. It does not authorize task reduction, resource commitments, personnel action based on performance, or compelling people to bear the load. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |

| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is approved, the designated responsible party must, within the stated deadline, revoke erroneous load judgments and their effects on performance or responsibility, and actually restore task, resource, and record states; versions and verification of completion must be preserved |

A=Aggregation level covers individual state events, cases of local groups, populations of intentional collectives, phase distributions, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of action, organizational or institutional boundaries, digital spaces for collaboration, and the reach of evolution across domains. T=Time span covers state windows, transition lags, regression, dormancy, hysteresis, and generational cycles. O=Organizational level covers member roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers state events, mechanisms of interaction in transitions, meso-level phase structures, institutionalization, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers raw state events, transition sequences, local cases, phase distributions, state metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct members and people taking up work, people who exit, indirectly affected people, second-order consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers local phase clusters, connections across levels, splits and mergers, functional transfers, and succession paths. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for naming phases, adopting monitoring, trials, advancement, exit, and elimination. Preserve mixed phases |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | States of human intentional collectives that are taking shape or operating. Components may be present, forming, missing, or inapplicable, as judged under the specific prototype conditions |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Conditions of applicability<br>2. Phase criteria<br>3. Nonlinear paths<br>4. X0 does not count as an eighth stage |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Distribution of local phases<br>2. Transitions across levels<br>3. Continuity of uptake through succession<br>4. Protection and exit |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Those taking up work, institutional carriers, and effective objects can change with the phase |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Populations, purely technical systems, or categories based on similarity when there are no grounds for identifying a human intentional collective. Lack of mature uptake during formation is not by itself a basis for categorical exclusion |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating the phase of one case as directly representative of the population<br>2. Transferring human stages into the universal core |

This triggers review independent of the original phase judgment or decision chain |

`scale_profile` is not a single-axis “micro–macro” label but the complete nine-axis record SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A is aggregation level, which must declare units, population, distribution, and aggregation rules. X covers physical space and digital boundaries. T covers windows, lags, and cycles. O is organizational level across roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C is causal level across events, interaction mechanisms, meso-level structures, institutions, and systemic conditions. R is resolution across original events, sequences, individual cases, distributions, metrics, and summaries, with compression loss recorded. I is the scope of direct, indirect, second-order, cross-domain, and intergenerational impacts. N is network topology scope. J is scope of jurisdiction and authorization. Expanding A, X, O, or I does not automatically expand J. Observing more cannot confer greater authority to act.

The object’s identity and its identity criterion belong to K in the object contract. Relationships and channels of effect belong in `input_dependencies`, `carrier`, or the causal contract. Membership and affected positions belong in `affected_positions`, `local_exclusion_zone`, and observation fields. None may pose as a scale axis. The N scale axis is network topology. Below, “explicit N premises at runtime” refers to N1–N5 in the normative choice layer. The two must not be conflated.

All 11 variables share the same limit on action. The variables themselves produce only descriptions, evidence gaps, and action needs. They do not authorize real-world adjustments. Their output ceilings are as follows.

| Variable | Descriptions or Needs This Variable Can Produce |
| --- | --- |
| HV01 | Candidate structural domains, boundary disputes, and needs for more evidence |
| HV02 | Boundary states, interface barriers, exclusion risks, and testing needs |
| HV03 | Candidate anchors, diverse expressions, comparison results, and needs for more evidence |
| HV04 | Candidate classifications for GC, GS, and GE, state transitions, and needs for more evidence |
| HV05 | CV, RS, costs, capacity, rights to stop, gaps in uptake, and needs to reduce load, add resources, or make reallocations |
| HV06 | Chain connectivity, time lags, losses, interruptions, costs, and uptake needs |
| HV07 | Intake, changes to record fields, execution, duration, gaps in write-back, and needs for procedural repair |
| HV08 | Candidate channels through which conditions could have effects, differences across positions, masking of evidence, and needs to reduce risk |
| HV09 | Load, capacity, recovery, local overload, and needs to reduce load or add resources |
| HV10 | Depending on the selected route: component applicability, prototype matches and mixed states, qualified explanations in terms of phase mechanisms or historical conditional increments, independent X0 path status, uncertainty, and observation needs |
| HV11 | Voluntariness, actual costs, direction, alternative explanations, structural consequences, and needs for stopping and protection |

However obvious the need may seem, any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures. The variables must not on their own authorize testing, load reduction, adding resources, repair, protection, attribution of responsibility, trials, advancement, exit arrangements, or disposition.

Several other symbols recur throughout the book. `S*=<B,X,R,I,T,SP,K>` gives the seven fields of a D0 candidate object contract (boundary, state variables, relations or transition constraints, interface, time window, scale profile, and identity criterion). `N0` is the null model. `K*/F*` are the target identity criterion and the target functional criterion for repair, respectively. `G-instance` and `H-instance` denote specific test instances for empirical question families and human-domain clauses, respectively. The first definition of each in the main text governs its use.

F1–F6 are identifiers for formal propositions. Used on their own, F and F* can variously denote a dynamic update, a functional criterion, or a repair target according to their local definitions. A shared letter must not be taken as grounds for presuming the same object. G3 tests only historical conditional increments beyond the given current representation. Carrier-mediated learning, accumulation, sustained effects, and intertemporal reproduction are tested under their respective mechanism contracts. None of them takes positive support from G3 as a prerequisite.

# Appendix C Common Method Kernel and Cross-Disciplinary Bridging

This appendix lists methodological responsibilities that can be shared when texts interoperate. Each text retains its own authoritative definitions and way of operating. The common kernel requires no shared chapter structure, artifact count, or numbering. It does not make one text independent empirical evidence for another.

## C.1 Method Kernel and Stable References

| Chinese Reference | Content | Where to Find It |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Definition layer | Objects, states, feedback, scales, circles, information status, and recasting | Framework definitions and authoritative definitions in *World Model* |
| Method constraints CMC1–CMC3 | Task and representation, evidential status of materials, and facts and eligibility to act | §4.17 |
| Conditional formal propositions F | Task recovery, closure, common feasible actions, composition of conversions, error, and dynamic refinement | §4.18–4.19 and this appendix |
| Empirical question families G1–G4 | Grouping, channels, historical increments, and representational closure | Instance contracts in Part 4 |
| Outcome and mechanism contracts C1–C12 | Outcome registration, mechanism composition, and bridging to normative procedures | The individual contracts in Part 4 |
| Governance invariants | Freezing, evidence, baselines, authorization, protection, and the life cycle | Existing governance clauses in both texts |

## C.2 Common Method Constraints

CMC1 requires the task, representation, horizon, environment, operations, and error to be declared together, and static recovery, dynamic closure, and intervention sufficiency to be tested separately. CMC2 requires preserving the evidential status of materials, source lineage, inferential premises, and scope of support, and not counting reprocessed material as independent empirical evidence. CMC3 requires facts, value judgments, and eligibility to act to be established separately. See §4.17 for the full formulation.

The three constraints specify responsibilities for using models. They do not constitute three empirical laws of the world. Whether they reduce errors or impose unnecessary burdens in real tasks requires separate evaluation. The framework must not be declared effective on the grounds that the procedures have been followed.

## C.3 Shared Governance Invariants

Invariant one. Freeze the record before results and only append afterward.

Invariant two. Lack of support for a positive claim does not amount to support for the null model.

Invariant three. Definitions, contracts, internal teaching examples, and internal simulations must not serve as external empirical support on their own.

Invariant four. The evidential status of materials, proposition role, artifact status, and whether fields are missing are orthogonal dimensions. They must not be merged.

Invariant five. A single artifact must not automatically span the responsibilities for source tracing, modeling, foresight, evaluation, authorization, and execution.

Invariant six. Model complexity must be subject to simplification, removal of unnecessary elements, and review of necessity suited to the task. Claims of predictive or empirical capability require comparison with a simple baseline and independent evaluation material. Formal proofs, interpretation of texts, and normative arguments are checked according to their own tasks. Inapplicable out-of-sample metrics must not be imposed on them.

Invariant seven. Residuals generate only competing candidates. They do not automatically generate variables in reality.

Invariant eight. Capabilities have domains of applicability and validity periods, and are subject to drift, suspension, rollback, and retirement.

Invariant nine. Aggregate results must not erase low-power positions, privacy, non-disclosure for protection, appeal, or exit.

## C.4 Preserving Differences Across Texts

Interoperability should separately check proposition layers, empirical question-family contracts, null conclusions, scale closure, noncommutation tests, and inferential dependencies, as well as information status, artifact systems, typed unknowns, experimental evaluation, evidence of capability, and model life cycles. A complete contract in one text must not be replaced by a similar name or case in the other. When the correspondence has yet to be established, the differences should be kept explicit.

The framework’s nine scale operators need not be aligned with the standard operator steps in *World Model*. The framework’s 14-section scale transformation record is also kept separate from the closed artifact system of *World Model*. The two share only the minimum meanings of what is preserved, changed, collapsed, or omitted, new unknowns, source lineage, domain of applicability, residuals, and stopping.

## C.5 A Mathematical Example of Bridging Scales

Consider a microstate x, a coarse-graining map M, microscopic evolution F, and macroscopic evolution F̄. If the difference between M(F\_t(x)) and F̄\_t(M(x)) remains within tolerance ε over the specified initial conditions, parameter domain, error norm, and time interval, one can discuss approximate commutation for that task. If this holds for only one statistic, it must not be extended to equivalence of the entire state. A proof must also state the order of limits, how the error is controlled, and the interval of validity. A long-term conclusion must not be inferred from a short-time approximation alone. This is a general formal example and does not claim to give such a theorem for social objects.

Mathematical bridging requires carrying the source object, target object, mapping, scaling law or limit, initial values, retained historical information, error, and failure boundary together. It does not require all models to use the same representation, imply that every transfer across scales causes task-relevant loss, or identify losses from social recasting with physical entropy. Establishing a mathematical proof, showing that a real object meets its premises, and deciding which option to adopt in practice are three separate responsibilities.

Related studies are 05.05 “Gödel, Incompleteness, and Computability,” 05.06 “Effective Theories, Scale Closure, and Coarse-Graining,” and 05.08 “Model Selection, Parsimony, and Expressive Power.” These numbers help locate material. A particular mathematical conclusion must be checked against the assumptions, proofs, and domains of validity in the corresponding original works. A bibliography entry must not be used in place of verification.

See Appendix D for the framework’s map of applications by domain. Each domain retains its terminology, measurements, arguments, and professional boundaries. Teaching examples in the map only illustrate conditions and the structure of counterexamples. They do not pose as experiments that have been carried out or as actual cases. Topic numbers connect existing research materials and do not rank theoretical importance.

## C.6 F1 Task Recovery and Worst-Case Error

Let X be a nonempty set of source states, π:X→Y a deterministic representation with Y=π(X), and q:X→V the target. For each y∈Y, the preimage X\_y={x:π(x)=y} is called a representation fiber. The decoder may use only y. It must not read the source state, identity labels, or undeclared history behind the scenes.

**Task recovery proposition.** There exists g:Y→V such that q=g∘π if and only if q is constant on each fiber. If it exists, g is unique on the actual image Y.

Proof. If q=g∘π, the same representation is passed to the same function, so it gives the same target value. Conversely, for each nonempty fiber, define g(y) to be the common target value on that fiber. This definition is unambiguous and gives g(π(x))=q(x) for every x. Each y has a source state, so any g satisfying the condition must take that common value. This proposition establishes only the existence of a function between sets. Whether such a function can be computed on an infinite domain by an algorithm that always halts, and whether a finite budget suffices, are separate questions.

**Finite scalar recovery proposition.** Further suppose X is finite, q is real-valued, the decoder can output any real number, and loss is the worst-case absolute error over the entire domain. Let a\_y=min\_{x∈X\_y}q(x) and b\_y=max\_{x∈X\_y}q(x). Then

inf\_g max\_{x∈X}|q(x)−g(π(x))| = (1/2)max\_{y∈Y}(b\_y−a\_y).

Proof. For any output c, the sum of its distances to the two endpoints is at least b\_y−a\_y. At least one distance is therefore at least half the oscillation. With c=(a\_y+b\_y)/2, all target values in the fiber lie between the endpoints, and the maximum distance is exactly half the oscillation. Taking the midpoint in each fiber and then the maximum over all fibers attains the lower bound.

For a nonnegative tolerance ε, a decoder with error at most ε over the entire domain exists if and only if the oscillation of every fiber is at most 2ε. With an infinite state domain, use suprema instead and check boundedness. Vector values, restricted outputs, average loss, and probability weighting each give a different optimization problem. Half the oscillation must not be used as a universal score of information loss across domains.

Suppose the target is binary, the loss is zero-one loss, and the finite set of source states is uniformly distributed. Let n\_y0 and n\_y1 be the counts for the two labels in each fiber. The minimum error rate is |X|⁻¹∑\_y min(n\_y0,n\_y1). This is because only one label can be chosen for each fiber, and choosing the more frequent label attains the smaller of the two error counts. The error under randomized labeling is a convex combination of these two counts and cannot be lower. This expression describes the given distribution. It does not estimate error frequencies in a real population.

## C.7 F4 Composition of Conversions and Actual Execution

Let f:X→Z and h:Z→W, with the final query q already satisfying q=g∘f. The composition h∘f can still recover q if and only if g is constant on every fiber of h restricted to f(X). If q=k∘h∘f, intermediate values corresponding to the same final representation must have the same g value. Conversely, define k by the common g value on each final fiber. The condition concerns only the reachable image f(X). It imposes no constraint on intermediate values without a corresponding source state.

For example, let X={0,1}². The first stage retains both positions, and the second stores only their sum, while the final query still asks for the first position. The stages individually complete the tasks of storing and counting. Their composition merges (0,1) and (1,0), so it cannot answer the final query. Retaining the Boolean value at the first position repairs this problem without recovering all source details. Changing the final query to counting is simply a different task that can succeed.

Representational sufficiency and correct execution must also be tested separately. Suppose the representations of three source states are f=(0,0,1), the required answers are q=(0,1,1), and the actual rule is g\_actual=(0,0). The first two states require different answers for representation 0, establishing that the representation is insufficient. The third state clearly requires 1 for representation 1, but the actual output is 0. This separately witnesses an independent error in the rule. Changing the rule to (0,1) repairs that error without removing the conflict in representation between the first two states. States for which the output is correct do not lose that correctness because of failures elsewhere.

This counterexample does not require every real-world fault to be uniquely classified. If the internal representation, input at the time, or actual rule is unknown, retain the gap. The continued existence of an original document must not be used in place of evidence that the reviewer obtained it in time. Being able to consult it after an error differs from having it available as input before the action.

## C.8 F2 Closure and F5 Error Propagation

Let F:X→X be deterministic one-step dynamics. There exists F̄:Y→Y satisfying π∘F=F̄∘π if and only if π(x)=π(x′) implies π(F(x))=π(F(x′)). The proof follows by replacing the target q in C.6 with π∘F. If the condition holds on the whole domain, then π∘F^t=F̄^t∘π for each nonnegative integer t. The zero-step case is immediate. To go from step t to step t+1, apply the commutation relation once.

If U is the set of admissible actions and each action has a specified F\_u, the condition must be verified for each admissible action before the updates can be composed step by step for any action sequence fixed in advance. If action feasibility depends on states that were collapsed, it must also be ensured that a macroscopic action can be executed in all relevant source states in the same fiber, or its availability must be explicitly limited. If different microscopic implementations are grouped under one macroscopic action, whether they give the same macroscopic result must also be checked separately.

In a finite stochastic system, let P(x,x′) be the transition kernel. If the probability sum ∑\_{x′∈B}P(x,x′) for reaching each target block B is the same for every source state in a given block, define the macroscopic transition kernel using that common value. Whatever the conditional distribution of source states within the block given the current macroscopic block and past records, averaging that common value leaves it unchanged. The process under the state projection therefore updates according to this macroscopic kernel. If the same macroscopic one-step law is required for all initial distributions, the blockwise equality condition is also necessary. Check it by starting with all mass at each source state in the same block in turn. Retaining only the next-step mean, or obtaining equality by chance for one initial mixture, does not meet this distributional requirement for all initial states.

Approximate closure also needs a horizon. Let d be a metric on Y. Suppose each reachable x satisfies d(π(F(x)),F̄(π(x)))≤ε, and F̄ satisfies d(F̄(y),F̄(y′))≤L d(y,y′) in the region reached. Starting from y\_0=π(x\_0), let e\_t=d(π(x\_t),y\_t). The triangle inequality gives e\_{t+1}≤ε+Le\_t, and the recurrence yields e\_t≤ε∑\_{j=0}^{t−1}L^j. If the per-step error or L changes over time, the recurrence should be carried out accordingly, and the formula for fixed parameters must no longer be applied.

These propositions state exact closure and error propagation under the conditions for approximate closure, respectively. They cannot determine whether an empirical model meets the statewise bound, whether it leaves the working region, or whether an intervention belongs to the original operation set. Closure under the usual distribution, closure under intervention, and normative usability still have to be established separately.

## C.9 Different Tasks for Two Queues

Consider two ordered queues (q₁,q₂)∈{0,…,30}², giving 961 states in total. The local overload target is max(q₁,q₂)>15. For the sole purpose of computing expected error, make the 961 source states equiprobable. This differs from making the means equiprobable and does not imply that real queues follow a uniform distribution.

If only the mean m=(q₁+q₂)/2 is retained, (0,20) and (10,10) both give m=10, but the overload answers differ. For a sum s from 16 to 30, the same fiber has 2(s−15) overloaded states and 31−s non-overloaded states. The labels of the other fibers are unambiguous. The minimum number of classification errors is therefore ∑\_{s=16}^{20}2(s−15)+∑\_{s=21}^{30}(31−s)=85, and the error rate is 85/961≈8.844953%. This is the exact count on the complete artificial state domain.

The rule “report local overload only if the mean exceeds 15” misclassifies 240 states. The 155 errors above the optimal risk for the representation are due to the choice of rule, so the 240 errors cannot all be attributed to information loss inherent in aggregation. If the task changes to recovering the maximum queue length, (0,30) and (15,15), both with mean 15, require answers of 30 and 15 respectively. The optimal real-valued output of 22.5 gives a worst-case error of 7.5. If the output must be an integer, the optimal radius for this fiber becomes 8. Output constraints themselves can therefore change the answer.

Adding the maximum M makes it possible to answer local overload and maximum length exactly, but still does not preserve which position is larger. With exactly two queues, the smaller value is 2m−M. If each position serves at most c tasks per round and no new tasks arrive, then

M′=(M−c)\_+，m′=[(M−c)\_++(2m−M−c)\_+]/2,

where a\_+=max(a,0). Thus (m,M) has dynamic closure under this symmetric model. When capacity is tied to position as (15,10), the states (0,20) and (20,0) with the same representation become (0,10) and (5,0) respectively. The next representations differ, so closure fails. With three queues, (20,20,5) and (20,15,10) both have mean 15 and maximum 20, but their totals after serving up to 15 queued tasks at each position are 10 and 5 respectively. The two-queue result must not be extrapolated directly.

This example retains both positive and negative results. A total can answer a query about the total exactly, and adding a task-relevant variable can repair some shortcomings. The same addition does not necessarily work for interventions tied to position, heterogeneous dynamics, or more dimensions. Recovery of all microscopic information need not be a success condition for every task.

## C.10 The Strict Stability Condition for Feedback With Fixed Delay

Consider x\_{t+1}=x\_t−k x\_{t−d}, where d is a fixed nonnegative integer, k is a fixed real number, and the initial history x\_{−d},…,x\_0 has finite values. The variable x is a signed deviation. The equation has no noise, external input, saturation, or state constraints. The zero solution is asymptotically stable for all initial histories if and only if

0<k<k\_c(d)=2sin[π/(4d+2)].

Expanding the state to (x\_t,x\_{t−1},…,x\_{t−d}) yields a finite-dimensional linear recurrence with characteristic polynomial p(λ,k)=λ^{d+1}−λ^d+k. All characteristic roots lie strictly inside the unit circle if and only if the solution tends to zero for every initial history. The modes associated with repeated roots decay despite their polynomial factors when the root modulus is strictly less than 1.

At k=0 there is a root at 1, so the system is not asymptotically stable. For k<0, p(1,k)<0, while p(λ,k) is positive for sufficiently large positive λ. There is therefore a real root greater than 1. For sufficiently small positive k, the root at 1 enters the unit circle as λ=1−k+O(k²). The remaining roots, originally at 0, stay inside the circle. Small positive gains are therefore stable. When d=0, the only root 1−k directly gives 0<k<2.

For positive k, write a boundary root as λ=e^{iθ}, with 0<θ<2π. From k=λ^d(1−λ) we obtain

k=2sin(θ/2)exp{i[(d+1/2)θ−π/2]}.

For k to be positive and real, θ=(π+4πm)/(2d+1) with m=0,…,d. The first boundary at positive gain comes from the smallest angle and its conjugate angle, giving k\_c(d). The roots vary continuously with the parameter and are bounded at finite parameter values. They must pass through the boundary before leaving the unit circle.

We must also rule out renewed stability at higher gains. On the unit circle, p\_λ=λ^{d−1}[(d+1)λ−d] is nonzero, so boundary roots are simple. Implicit differentiation gives

d log|λ|/dk = (2d+1)(1−cosθ)/{k|(d+1)e^{iθ}−d|²}>0.

Thus all crossings at positive k go outward, with no re-entry. Together with stability at small gains, this gives the necessary and sufficient interval stated above. Equality at the boundary leaves a nondecaying mode. The boundary must not be included on the grounds that a root modulus computed in floating-point arithmetic is slightly below 1. Above the boundary a growing mode exists, while the special all-zero history still remains zero.

With k=0.8 fixed, the critical values for d=0 and 1 are 2 and 1, respectively, and the system is stable. The critical values for d=2 and 3 are approximately 0.618034 and 0.445042, respectively, and the system is unstable. For an all-ones initial history, the first step in all four cases gives 0.2, so the feedback term already affects the state in the model, while convergence differs. Even when the system is stable, the result still places no bound on transient peaks or costs and does not establish acceptability to people or real-world repair. Time-varying delays, stochastic disturbances, nonlinearity, and constraints on execution require new analysis.

## C.11 Historical Increments and Omissions in the Current Representation

Use the same 961 equiprobable initial states, but let each queue serve at most 5 tasks per round with no new arrivals. Advance the full state by F(q₁,q₂)=((q₁−5)\_+,(q₂−5)\_+). The prediction target is the mean after the second round. Compare four inputs after the first round: the current mean, the current mean with the initial mean, the full current state, and the full current state with the initial mean. Under squared loss, outputting the conditional mean of the target for each input fiber attains the minimum expected loss.

| Prediction Input | Minimum Mean Squared Error Under the Given Distribution |
| --- | ---: |
| Current mean | 0.579016561 |
| Current mean and initial mean | 0.271143397 |
| Full current state of both queues | 0 |
| Full current state of both queues and initial mean | 0 |

The calculation advances each of the 961 initial states and retains its original weight. It does not make the states after the first round uniform again. In particular, (0,20) advances to (0,15) and has a next mean of 5. The state (10,15) advances to (5,10) and has a next mean of 2.5. Both current means are 7.5, while the initial means differ. The historical mean helps distinguish differences collapsed by the current state projection. The full current state already suffices to determine the future.

This establishes that a historical predictive increment can arise from an omission in the current representation. It does not establish that real systems universally lack memory. Real data still require attention to measurement, confounding, temporal order, leakage, and model selection. If the full current carrier is unavailable, it must not be claimed that a historical increment has been ruled out. If earlier experience affects the outcome through a current skill and the residual disappears once that skill is represented sufficiently, the result limits only the additional prediction given the skill. It does not negate the total effect of earlier experience or rule out learning in general.

## C.12 Three Limits on Composing Local Successes

Whether conversions can compose to give the same final answer, whether causal effects can link into a chain, and whether actions can be jointly executed with available resources concern functions, joint responses, and sets of constraints, respectively. They can occur in the same workflow, but a generic status of “connected” must not be used to make them interchangeable.

See §2.4.2 for the causal counterexample. The types s∈{0,1} have equal weight, with m(a)=a(1−s) and y(a,m)=sm. The average effect from treatment to mediator and the average effect from mediator to outcome are both 1/2, whereas the effect of the full chain is 0 because the two effects are borne by different types. This rules out multiplying edge means unconditionally. It does not withdraw an independently identified total effect.

The resource counterexample consists of two unit tasks, each taking a full period. Both must be completed before the end of the first period, which has capacity of only 1. Each task is feasible alone, but there is no feasible joint schedule. Joint execution becomes feasible if capacity in that period rises to 2, or if one deadline is lawfully moved to the second period and that period has capacity of 1. If both original deadlines remain, adding capacity in the second period cannot make up for the original deadline. The conclusion follows directly from the stated constraints. It does not certify real-world demand, capacity, or authorization.

**F3 Common feasible action proposition.** For a finite, single-round deterministic task, let X\_y be a nonempty observation class and A\_ok(x) the set of eligible actions for each state. There exists an action depending only on y that is eligible for every x∈X\_y if and only if ∩\_{x∈X\_y}A\_ok(x) is nonempty.

Proof. If such an action a exists, it belongs to the eligible set of every compatible state and therefore to the common intersection. Conversely, choose any a in the nonempty intersection. It is eligible in every compatible state, and choosing it does not require knowing which state actually holds. Nonemptiness of each state’s set separately is only a necessary condition. For example, if the eligible sets of two states are {左} and {右}, meaning left and right, respectively, each has an option but they share no action. If both allow an authorized preservation action, the intersection can be nonempty while the state remains incompletely identified.

The eligible sets in the proposition must already incorporate the resources, protections, and valid permissions required by the current task. Writing an action down mathematically must not be used as a substitute for these real-world conditions. The proposition concerns only the listed compatible states and cannot guarantee that unknown states will never occur. When undertaken lawfully, further observation, waiting, or preservation can change the information and policy sets, but none of these occurs automatically. Probability-based choices that tolerate risk, randomization, and sequential policies are analyzed separately according to their own objectives. Representational sufficiency, computability, accessibility, implementability, resources, and authority to adopt should be demonstrated separately for the specific use.

<a id="part_20"></a>

# Appendix D A Map of Domain Explanations and Applications

This map draws on existing research across 32 domains. Each section preserves the questions of its domain before connecting them to the applicable limits on representation, evidence, normative judgment, and action in the main text. The domains do not bear responsibility for validating one another. Several studies citing the same original work, data, or case still form one linked chain of evidence.

## D.01 The Framework Itself, Explanatory Tasks, and Borrowing From Theory

In the main text, see §1.3–1.10, §2.2–2.7, and §16.15–16.16. Research entries cover 01.01–01.08.

Start by asking which outcome needs explaining. Do not begin with whichever vocabulary is most familiar. Explaining why equipment failed, why a decision took shape, who bears the consequences, and how the people concerned understand what they experienced are related but distinct tasks. Technical mechanisms, institutional authority, and lived experience can correct one another or each provide the main account of a particular part of the problem. Structure, interests, power, psychology, culture, and technology should not have permanent priority before evidence is examined.

Borrowing from theory requires distinguishing at least guidance for observation, definitions, testable mechanisms, formal inferences, and normative choices. The same term appearing in two theories does not guarantee that the objects, variables, or permitted inferences are the same. A formal mapping should state which relation it preserves, which distinctions it loses, and what circumstances would change the conclusion. If a domain method already answers the question sufficiently, the framework may limit its role to checking sources and boundaries. It need not introduce additional circles into the analysis.

In a teaching example, an automatic reminder reduces missed tasks, but appeals still cannot change errors. Improvement on the technical task can coexist with inadequate procedural remedy.
