Crude rates, age-specific rates, aggregate period measures, and actual cohort outcomes are distinct. Net migration can be zero even when many people move in and many move out. Age structure must not be translated directly into actual care and fiscal burdens for each family or institution. Comparisons across files also require checking what was actually revised. Under the same version number, country tables, aggregates, and scenarios may not all have been updated together (United Nations Population Division, 2026).

Age, period, and cohort satisfy a specific linear relation. More observations cannot by themselves eliminate the resulting linear identification problem. Shocks at particular stages, curvature, and comparisons that make additional assumptions can still be studied. Early experiences may affect later outcomes through current resources and skills. If current conditions fully absorb the historical residual, that is not the same as earlier effects never having occurred, much less does it mean that change is impossible in the future.

Analysis of migration must distinguish legal status, wishes, actual feasibility, and coercion. Conditions for choice may change over the course of the process. Having signed a contract does not establish continued voluntariness (ILO, see the definition of “voluntariness”). People forced to leave may still have choices in some respects and no feasible alternatives in others. When explaining recruitment, labor, settlement, or return, check the relevant conditions separately for the current question. Members of the same family may face different constraints involving remittances, care, status, and geography. Succession involves knowledge, positions, resources, decision-making authority, and responsibility. Age does not confer authority by itself. A new generation may revise old arrangements. Preserving memory is not the same as requiring a repetition of the old way of life.

In a teaching example, membership in an organization remains the same in number, but experienced people leave, new people enter, and care needs grow. Treating the headcount as evidence of “complete continuity” would miss changes in knowledge and burden. The shared goals of population policy do not give it ownership of individual bodies. Having children, not having children, and having conditions for raising children safely should be stated as separate normative questions, and statistical needs should not be used to assign duties to individuals.

The year of a population turning point depends on the question. The first decline, the first year of a continuous decline, the year a sustained decline is confirmed, and natural decrease do not designate the same point in time. When early experience affects later outcomes through current resources, controlling for those mediators changes the estimand. It must not be inferred from the disappearance of a historical conditional residual that the earlier experience never mattered. Lower public expenditure does not establish lower needs. It must also be checked whether burdens shifted to families and people who provide support from outside the household.

Research topics. 24.01 Demographic transition, fertility, and mortality. 24.02 Age structure, aging, and care. 24.03 Cohort, period, and age effects. 24.04 The life course, critical periods, and cumulative advantage. 24.05 Migrants, refugees, diasporas, and boundaries. 24.06 Generational succession, inheritance, and transmission within organizations. 24.07 Population policy and reproductive justice. 24.08 Dispersal, leaving home, belonging, and living across places.

## D.25 Education, Knowledge Production, the Professions, and Lifelong Learning

In the main text, see §1.8, §6.3, §13, §15.3.3, and §16.16. Research entries cover 25.01–25.08.

The aims of education, access to learning, actual knowledge and skills, the interpretation of test results, the award of qualifications, and subsequent opportunities must be kept distinct. Specialist instruction and jointly posing questions can coexist. Student participation does not require adopting every proposal. A reasonable refusal on curricular grounds and the framework’s own `applied` status under GOV-02 belong to different levels. An examination can measure a particular kind of knowledge without thereby establishing that opportunities are fair.

When an assessment is used to determine individual qualifications, checking distributions across groups must not by itself substitute for evidence about the individual and a review appropriate to the use. Judgments by teachers or experts must also account for consistency and uncertainty (see OSR, 2021). After a grade record is corrected, it must also be checked whether the correction entered downstream decisions such as admissions in time. Reissuing the grade does not by itself establish that the original opportunity or all losses have been recovered.

Teaching, family support, and organizational settings work through specific arrangements of time and resources. Sending a notice, activating an account, parents attending a meeting, and improvements in administrative attendance records are distinct from gains in knowledge. Participation can also increase the work of translation, care, and maintenance. The absence of a G3 conditional residual does not rule out learning by ordinary people. Feedback-mediated learning through a carrier and the additional historical increment are each tested under their own contracts.

The stages of knowledge production that matter for the actual object under study should be traced. Examples include setting topics, budgeting, reviewing, practicing, drafting, publishing, and earning returns, as well as making, repairing, editing, translating, and corresponding. There is no requirement that every practice pass through the same gates. Results in submitted papers must not be taken directly to represent research that lacked the resources to produce a draft. Reanalysis of the same data does not constitute independent replication. Professional capabilities, credentials, decision authority, and public trust each require separate evidence. The experience of patients or practitioners can change which questions matter without by itself establishing the overall causal effect.

In a teaching example, trainees acquire new skills, but there are no corresponding jobs locally. It must not be said on that basis that the training necessarily yielded no learning, and course completion must not be used to promise employment. Open access for reading, permission to reuse, opportunities to contribute, joint agenda-setting, and recognition of labor are distinct entry points. The absence of records from citizen observers may reflect inadequate coverage. It does not establish that the object is absent locally. Expression and appeal should not depend on prior mastery of the framework’s terminology.

Whether existing skills or qualifications are visible and whether further training is needed are separate questions. Displaying a credential, receiving a response in hiring, securing a job, wages, and net job creation are assessed through separate outcomes. When a correction to a qualification enters admissions criteria in time, it can constitute a real downstream improvement, but it is still not the same as the final position having been secured. The cumulative number established, the number operating in a given year, the number of places set by rule, and the actual number of learners must not be conflated. Failure to complete a program does not automatically negate learning in part of it.

Research topics. 25.01 The aims of education (Dewey and other philosophies of education). 25.02 Critical pedagogy (Freire and others). 25.03 Cultural capital, the hidden curriculum, and qualifications. 25.04 Teachers, students, families, and organizational settings. 25.05 Universities, academic communities, and knowledge production. 25.06 Experts, professionalization, and public knowledge. 25.07 Knowledge sharing, citizen science, and open practices. 25.08 Lifelong learning, skill formation, and technological change.

## D.26 Networks, Media, Language, Narrative, and Identification

In the main text, see §5.5.3, §5.10, §9.7, §10–11, §15.3.4, and §15.3.6. Research entries cover 26.01–26.12.

Networks can create opportunities. Language can make experience intelligible, narratives can help people mourn and discuss responsibility, and identity can support cooperation. Analysis should retain these benefits and distinguish ties, actual help, exposure, reading, beliefs, public expression, and action. Weak ties, bridging positions, structural holes, centrality, and resources that can actually be mobilized are not synonyms.

In a teaching example, each of two groups consists of three people who are all connected within the group. Only one edge connects the groups. If people act after receiving a prompt from one neighbor who has already acted, the behavior may spread across the edge. If support from two distinct neighbors is necessary, receiving the message is not necessarily sufficient. The cumulative success probability for repeated independent attempts, 1−(1−p)^n, is not the same as social reinforcement. Several social nodes can offer support while still sharing the same factual source.

Silence can stem from risk, privacy, lack of exposure, or lack of interest. It must not automatically be taken as approval or opposition. Being unverified, being demonstrably false, and being deliberately deceptive are distinct states. Strong collective emotion need not be irrational, and actual harm must not be used to make an identity group bear all the responsibility. Clear boundaries are not the same as closure, and identity fusion or behavior that comes at a high price does not automatically establish free consent.

Translation needs to distinguish words and sentences, the message as a whole, the audience’s task, and the authority to represent others. Language proficiency, listeners’ understanding, institutional access, and the quality of the content each have their own channels. Close reading of narratives can explain experience and meaning without substituting for technical causal analysis. Conversely, identifying a technical cause does not make the work of memory and literature redundant. Facts learned later must not be backfilled as what the narrator already knew on the day of the account.

Platform treatments need to be tracked in their actual versions: changes to the feed, a ranking weight, content matching, and author feedback must not share a single treatment label. A fixed recommendation rule can still generate feedback through human responses. Retraining the model is unnecessary for some kinds of feedback. Evaluate changes to system-maintained profiles on their own, and assess private preferences, actual opportunities, and long-term harm separately. Repetition of the same original factual source does not negate the social coordination that multiple supporters may produce.

Research topics. 26.01 Weak ties, structural holes, and network resources (Granovetter and Burt). 26.02 Diffusion, thresholds, and complex contagion, with Granovetter, Watts, and Centola. 26.03 Social identity, self-categorization, and group boundaries (Tajfel and John Turner). 26.04 Agenda-setting, framing, and media selection. 26.05 Silence, spirals, rumors, and misinformation. 26.06 Narrative, memory, metaphor, and public explanation. 26.07 Attention on platforms, recommendation, and public opinion. 26.08 Translation across languages and the transfer of concepts. 26.09 Language learning, language institutions, and inequality in expression. 26.10 Uncertainty–identity and optimal distinctiveness. 26.11 Collective emotion, terror management, and moral panic. 26.12 Identity fusion and shared experiences that come at a high price.

## D.27 Aesthetics, Art, Literature, Games, and Everyday Culture

In the main text, see §1.8–1.10, §5.10, §9.7, §10.6, and Appendix A.3 and A.11. Research entries cover 27.01–27.09.

Beauty, liking, status as art, use, popularity, revenue, reasons for public funding, and social effects are distinct. A work’s form, rhythm, genre, and the experience of viewing it can answer questions in their own right. There is no need to establish group coordination or long-term improvements in virtue first. The study of institutions and power also has an independent role. Materials, skills, evaluation, and channels of dissemination affect what can be made and seen.

Collaboration, conventions, and control in cultural production need to be analyzed at each stage. Critical approval does not guarantee sales. The liquidation of an organization is not the same as the end of works, skills, or movements. In literature, distinguish voice, focalization, characters’ judgments, and actual reader responses. A point of view in a story is not automatically the verdict of the author or of an omniscient narrator. Science fiction thought experiments can show how paths diverge under different conditions. They do not serve as empirical evidence for cosmic social mechanisms.

Play can be worthwhile for the process itself. Seriousness and free play can coexist. Deliberately imposed difficulties, competition, and rules for roles need not be reduced to their ultimate payoffs. Consumption may also involve practicality, durability, aesthetic appeal, shared experience, or the expression of status. In a teaching example, two objects with the same function offer different experiences because of their shapes and how they feel to the touch. This shows that the specified function does not exhaust the aesthetic experience. It does not establish that all functional explanations are invalid.

Fans’ enthusiasm can coexist with maintenance work, commercial control, and internal dissent. An archive still being online is not the same as the original community still being active or safe. Macro-level indicators cannot exhaust the meaning of everyday eating, solitude, or care. Discuss ownership, custody, research, exhibition, and interpretation of heritage as separate matters. The right to continue changing how one lives also merits separate discussion. Return and authorized loans for exhibition can coexist. Preserving a streetscape or local memory also does not automatically bring about the recovery of the original residents’ relationships.

The claims that function determines ratings, that function is sufficient for beauty, and that an aspect of an object has functional beauty are distinct. Different experience ratings for objects with the same function can limit the first claim without by themselves refuting the other two. Close reading and interpretation of meaning can be completed within the scope of those tasks. Claims of long-term behavioral change require evidence from readers. Archival preservation, personal enthusiasm, ties among members, and the safety of the environment can also persist or end on different terms.

Research topics. 27.01 Aesthetic experience, beauty, and judgment. 27.02 Art production, institutions, and cultural industries. 27.03 Literature, narrative ethics, and understanding from multiple perspectives. 27.04 Games, ritual, sports, and play (Huizinga, Caillois, and others). 27.05 Fan communities, subcultures, and shared enthusiasm. 27.06 Consumption, fashion, taste, and identity. 27.07 Thought experiments and science fiction (for example, *The Three-Body Problem*). 27.08 Everyday life, festivals, food, and bodily practices. 27.09 Historical memory, museums, and cultural heritage.

## D.28 Technology, Infrastructure, Platforms, and Artificial Intelligence

In the main text, see §10–11, §13.8–13.11, §15.3.3–15.3.5, and §16.23–16.24. Research entries cover 28.01–28.08.

The material properties of technology, protocols, deployment configurations, institutions, maintenance, and actual use jointly determine specific outcomes. This does not mean that technology and society each account for half. Bandwidth, materials, and interfaces can each impose constraints in their own right. Default configurations and commercial arrangements can also change who is reached. Compliance with written standards, interoperability between implementations, consistency of business semantics, reliability under stress, and whether people can actually use the technology must be judged level by level.

Maintenance depends on spare parts whose quality can be verified, skills, budgets, permissions, and ongoing labor. If several sets of equipment share one upstream power supply or the same defective software, their number does not guarantee independent redundancy. Platform matching and flexibility in working hours can bring real benefits. Pricing, eligibility, future opportunities, and appeals can also be centrally controlled. The presence of an algorithm must not be used to determine all aspects of legal employment status directly.

For AI, the attribution of generated content, support in the source text, external facts, and eligibility for the intended use must be distinguished. Retrieval helps obtain material and provides no guarantee of its truth. Internal consistency can help detect one class of errors and can also reproduce errors consistently. Suggestions, tool requests, execution of a procedure, changes to external state, and consequences are five distinct stages. Human oversight requires access to the relevant material, capability, time to review beforehand, and a manageable workload. A rejection signal must also actually reach the actuator.

In a teaching example, a user clicks Reject after the task in the backend has become irrevocable. Approval in name only does not provide a genuine veto. Privacy likewise breaks down into collection, inference, reuse, retention, and downstream deletion. Issuing a deletion instruction does not establish that deletion has been completed. Assess actual possession of data, permitted use, recognition for accounting purposes, transactions, and eligibility to supply the data across borders separately. A lack of recognition as an asset for accounting purposes is not the same as a lack of use or value (see Ministry of Finance, 2023). It also does not lift applicable restrictions on use. In technological transitions, tasks, independent skills, career opportunities, and meaning should be tracked separately, and tool proficiency or occupational exposure should not be treated as long-term outcomes. Before an older support that still serves a necessary function is removed, the availability of a substitute and the consequences of disruption should be checked. Stopping activity and taking protective measures in the face of immediate danger are handled separately under the applicable procedures.

Comparisons of technological necessity must specify what is held fixed. This could be the product name, the binary, the configuration, or the complete deployment including training, permissions, and maintenance. If the later items in that list change, the comparison must not still be described as a change in institutions alone. The search for alternatives must also hold the task, resources, time limit, and necessary protections constant. Assess attribution of AI-generated content, the existence of bibliographic entries, metadata, source-text support, facts about the world, and permission for the use in question individually. A limited reanalysis or a recalculation using synthetic data can correct a technical quantity, but it must not be reported as actual users having obtained the corresponding improvement.

Research topics. 28.01 Technological determinism and the social shaping of technology. 28.02 Infrastructure, standards, and maintenance. 28.03 The politics of technology and sociotechnical systems (Winner, STS, and others). 28.04 Platform governance, algorithmic management, and digital labor. 28.05 AI knowledge, generation, and evidence. 28.06 AI agents, automation, and responsibility. 28.07 Data, privacy, surveillance, and digital rights. 28.08 Technological transitions, substitution, and loss of capabilities.

## D.29 Cities, Space, Housing, Rural Areas, and Public Accessibility

In the main text, see §5.5.1–5.5.3, §10, and §15.3.7–15.3.8. Research entries cover 29.01–29.08.

The material conditions for urban formation, historical paths, and current agglomeration benefits are distinct. Geographic location, administrative jurisdiction, membership, functional networks, and service areas also have distinct boundaries. When the same city ID is used across periods, the statistical coverage and tracking rules must also be checked, and an unchanged ID must not be taken as grounds for concluding that the spatial boundary is fixed (United Nations Population Division, 2025). Once connectivity is established on a map, actual access still depends on times of access, capacity, cost, eligibility, support needs, and alternative routes. Centrality does not directly establish power or welfare.

Everyday contact, use at staggered times, and familiarity with a place can support help-seeking and looking out for others, but safety, basic respect, and users’ ability to remain in place still require evidence. Local participation can also exclude renters, future residents, and users from other areas. Whether a space is visible, whether it can be used lawfully, whether affordable access is continuous, whether people can use it safely and autonomously, and whether people can change the rules under which it is managed should be observed separately.

Distinguish land rights, building rights, tenure security for renters, mortgages, eligibility to return, and maintenance costs. A neighborhood’s name and appearance must not by themselves be used to determine an individual’s poverty, a violation of law, or eligibility for action (see Roy, 2005). When combining categories for a specific statistical use, retain the classification mapping and deduplicate the records. Rural areas simultaneously support production, nonfarm work, care, and supply across places. They are not a less advanced stage of urban development. New roads can expand peripheral markets and can also intensify competition as firms from the center enter. Growing more slowly in relative terms does not amount to absolute decline.

In a teaching example, a redeveloped neighborhood has a higher average income, but a different set of residents. The change in the current neighborhood can be real, but it must not stand in for outcomes in the lives of original households and those who moved away. After a disaster, building safety and renewed exposure, services for households, relationship networks, local memory, feasible choices, and remedies should be tracked separately. Moving is not necessarily bad, and rebuilding in place is not necessarily good. The observable pattern of partial recovery does not automatically establish a feedback trap.

Using a common list of cities at each time point addresses differences in the sample at that time only. A fixed statistical cohort requires holding the list fixed across all time points. Even a fixed cohort does not ensure that geometric boundaries or residents stay the same. Holding the historical area fixed and holding the current area fixed can serve different valid indexes, and the denominator should be stated explicitly. A facility’s service area is only a spatial representation based on assumptions. Continuous routes, actual times of access, people’s physical circumstances, and autonomous use still require corresponding evidence.

Research topics. 29.01 Urban formation and agglomeration. 29.02 The production of space and the right to the city (Lefebvre and Harvey). 29.03 Local knowledge and urban diversity (Jacobs and others). 29.04 Housing, land, ground rent, and differentiation. 29.05 Rural society, land institutions, and urban–rural relations. 29.06 Regional development, centers and peripheries, and transportation networks. 29.07 Public space, accessibility, and everyday safety. 29.08 Postdisaster reconstruction, demolition and relocation, and local memory.

## D.30 Ecology, Resources, the Commons, and Sustainable Living

In the main text, see §2.8, §5, §6.9, §12, and §14. Research entries cover 30.01–30.08.

Natural processes, institutional rules, knowledge, markets, and everyday life are connected through specific interfaces. Social choices can change resource use and the distribution of risks. They cannot remove hydrological or material constraints by definition. Resource systems, resource units, appropriators, rules in use, and outcomes call for domain-specific analysis. Forest cover, species, access to fuel, and household life must not stand in for one another.

Food stocks, supplies an organization can requisition, and food households actually obtain are distinct (see Sen, 1981). In analyzing a food crisis, assess supply, access, delivery of relief, and consequences for nutrition and disease separately. Total stocks in storage or total imports alone must not be used to judge that everyone’s needs have been met.

The costs of exclusion and the subtractability of common-pool resources are distinct from joint ownership under law. Rights of access, withdrawal, management, exclusion, and transfer must be considered separately. Collective organization and engineering support can complement one another. Local rules are not automatically fair. Analysis of energy transitions needs to distinguish continuous fuel flows, stocks under construction and in use, uses of minerals, grades of refined materials, and comparable services. Conservation accounting does not yield a unique allocation of value.

Water withdrawals, net consumption, evapotranspiration, return flows, recharge, and water levels are distinct. In a teaching example, the cultivated area expands after field-level efficiency improves. Local water savings need not reduce total net consumption. Better information is also insufficient to guarantee the overall outcome if it does not feed into withdrawal constraints and maintenance practices. Distribution, recognition, procedure, and remedy in environmental justice each require their own evidence. Proximity to a pollution source does not by itself identify individual health effects.

In discussions of climate, distinguish historical contributions, current capabilities, future risks, and adaptation needs. Uncertainty about measurements, mechanisms, scenarios, and normative matters must also be kept separate. Support through cooperation, determinations of legal responsibility, and compensation for specific harms each depend on the applicable sources of law, objects, and conditions. Limits on one mechanism do not automatically rule out other grounds. Scientific assessments do not directly determine shares of compensation or authority to act. See International Court of Justice (2025, §§414–415, 433–435). GDP, material outcomes, living conditions, care, and ecological outcomes need not be combined. Conservation can concern individual nonhuman beings, populations, and ecological processes at the same time. Scientific knowledge, local knowledge, and legal representation involve distinct qualifications. Success in protecting one position does not automatically offset losses at another.

For ecological restoration, the actual manipulation, the measured endpoint, and the target system must be stated separately. If the study directly changes fencing and hydrological infrastructure, the treatment must not be renamed random reintroduction of predators. Even if only the combination reaches a given threshold, that does not by itself establish a statistically significant interaction. The number of species, biomass, water use, access to food, and people’s lives can change in different directions. Keeping the outcomes separate has more explanatory power than combining them into a health score without a basis.

Research topics. 30.01 Social–ecological systems and human–environment relations. 30.02 Commons, shared resources, and collective governance. 30.03 Energy transitions and material metabolism. 30.04 Agriculture, food, and water resources. 30.05 Environmental justice and the distribution of risk. 30.06 Climate change, adaptation, and intergenerational responsibility. 30.07 Growth, degrowth, and multiple scales of well-being. 30.08 Conservation, local communities, and nonhuman values.

## D.31 Risk, Disasters, Public Health, Command, and Recovery

In the main text, see §6, §8.6, §12.6, §15.3.8, and §16.13. Research entries cover 31.01–31.11.

Hazard sources, exposure, vulnerability, observation, acceptance of risk, and action are distinct. Social conditions cannot erase the intensity of a flood or the presence of a pathogen. Technical estimates must not by themselves determine who should bear the risk. Prevention should weigh the harms of the action itself, proportionality, feasible support, and provision for review. It should neither expand authority without limit because of uncertainty nor wait for complete certainty before permitting limited protective action.

Interactive complexity and tight coupling, high-reliability organizing, latent conditions in layered defenses, system constraints, and routine task adaptations each have their own explanatory scope. A temporary workaround that retains checks is distinct from removing checks. Whether checks have been retained depends on whether the protective function and task-critical distinctions are still tested, not on whether the format is the same. A substitute verification procedure appropriate to the task must have a professional basis. The evidentiary value of observing no incidents depends on exposure and observation. Three items can stand side by side: a new process was genuinely established after an incident, its net effect on safety is still undetermined, and one branch of feedback-mediated learning is not yet supported.

Critical slowing down is discussed only under the corresponding conditions of dynamics, noise, sampling, and bifurcation. Arbitrary fluctuations must not be taken as signs of impending collapse. An early warning must reach an endpoint where the warning can be understood, a response can be organized, and protective action can be completed in time. An absence of harm after a warning may mean the measures worked. Infection, testing, confirmed diagnosis, reporting, and hospitalization have different denominators. Changes in beliefs, actually presenting for testing, and health outcomes must not be inferred from one another.

Medical classification, lived experience, the course of illness, provision of benefits, and treatment choices each bear their own evidentiary burden. Shared decision-making can improve knowledge and does not automatically guarantee long-term health. Emergency command handoffs, coordinated demobilization, the end of special authority, and recovery of living conditions have different clocks. Function that has genuinely recovered with temporary support is not fake. A later shock does not necessarily show that the earlier conclusion was entirely wrong. Only recovery claims that exceed the evidence already available need to be narrowed accordingly.

Warning lead time must cover verification, reaching recipients, comprehension, mobilization, and the time it takes for protective measures to take effect. If labels are complete and the target has been fixed, the absence of a post-warning event can still be scored against that target. Estimating how much harm action prevented requires a separate causal comparison. Periods when monitoring is offline must not be coded as zero events. Recovery of function with temporary power or ongoing support can be genuine recovery. The assessment must retain the support conditions and the population actually covered, and that population must not automatically be replaced by all pre-disaster users.

Research topics. 31.01 Risk society, prevention, and uncertainty (Ulrich Beck and others). 31.02 Normal accidents and high-reliability organizations (Perrow, Weick, and others). 31.03 Normalization of deviance and organizational silence (Diane Vaughan and others). 31.04 Disaster vulnerability, exposure, and resilience. 31.05 Early warnings, critical slowing down, and response clocks. 31.06 Epidemics, public health, and social trust. 31.07 Medicalization, mental health, and determinants of health. 31.08 Incident investigation, accountability, and collective learning. 31.09 Health care institutions, clinical ethics, and shared decision-making. 31.10 NIMS, ICS, emergency command, and functional degradation. 31.11 Rescue, repair, reconstruction, and false recovery.

## D.32 War, Revolution, Empire, Development, and Historical Paths

In the main text, see §2.4–2.7, §8.1, §10–12, §14, and §16.4. Research entries cover 32.01–32.08.

Identify the state as a legal entity, the government, the military, organizations, and the populations that bear the consequences separately. Realism, institutionalism, and constructivism focus on resource security, the monitoring of commitments, and constituted status. These concerns can support different conditional explanations and are not combined into a single will of the state. Defensive intent, actual effects on security, the other side’s assessment, future capability to honor commitments, and normative justification are also distinct. Communication can reduce a type of uncertainty about information. It does not automatically resolve future incentives or enforcement.

Analysis of mobilization needs to distinguish initial participation, repeated participation, sustained organizing, obtaining concessions, transfer of power, and subsequent living conditions. Supporting without signing up, signing up without taking part, dropping out, and continuing to participate are distinct positions. A later victory must not be used to infer that the outcome was inevitable beforehand. Analyses of empire and decolonization retain separate channels for trade, conquest, cultural exchange, and unequal dependence. Formal sovereignty, administrative capability, economic choices, and citizenship rights must not serve as evidence for one another. Local actors and internal differences must not be replaced by histories centered on leaders.

In historical dynamics, Khaldun’s account of cooperation and the reproduction of ruling coalitions, Toynbee’s account of responses to conditions, Tainter’s question about the costs of complexity, and Turchin’s feedback hypothesis are retained, but they are not combined into a unified civilizational cycle. Diminishing marginal returns do not imply negative marginal returns, much less negative net returns or imminent collapse. The cost of maintaining a complex model must also be kept distinct from the costs of actual social organization. Retain separate versions for the original prediction, later models, retrospective assessments by the authors, and independent tests.

Modernization, industrialization, and global connections are not a single development ladder. Actual effects on industry must not stand in for outcomes for workers, caregivers, and people who cannot move. Peace encompasses at least the cessation of violence, procedures for handling disputes, political arrangements, responsibility, recovery of living conditions, and reconciliation. Cooperation between groups does not establish individual psychological recovery. A counterfactual changes only nodes for which there is evidence and reassesses downstream effects. It retains the conditional roles of historical figures, ordinary people, and implementation at the end of the chain, and it is not written as another history whose course is already known.

When comparing historical quantities, first check the target and the range of common support, then preserve the disagreements that remain. The total number of violent deaths and the violence-related component of excess deaths overlap substantially and are not identical. The gap must not be removed by renaming them, and the intervals from different studies must not be stitched together into a common confidence interval. Diagnostic completeness, assumptions used to correct for actual underreporting, and adjustments for missed interviews belong to different steps. Signing, approval, funding commitments, receipt, settlement, and consequences for people’s lives each require their own evidence. An accurate answer about the documentary record need not wait for the overall causal share to be established.

Research topics. 32.01 Realism, liberal institutionalism, and constructivism. 32.02 The origins of war, the security dilemma, and deterrence. 32.03 Revolution, social movements, and collective mobilization. 32.04 Empire, colonialism, decolonization, and state-building. 32.05 The rise and fall of civilizations and historical dynamics (Khaldun, Toynbee, Tainter, Turchin, and others). 32.06 Modernization, industrialization, and globalization. 32.07 Peace, negotiation, reconciliation, and the postwar order. 32.08 Contingency, historical figures, structure, and counterfactual history.
